# Dead ends — tried, rejected, and the condition each rejection depends on

**What this is:** the index of approaches this project has already tried
and rejected, extracted by a ten-agent census (2026-08-21, 542 entries)
over the source comments, the Reports, `README.md` and `PLAN.md` that
record them; 549 after the plant-line merge of 2026-08-21 added five and
its decay work added two more on 2026-08-22, and 563 after the gnome
tree-interaction pass added a `character` section of twelve on 2026-08-23
and the chest-rule fix added two more; 565 after the physical-trees design
session added the two measured sway rejections on 2026-08-23, and 572
after package P2's economy re-derivation added seven the same day — six of
them mechanisms that package built, measured and withdrew inside one
session, which is the shape this file exists to make cheap; 573 after the
frame-cost audit's converged-pass prototype on 2026-08-25, which is that
shape again and is worth reading for its control rather than its verdict;
574 after §S's reactive-invalidation arm on 2026-08-26, which is the same
shape a third time and whose value is that it *fired* — a null with a
working positive control is a far stronger rejection than a null without
one; 575 after the per-tile momentum gate the same day, which fired, was
bit-identical, removed 91% of its target work and made the frame **slower**;
578 after the support-model report's three the same day, one of which is a
pair of ablations whose value is that the second is *vacuous* -- its counter
reads zero on every frame, which is the only reason its byte-identical null
is readable at all.
576 after §S's riser-seeded climb collapse, the third boxing attempt and
the one that finally says why no box can work.
Re-attempting a known dead end costs a whole session, and it
has happened here; this file exists so it stops happening.

**How to use it:** grep your area's `##` section before proposing or
retrying anything in it. Each entry says what was tried, what happened,
and — where the record says — the **condition** the rejection depended on.
Per the standing convention (`CLAUDE.md`), a dead end whose condition has
since changed is due for re-testing, not permanently banned: an
exactly-zero result in particular usually means the condition it keyed on
was degenerate, not that the lever is dead.

**What this is not:** the authoritative record. Every entry cites where
the full account lives (a source comment, a report section, a status
paragraph) — the citation is the record, this is the index of it. Some
dead ends appear more than once because more than one place records them
with different detail; that is deliberate. Division of labour:
`open-bugs-handoff.md` owns *"is this broken?"*; this file owns *"was this
tried?"*.

**Maintenance:** a session that reverts or rejects an approach adds its
entry here in the same change, citing where it recorded the full story —
the "a revert keeps the knowledge" convention, given an address.


## liquids  (57 entries)

- **PLAN.md 'Automatic promotion (§3e): attempted, reverted — a real architectural gap found, not a tuning problem'** - Automatic promotion as designed (per-chunk liquid_hint + round-robin try_promote_one + demote cooldown) was fully implemented and fully reverted: a uniform rectangular block is internally flat, so it promotes immediately, snaps, sleeps, and never spreads again (spread stuck at 106 from frame 10 to 6000; five previously-passing tests broke). The solver only redistributes among claimed columns and overloaded_edge is defined purely on internal imbalance (h[edge]==avg for a uniform body), so nothing can ever express 'spread onto the open floor beside me'. Reverted in full rather than left disabled — this is a design gap in what the Step 3 solver plus Step 5 edge demotion can express together, not a tuning problem.
  *Re-test when:* Needs real design work first: candidates sketched but not decided — an external-bed pressure term, a suspicious-uniformity promotion refusal (itself a relocated per-cell heuristic of the kind already proven insufficient), or letting try_extend claim open floor when it lowers potential energy. Any fix needs an acceptance scene run through promotion itself, not just the solver in isolation.
- **PLAN.md 'Liquid (Report B) — what changes in the plan' item 5 (LBM)** - LBM as the liquid layer was re-evaluated and deferred for composability, not cost: it would own the entire liquid layer with no notion of the CA's free density-displacement, and its cost scales with resident world size rather than activity — breaking the engine's cost-proportional-to-change architecture under M10 streaming.
  *Re-test when:* Any LBM prototype must demonstrate real sleep/coarsening, be measured at actual streaming scale (the 10x resident-chunk figure is assumed, not measured), and report mass conservation across a coarse/fine boundary; read Thürey & Rüde 2009 in full first.
- **PLAN-log.md 'Live playtest finding: water settles into sand-like piles' — 'The verdict: don't adopt SPH/PBF/PIC-FLIP'** - Particle solvers (SPH, Position Based Fluids, PIC/FLIP) were researched and rejected: no GPU compute path exists (pixels/wgpu are presentation-only here), and the numbers do not fit — real-time SPH's own paper caps at 5,000 particles, PBF needs CUDA, PIC/FLIP runs seconds per frame offline — against a 163,840-cell grid in ~16ms. Every surveyed falling-sand engine (TPT, Sandspiel, Noita) uses a per-cell CA rule for liquid.
  *Re-test when:* A GPU compute path plus a drastically different budget; otherwise fix the CA rule, not the paradigm.
- **PLAN-log.md 'Overnight run, section 4' (MIN_LIQUID_TRANSFER tuning); 'Liquid (Report B)' item 2; 'Step 6 (§11) — drop MIN_LIQUID_TRANSFER toward 8'** - MIN_LIQUID_TRANSFER at 8 under the pure per-cell leveler left a ~12,000-frame settling tail, so it was raised to 150 — which Report B reframed as a 15% dead band locking in a permanently uneven surface, diagnostic of slow leveling, not a correct setting. After the heightfield steps landed, 8 was tried again per the design doc and still failed the bar (31 units residual vs 20 allowed at 300 frames); it landed at the measured 16, not the doc's 8.
  *Re-test when:* Re-test lower values only after a further leveling-speed improvement (e.g. automatic body promotion working); do not lower it ahead of the mechanism or the 12,000-frame tail returns.
- **PLAN-log.md 'Overnight run, section 4' (third finding) and 'Live playtest finding: water settles into sand-like piles, not flat' (quick mitigation)** - Raising flow_rate does not fix wide-body leveling or the sand-like initial slope: nearest-neighbour diffusion moves a fill difference one cell per frame regardless (O(width^2)), so 200→500 made no measurable difference, and the later 200→1000 mitigation sped only the late smoothing phase while the trapezoidal pour shape (diagonal-fall running to exhaustion first) was untouched.
  *Re-test when:* flow_rate is not the limiting term once fill differences are small; the shape problem lives in mechanism ordering / body-level leveling, not this knob.
- **PLAN-log.md 'Overnight run, section 4', first 'real bugs found' bullet** - Transferring the whole fill difference horizontally (to 'reach equality faster') overshoots past equality — 500/300 becomes 300/500 — and the alternating scan direction flips it back every frame forever (active_chunk_count still nonzero at 24,000 frames). Transfer half the difference.
- **PLAN-log.md 'Overnight run, section 4: water/liquid leveling — compressible-volume rewrite'** - The old discrete liquid model (search up to dispersion=5 cells for a reachable empty destination) leaves any cell buried more than 5 cells from an opening with no move on any frame — a wide water column eroded only from its edges and never flattened. Replaced by continuous fill-in-aux exchange (compressible-volume model).
- **PLAN.md 'Reports/liquid-heightfield-design.md — landed; Step 1 implemented' ('A real cost regression found and fixed')** - Implementing World::set's disturbance check as a separate self.get(x,y) read before the write — a second HashMap lookup in the hottest function — cost ~1.7x the serial stress worst frame (38ms → 65-69ms). write_cell must return the cell it just overwrote so one lookup serves both write and check.
- **PLAN.md same section, 'Cell::flowing() gating' sentence in the kept-for-the-record paragraph** - Gating the liquid horizontal-first move on Cell::flowing() (Report B §8's proposal) was tried and rejected: a packed liquid column rarely earns flowing() under its move-only definition, so the gate would silently undo the fix for exactly the cells it targets. The landed version was unconditional.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while FLAG_FLOWING is set only on successful moves; a different flowing definition would need re-evaluation.
- **PLAN.md 'Step 4 (§11) — quiescence, the terminal snap, and body sleep' (SNAP_EPSILON paragraph)** - A small SNAP_EPSILON cannot work: the integer-rounded persistent-flux solver has a genuine non-decaying limit cycle — round(0.9*n)==n for n in 1..=5, so residual flux of 11-21 units oscillates forever and never reaches zero (measured on the 40-column spike scene, independently reproduced in a standalone 20,000-frame probe). SNAP_EPSILON=30 clears that floor with margin.
  *Re-test when:* Tied to SOLVER_GAIN=0.4 / SOLVER_DAMP=0.9 and integer flux rounding; re-derive the epsilon if those constants or the rounding change.
- **PLAN.md 'Step 5 (§11) — try_extend, edge demotion, and post-demotion cooldowns' (try_extend paragraph and thrash bullet)** - Two rejected sub-designs: (1) validating a try_extend candidate against the surviving neighbour's row range fails because a demoted column is routinely much taller than the flat neighbour — the candidate's own vertical extent must be scanned independently; (2) preventing extend/demote thrash by returning-after-claim alone only stretches the cycle across two frames with an unboundedly growing cooldown (confirmed via a hung background test) — a successful claim must also set extend_cooldown_until, and overloaded_edge must gate on that same field.
- **PLAN.md 'Superseded — the original rejection below was itself a false negative...' and 'Reverted again, for a real reason this time' paragraphs (liquid Report B section)** - Horizontal-before-vertical liquid ordering (eeefceb, reverted in dcb761c): it genuinely fixed the chunk-boundary pour stall (flat by frame 900 vs a residual step at 1800), but landing columns ballooned to ~5x their cell count — the old vertical-first order's tiny 1% compress transfer had been accidentally throttling packed interiors out of horizontal transfer, and the reorder removed that everywhere at once. A second variant scoped to a local 'am I at the free surface' check also failed (~4.8x peak): an irregular top makes many cells read as surface. Fully reverted to vertical-first. Note the first small-scale serial test of the reorder showed no effect and that null was wrongly generalized to 'doesn't work' — the effect was real at realistic scale.
  *Re-test when:* Do not attempt a third per-cell ordering tweak without a genuinely new way to distinguish a connected body's free surface; the heightfield/virtual-pipes redesign is the intended fix. Any replacement must beat eeefceb's own frame-900 flatness numbers (reconstructable via git show eeefceb:src/sim/update.rs) while never regressing a_landing_column_does_not_balloon_in_cell_count.
- **README.md '## Liquid physics' — horizontal transfer paragraph** - Raising flow_rate alone to flatten a wide body: pure nearest-neighbour fill diffusion needs on the order of width-squared frames (a 100-cell column was still a visible mound after 3000 frames) and no flow_rate setting fixes that. HORIZONTAL_TRANSFER_REACH (8-cell scan for the emptiest reachable cell) was the answer.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for nearest-neighbour-only transfer; the reach mechanism is the replacement, not a bigger rate.
- **README.md '## Liquid physics: compressible volume, not discrete occupied cells' opening paragraph** - The original liquid model — search up to dispersion (5) cells for an empty destination — structurally could never level a wide body: a cell buried more than dispersion cells from an opening had no destination on any frame, confirmed by a live playtest screenshot of a water column eroding only at its edges. Replaced by continuous fill exchange (compressible-volume technique).
  *Re-test when:* Structural impossibility, not tuning; permanent for occupancy-based liquid movement.
- **Reports/liquid-heightfield-design.md §1a (fact 1, wake_all control)** - The hypothesis that the pour-leveling stall was a chunk/parallel/sleep bug was ruled out by measurement: forcing World::wake_all() before every parallel::step — bypassing dirty rects and chunk sleeping entirely — reproduced the identical stall. The fault is update_liquid's transfer priority (HORIZONTAL_TRANSFER_REACH 8, uncapped flow_rate, MIN_LIQUID_TRANSFER 150 dead band); the fix has to be architectural.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for that stall symptom; chunk decomposition remains the recorded first suspect for other seam-aligned artifacts (CLAUDE.md).
- **Reports/liquid-heightfield-design.md §1a fact 2–3 (scoped free-surface variant)** - Scoping the reorder to a literal local free-surface test ('cell above me is not more of the same liquid at full fill') was also tried and reverted: it did not fix the ballooning (~4.8x peak). The approach-level conclusion: no per-cell local heuristic can distinguish the free surface of one connected body from any cell with room nearby once the body's profile is roughened — many interior cells legitimately read as 'at the surface' and collectively over-dilute. Being at a free surface is a whole-body property.
  *Re-test when:* Terminal for per-cell approaches; the heightfield body representation (where the over-diluted state has no encoding) is the recorded answer. §12 B-1/B-2 deliberately share one scene because responsiveness and no-ballooning were in direct tension under every per-cell attempt.
- **Reports/liquid-heightfield-design.md §1a–§1c (the two reverted reorders)** - The unconditional horizontal-before-vertical transfer swap in update_liquid (commit eeefceb, reverted by dcb761c) fixed the stall and made a landing column balloon to ~5x its cell count (23,400 → 102,915 cells) with mass exactly conserved every frame, at a measured ~12% worst-frame serial regression (37.9 → 42.6 ms). Exact conservation is necessary and not sufficient — the state was incompressibility-nonsense.
  *Re-test when:* Its genuine improvements are the bar for any replacement: full flatness by frame 900 on the three-tall-columns repro (vs a 3-cell step at 900 and ~1800 to resolve without it), with no ballooning and less than the ~12% cost. It is a four-line swap, trivially reconstructible for baseline runs.
- **Reports/liquid-heightfield-design.md §2b–§2c (integer heights)** - Float column heights were rejected for integer h on the LIQUID_FULL scale: a float height needs a rounding policy at the cell boundary and drifts against the sum of the cells it produced, and integer state removes the cross-platform float-determinism question entirely. The representation also makes the ballooning state unencodable (a managed column is floor(h) full cells plus one partial, asserted as invariant B-1).
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional while determinism (same-build) is required and cells hold integer fill.
- **Reports/liquid-heightfield-design.md §4a 'Quiescence is the wrong gate'** - Report B §5's requirement that bodies be settled/quiescent before promotion was rejected: waiting for quiescence means the heightfield never accelerates the slow part (the still-moving pour), a fed lake is the central case not an exclusion (absorption handles inflow), and any per-cell 'is this settled' test is the same local-heuristic trap the two reverts fell into.
  *Re-test when:* Quiescence keeps exactly one job — gating a body's sleep, computed as whole-body max |Δlevel| / max |flux| in O(width), never per cell.
- **Reports/liquid-heightfield-design.md §5a (disturbance detection alternative)** - Detecting body disturbance by hooking each mutating call site individually (brush, explosion, fire transform, structural debris, etc.) was rejected despite being cheaper per frame: a correctness property depending on an enumeration staying complete is the failure mode this project has hit three times (light with no writer, canopy density with an always-zero reader, pressure with no liquid consumer). The test lives in World::set / set_owned instead.
  *Re-test when:* Explicit fallback recorded: if the World::set old-cell read blows §12's 15% worst-frame ceiling, revert to the enumeration plus a debug_assert at the seam — that would be a real finding, not a defeat.
- **Reports/liquid-heightfield-design.md §7a / §13 deletion tests (persistent flux)** - A stateless pipe solver (flux recomputed from scratch each step) was ruled out: it is explicit diffusion again, and a 512-column body reverts to O(W²) leveling — the symptom the design exists to fix. Report B's 'the pipe physics is trivial' holds only if the per-interface flux is persistent state.
  *Re-test when:* Structural to the virtual-pipes method; B-2 bar 2 is the deletion test.
- **Reports/liquid-heightfield-design.md §7e (rendering directly)** - Rendering heightfield bodies directly and leaving the grid cells stale was rejected outright ('not a close call'): it would make the grid lie, breaking take_touched_chunks's contract, every other subsystem's reads, and every existing liquid test at once. Bodies rasterize back to cells lazily, only when a column's whole-cell count changes.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — the grid is what every other subsystem trusts.
- **Reports/liquid-simulation-research.md §0 The bug, in the engine's own terms** - Raising flow_rate from 200 to 1000 was tried as a fix for poured water's sand-pile silhouette and did not fix it: flow_rate only throttles the horizontal-transfer mechanism, and the pile shape is produced by the powder-identical diagonal free-fall step, which never consults flow_rate. The bug is mechanism ordering, not rate.
  *Re-test when:* The 1000 (uncapped) setting was kept for its own reason (removing a redundant bottleneck); heightfield-design §7f later re-purposes flow_rate as the pipe gain, at which point its water.ron comment must be rewritten.
- **Reports/liquid-simulation-research.md §5 Recommendation** - SPH, Position Based Fluids, and PIC/FLIP were rejected for bulk liquid after direct arithmetic: the 163,840-cell world already runs six material kinds inside a ~16 ms worst-frame budget, 2003 SPH's ceiling was 5,000 particles for a dedicated demo, PBF needs a GPU to reach 128k particles, and PIC/FLIP granular ran ~6 s/frame offline. The existing compressible-fill leveling mechanism is not discarded — the problem was only the first-frames shape.
  *Re-test when:* Revision 2 confirms the rejection but bounds it: the architectural-mismatch argument must not be generalized beyond particle methods (see r2 entry).
- **Reports/liquid-simulation-research-r2.md §2 + §4c (LBM gate)** - Revision 1's load-bearing argument — every surveyed technique needs global coupling or interacting particles, structurally incompatible with parallel.rs's bounded-local-write proof — was found wrong as a general argument: LBM's step is strictly nearest-neighbour and a 1D heightfield never touches the CA write model. Do not reuse that argument to reject them. Free-surface LBM itself is neither adopted nor rejected: per-step cost fits today (0.6–1.2 ms) but rises to 6–12 ms at streaming-scale resident area, it cannot express density-driven displacement (sand through water), and it would be the only subsystem whose cost tracks world size rather than activity.
  *Re-test when:* Prototype-gated: adopt only after a standalone D2Q9 prototype demonstrates a working sleeping/coarsening scheme, measured at streaming-scale resident area, with mass conservation across any coarse-fine boundary — 'can it sleep', not 'does it fit the budget'.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §1 'Whiskers on a spreading front'** - Adopting the liquid research reports' VOF flotsam fix (three-cell height function for partial-fill orphan droplets): measured here the mechanism does not match — the drained basin strands 54 cells while producing zero films, and the standing films are mostly full cells.
  *Re-test when:* Reconsider only if a measurement shows the films are partial-fill droplets orphaned by interface reconstruction; today they are not.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §1 'Whiskers on a spreading front'** - Shrinking LIQUID_LATERAL_REACH: a pure trade against levelling with no path to zero — reach 24/12/6/3 gives whiskers 290/175/151/119 against levelling 343/557/1017/1661 frames.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional: the curve flattens well above zero whiskers at any reach.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §1 'Whiskers on a spreading front'** - Landing the lateral mover at (tx, y) and letting it fall next frame: whiskers 2540 to 1635, but enclosed holes 289 to 1040 — a worse artifact bought with the better number.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional as measured; any retry must gate on the enclosed-hole count, the artifact it introduces.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §1 'Whiskers on a spreading front'** - Disabling find_lateral_descent to kill whiskers: minus 75% whiskers, but water reads as sand again — the exact bug the mechanism exists to fix.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional as a whisker fix; the mechanism's purpose returns as the regression.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §2 'Sand-into-water displacement'** - Sideways-preferring displacement implemented as a mass-conserving 3-cycle: stripes 1379 to 1370, stall unchanged, and it regressed water rise to 2 rows/frame. Reverted, not committed — inside a pool no free-or-lighter cell exists beside the mover, so the sideways path only opens where striping was never the problem.
  *Re-test when:* Cannot work as specified; only removing the 'displacement is a straight vertical one-row swap' premise (moving a coherent body as a body via rigid.rs) reopens the area.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §2 'Sand-into-water displacement'** - Reverting abffff2 to recover pre-striping behaviour: the pre-abffff2 state let water rise 29 rows in one frame, a gross physics violation, against 1 row/frame now. The striping (41 to 1379) is the explicitly-decided price; no local try_move tweak removes it since it follows from two individually-correct premises.
  *Re-test when:* Stands until a body-level move removes the one-row-per-frame premise, or the trade is explicitly re-decided.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §5 'Automatic promotion — blocker removed, still not ready'** - Turning on automatic liquid-body promotion: originally reverted (127e177) because the flux solver could not expand a level body; with that fixed (edge_with_room) it was re-measured and still ends worse than plain CA — spread 57-68 vs 128 at frame 6000 on the revert's own scene — because shedding one edge column per DEMOTE_COOLDOWN_FRAMES is slower than the CA spreading directly.
  *Re-test when:* Re-test if the shed/outflow rate is made faster than the CA's spread, or if the heightfield's purpose is redefined as accuracy rather than speed.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §6 'The heightfield does not deliver the speed it was built for'** - Building anything downstream of the heightfield's O(width) speed premise: measured, the promoted body levels in O(width²) just like the CA (2.6x/2.4x/1.8x/1.2x slower at 50/100/200/400 columns) and never wins inside a 512-wide world. It wins accuracy only (final flatness 1 vs the CA's 11). Promotion criteria, trigger, and the deferred B-8/B-2/B-6/B-7 bars are not worth building until this is resolved.
  *Re-test when:* A crossover presumably exists past 400 columns — re-test at streamed world sizes (M10) or after diagnosing whether flux is actually persisting per report §7a ('flux must be persistent state or you have rebuilt diffusion').
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md 'Closed this session' (chunk-seam cliffs and terracing)** - The previous handoff's leading hypothesis for powder seam-cliffs and liquid terracing — seam cells never getting flowing() — was measured false. The cause was the chunk-by-chunk sweep order, fixed via FLAG_UNDERCUT.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; recorded so the hypothesis is not re-chased.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md 'Closed this session' (dark lines on horizontal chunk seams)** - Penalising the cell that crosses a horizontal chunk seam: two separate attempts, both reverted, because each replaced the tear with a throttle at the same seam (summed row-fill deficit 2236 and 1948 against 988 for correct ordering). The fix was sweeping chunk rows bottom-first (pass_key) — change the sweep order, not the cell.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional: two fixes failing the same way means the approach, not the tuning, was wrong.
- **src/sim/cell.rs Cell::is_empty** - Letting a promoted body's materially-empty container cells read as empty was found the hard way: movement rules treated the body's wall as open air and moved in, demoting the body — winning a race against intended vertical absorption on incidental scan order, and any loose material merely falling near a lake would graze the wall and demote it. is_empty is managed-aware; use the raw material check when the question is "is material here".
  *Re-test when:* Latent until body promotion goes live in production, but the managed-aware semantics must persist for every "is this position available" caller.
- **src/sim/evaporation_tests.rs module doc ('Volume, never cell count')** - Measuring liquid amount by cell count was read wrong once already: six full cells thinning into a film across thirty-eight cells is constant volume but a 6 -> 228 'increase' by cell count, and was taken for evaporation manufacturing water. Measure summed fill volume, never occupancy.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while Liquid cells carry continuous fill in aux; any occupancy-based liquid metric repeats it.
- **src/sim/liquid.rs DEMOTE_COOLDOWN_FRAMES and test a_level_body_spreads_onto_open_floor_beside_it (thrash comments)** - Letting try_extend reclaim a just-demoted column immediately produced an extend/demote thrash that moved no water at all — the shed column sat there and was reclaimed on the next cycle. A first version of the spread test ran step_liquid_bodies without the CA sweep and reproduced exactly that, reading as a failure. Outflow-by-demotion requires both the cooldown and the CA sweep running alongside to carry the shed column away.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while §6c outflow is edge demotion; if bodies gain a direct outflow mechanism, re-derive the cooldown.
- **src/sim/liquid.rs fn edge_with_room ('Alternated by frame, not scanned left-first')** - Always testing edge 0 first meant the left edge won every time it had room: a body with room on both sides was eaten entirely from the left — x0 walked 60 -> 110 across 6000 frames while the right edge sat at 159 throughout. Alternate the scan order by frame, the same answer as update::step's alternating rightward sweep.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any fixed-order edge scan; directional bias is a recurring failure shape here.
- **src/sim/liquid.rs fn edge_with_room (doc) and test a_level_body_spreads_onto_open_floor_beside_it** - Automatic body promotion was implemented and reverted (127e177) because the persistent-flux solver could not spread an internally-level body: spread was gated on an edge column exceeding EDGE_OVERFLOW_RATIO times the body average (a level body never is) and on the asleep early-return skipping the check ('fell asleep flat, cannot have changed' — flat is not contained). Measured: a four-deep forty-column body asleep by frame 100 had not moved one cell by frame 4000.
  *Re-test when:* The gap is now closed for the manual case (edge_with_room + edge demotion; after the fix water crosses 150 columns of open floor). Automatic promotion is still reverted — re-test it against this exact scene when re-landing it.
- **src/sim/liquid.rs fn try_extend (doc)** - Checking for a free surface only one row above the neighbouring edge column's own height, assuming the candidate matches it, permanently refused to reclaim the very puddles try_extend exists for: a just-demoted column is very often much taller than the neighbour it demoted from (overload is why it was demoted), so the check found more of the candidate's own water instead of open air. Found the hard way; the candidate's vertical extent is scanned independently, anchored at the shared bed.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while demotion sheds overloaded (tall) columns; any height-assumption between adjacent columns re-creates it.
- **src/sim/parallel.rs test falling_water_does_not_tear_a_line_along_a_horizontal_chunk_seam (metric note)** - Occupancy metrics find nothing on torn/dark seam rows: the rows are fully occupied but uniformly low on fill, which render.rs draws as a dark line. Measure the summed fill deficit.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent while the renderer dims liquid toward black by fill.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn find_lateral_descent (buried-cell gate comment)** - Gating the buried-cell skip on "more of my own kind on top" rather than "anything on top" left liquid under a different material (the whole water-under-sand interface) running the full 2*reach scan every frame — measured as the dominant cost of the rule on the ascii stress scene. The wider any-cover test is correct: whatever covers the cell falls into the vacated spot either way.
  *Re-test when:* Measured on the ascii stress scene with mixed materials; holds while the scan pays per-cell chunk lookups.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn transfer_liquid_horizontal (half-the-difference comment)** - Transferring the whole fill difference overshoots past equality — 500/300 swaps to 300/500 and the alternating scan direction swaps it back forever; a debug run showed active_chunk_count still nonzero at 24,000 frames. Transfer half the difference; an isolated pair converges in one step.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the sweep's horizontal direction alternates per frame; MIN_LIQUID_TRANSFER covers the residual many-cell chain case.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn try_move (dst.moved refusal) and src/sim/surface.rs CellSurface::move_cell (displaced-cell revisited comment)** - Letting a displaced cell be displaced again the same frame (unconditionally clearing its flag / not refusing displacement of a moved cell) let water surf the bottom-to-top sweep: the same parcel crossed 50 rows of sinking sand in one frame and erupted out of the top of the blob. The displaced cell needs the opposite revisited answer, and try_move must refuse a dst that already moved — the earlier "one cell per frame" reading of the line was wrong.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while rows sweep bottom to top and denser material displaces upward.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn update_liquid doc (vertical-before-horizontal) and src/sim/parallel.rs test a_landing_column_does_not_balloon_in_cell_count** - Trying horizontal fill transfer before vertical (both forms: unconditional, and gated on an empty cell directly above) to fix tall columns stalling at chunk seams was reverted: a landing column ballooned to ~5x its cell count within a couple hundred frames with fill exactly conserved, because vertical-first's tiny 1%-compress transfer is what throttles buried cells out of horizontal leaking. Reverted, not fixed forward.
  *Re-test when:* Retry only with a mechanism that distinguishes the free surface of a whole connected body from any cell with room nearby (the heightfield/virtual-pipes redesign) — not another per-cell ordering tweak.
- **src/sim/update.rs mod displacement fn displaced_water_rises_at_most_one_cell_per_frame (walls comment)** - An earlier unwalled version of the displacement reproduction measured identically with and without the fix, because find_lateral_descent carried the surface water away before displacement ever fired. The scene must be walled to isolate the vertical displacement path.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent for testing displacement; any repro must block the lateral escape route.
- **src/sim/update.rs mod seam_terracing fn column_volumes doc** - Measuring seam terracing by the topmost water cell per column failed three reproductions: near-empty one-cell films fringe the risers and a topmost-cell metric counts them at full height, smoothing exactly what was complained about — seams read 1.7x interior that way against 9.0x by column volume.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent while Liquid cells carry continuous fill; measure column volume.
- **src/sim/update.rs module doc "Liquids are a compressible volume" and the same test's dispersion-revert note** - The old fixed-radius dispersion search can never level a column wider than its radius — an interior cell with same-height neighbours past its reach has no destination on any frame, so a column erodes only from the edges (confirmed from a playtest screenshot). Note the endpoint test does NOT fail on a revert to it, because the old model eventually levels; the complaint was how it looks in motion.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent for any fixed-radius per-cell search; judge replacements visually (capture tool), not by final spread.
- **src/sim/update.rs test a_spreading_front_does_not_shed_a_comb_of_detached_ledges (candidate 1)** - Disabling find_lateral_descent to kill the comb-of-detached-films artifact takes runs of 12+ to zero but destroys the property the rule exists for: water reads as sand again.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent; the artifact is instead fixed by LIQUID_SETTLE_DROP (land where the cell rests, films 277 -> 0, levelling slightly faster).
- **src/sim/update.rs test a_spreading_front_does_not_shed_a_comb_of_detached_ledges (candidate 2)** - Landing a lateral descent at (tx, y) and letting it fall next frame cut film runs of 6+ from 277 to 55 but blew fully-enclosed holes up from 12 to 215 and levelling time from 311 to 1265 frames. Rejected on both.
  *Re-test when:* Re-test only if the LIQUID_SETTLE_DROP landing rule is removed or changed.
- **src/sim/update.rs test a_spreading_front_does_not_shed_a_comb_of_detached_ledges (candidate 3)** - Shrinking LIQUID_LATERAL_REACH to reduce shed films is a pure trade against levelling speed with no path to zero films. Rejected.
  *Re-test when:* Re-test only if the settle-drop landing rule changes; reach itself trades levelling speed, not the artifact.
- **src/sim/update.rs test a_spreading_front_does_not_shed_a_comb_of_detached_ledges (metric doc)** - Counting film cells outright (water with air above and below) counts every falling droplet in the world — attribution under that definition blamed the plain straight-down fall for 76% of films, true and useless. Count cells in horizontal runs of 6+/12+ (standing artifacts) instead.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent: a cell in free fall looks like a film by definition.
- **src/sim/update.rs test a_wide_deep_water_column_levels_out... (promotion paragraph)** - Wiring automatic liquid-body promotion (World::try_promote_one) was tried directly and reverted: an already-flat block promotes almost immediately, sleeps, and freezes at promotion-time width (spread stuck at exactly 106 columns from frame 10 to 6000 vs the >150 bar) because overloaded_edge triggers on imbalance among a body's own columns — structurally impossible for a flat body. An architectural gap, not tuning.
  *Re-test when:* Retry only once a mechanism exists for a uniformly-full body to notice open floor beside it and shed columns back to CA.
- **src/sim/update.rs test a_wide_deep_water_column_levels_out_instead_of_only_eroding_at_the_edges (reach note)** - HORIZONTAL_TRANSFER_REACH = 1 (pure nearest-neighbour diffusion) cannot level a 100-cell-wide column — active_chunk_count stays nonzero at 3000 frames with a visible 6-39 cell mound — though it passes at 40 wide, which is why the test is 100 wide. Reach 8 was derived from a 100-cell measurement (doc in material.rs).
  *Re-test when:* Width-dependent: re-test if pool scale, reach, or the transfer model changes.
- **src/sim/update.rs tests a_splash_settles_with_no_stray_droplets_and_no_mass_drift and a_drained_basin_does_not_strand_water_above_the_drain** - The r2 report's local-height-function (VOF flotsam) fix was investigated across three scenes before building and rejected: no stray droplets survive settling and fill is conserved exactly, and the stranded-water symptom the report attributes to flotsam strands 54 cells while producing zero one-cell films (films elsewhere are full cells thrown by find_lateral_descent, not partial-fill remnants).
  *Re-test when:* Adopt only if measurement shows partial-fill remnants actually producing the cells, or if the splash regression guard starts failing.
- **src/sim/update.rs tests a_wide_shallow_pool_levels_within_budget / a_wide_deep_water_column_levels_out...** - MIN_LIQUID_TRANSFER (now Material::min_transfer) at 150 made the B-9 levelling budget unreachable by construction and left visible residual unevenness; 16 reaches exactly flat, at the recorded cost of slower final settling (fully asleep by 5000 frames instead of 3000). It buys flatness by giving up levelling, unlike reach, which costs nothing.
  *Re-test when:* The bound must stay <= 16 for the 300-frame / 2%-of-LIQUID_FULL bar; re-derive if that budget or the transfer floor changes.

## powders  (16 entries)

- **PLAN.md M3 section, 'Angle of repose from a friction angle' bullet** - Full MPM (Drucker-Prager elastoplasticity per Klar et al.) was evaluated and rejected as far too slow for a per-frame CPU CA; only its parameterization (per-material friction_angle instead of a magic spread factor) was kept.
  *Re-test when:* CPU-only simulation with a ~16ms frame budget; a GPU compute path or radically different budget would be needed to revisit.
- **PLAN.md M3 section, 'Granular flow as upward hole propagation' bullet; 'Granular (Report A)' item 5** - The naive single-cell void-random-walk formulation of hole-propagation flow measurably over-mixes against real hopper data (confirmed against the same literature the plan cites). If hopper/silo flow is ever needed, build Bazant's correlated 'spot' model instead — recorded specifically so it is not rediscovered the naive way.
  *Re-test when:* Only relevant if a hopper/silo use case surfaces at all; deferred past piles/pours/avalanches, which the two-angle model covers.
- **PLAN.md M3 section, struck 'Pile relaxation via BTW toppling' bullet and its 'Superseded' note; reaffirmed in 'Granular (Report A) — what changes in the plan' item 2** - BTW sandpile toppling was planned, then rejected outright before building: real 2D sandpiles do not show the power-law avalanche distribution BTW predicts (Jaeger/Liu/Nagel, Oslo rice-pile), and the two-angle theta_ms/theta_r model with one FLAG_FLOWING bit produces better avalanches, hysteresis and bistability for less code. The BTW lattice stability condition survives only as a test invariant.
  *Re-test when:* Research-grounded verdict; the acceptance suite asserts a Gaussian (not power-law) avalanche-size distribution — retry only if primary-source evidence overturns that finding.
- **README.md 'Materials' — friction_angle paragraph ('That draw is keyed on position, not the random generator')** - Drawing the fractional-reach roll fresh from the RNG each call instead of keying it on position: a chunk can fall asleep on a frame the dice said no, freezing grains that should have kept rolling. The draw is position-keyed and has to stay that way.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while chunk sleeping exists. Contrast recorded in M14: neighbour-driven ignition may roll fresh, because a burning neighbour independently re-dirties its own position every frame.
- **README.md 'Status' — 'Not yet built' paragraph (see Reports/granular-mechanics-research.md)** - Bak-Tang-Wiesenfeld avalanche toppling is deliberately not planned at all: the granular-mechanics research found real sandpile avalanches do not follow BTW's power-law prediction, so the two-angle (repose vs maximum-stability) model replaces it rather than sitting alongside it. Hole-propagation granular flow is likewise on hold pending an actual hopper/silo use case.
  *Re-test when:* Grounded in the realism goal; re-open only if reproducing BTW statistics ever becomes a goal in itself, or (for hole-propagation) when a hopper/silo use case exists.
- **Reports/granular-mechanics-research.md §3 'Finding 2: do not build BTW toppling'** - Bak–Tang–Wiesenfeld toppling, listed in PLAN.md as pending avalanche work, must not be built: its defining prediction (power-law avalanche sizes) is contradicted by the experimental record (Jaeger/Liu/Nagel found sharply peaked distributions; drum work across five bead types found Gaussian-like, first-order behaviour; only elongated rice grains show SOC). Building it would produce worse granular behaviour at real cost; the two-angle model plus velocity weakening is the more accurate substitute, not a cheaper one.
  *Re-test when:* Keep the SOC literature's lattice stability condition |h_x − h_{x+1}| < tan(θ_r) as an invariant/test formulation — the dynamics are what is rejected, not the statement.
- **Reports/granular-mechanics-research.md §6 'hole propagation — the void model, and its known failure' (+§0 item 3)** - Single-cell void random walks as the drainage mechanism are experimentally known to over-predict particle diffusion badly (measured diffusion is significantly less than the void model predicts), which turns a drained layered pile to mush. The fix is Bazant's spot model: correlated multi-cell motion rather than independent void walks.
  *Re-test when:* Applies if/when hole propagation is built; deletion test 8e (layers must survive drainage) is the guard.
- **Reports/granular-mechanics-research.md §7 'What was considered and rejected for now: μ(I) rheology'** - μ(I) rheology was rejected for this pass on structural grounds, not performance: it requires a per-cell shear rate and local pressure, and this engine's powder cells have no velocity (movement is a discrete swap) and no granular stress field. Adopting it means adding both — larger than everything else in the report combined — beside a one-cell-per-frame movement rule. It also breaks down above I ≈ 0.5 and needs nonlocal extensions.
  *Re-test when:* Explicitly flagged for re-evaluation if Report C (coupling) concludes a granular stress field is needed for rigid-body force feedback; §5's packing scalar is already its φ(I) term.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn roll_along_slope doc** - The old one-angle repose model was superseded: a single angle implies settled piles relevel to that one slope, where real avalanches have hysteresis (harder to start than to keep going). Replaced by two-angle repose — lenient roll reach while flowing, stricter stability reach at rest.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn update_powder (sinking comment)** - Rate-limiting a grain's sink through liquid (grain sometimes stays put) instead of diagonal drift was tried and is a trap: a deferral that writes nothing lets the chunk settle, and the grain freezes in mid-water forever with the world asleep around it.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while chunk sleep is keyed on writes; any movement rule that defers by doing nothing has the same trap.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn update_powder (water opt-in placement comment)** - Guarding the soil-water pass inside update_soil_water instead of at the dispatch site still cost a surface.get per powder cell per frame; the all-sand ascii stress scene went from ~8.1 ms worst frame to 10.2-12.7 ms. The opt-in test must stay at the call site that already holds the Cell (a Vec index).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while a per-cell surface.get is a chunk-map lookup in the hottest loop; re-measure if cell access at that site becomes free.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn update_soil_water (infiltration loop comment)** - Gating infiltration on `moisture + SOIL_SATURATED/2 <= capacity` made all soil at or above half saturation waterproof (a puddle sat on damp ground forever); the gate existed to stop a full water cell being destroyed for a sliver of moisture, which was the wrong place to solve it — take min(fill, room) and write the remainder back as water. The gate also made the conservation test pass against dead code, since infiltration never ran.
  *Re-test when:* Gate is unneeded once partial absorption writes the un-absorbed remainder back; do not re-add a saturation gate to prevent over-absorption.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn update_soil_water const SOIL_CAPILLARY_REST** - Any capillary rest threshold smaller than the drainable band (SOIL_SATURATED - SOIL_FIELD_CAPACITY) creates a perpetual two-cell pump at every water-table boundary: drainage empties the cell, capillary refills it from the saturated side, forever, keeping chunks awake. The threshold must span that band; the derivation cost a real session.
  *Re-test when:* Re-derive if drainage's rest state (field capacity) or capillary's rest condition changes.
- **src/sim/update.rs mod seam_cliffs fn dropped_blobs doc** - Two seam-cliff reproductions are recorded as failures so they are not retried: measuring the spreading front shows nothing (the front crosses seams smoothly; the vertical face persists behind it), and a single contiguous block produces tall cliffs but none land on seams — only falling-blob impacts put a free face at a seam.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent for reproducing seam faces; use dropped blobs and measure the face itself.
- **src/sim/update.rs module doc (M3 note) ** - Bak-Tang-Wiesenfeld toppling was evaluated for the granular model and rejected: Reports/granular-mechanics-research.md found it does not match how real sandpiles avalanche. The two-angle repose model was built instead.
- **src/sim/update.rs test a_straight_fall_leaves_a_hole_anything_may_drop_into (FLAG_UNDERCUT scope)** - The undercut rule cannot simply flag "any hole a move leaves": a falling column descends as a unit precisely because each cell drops into the vacancy below within the same sweep, so flagging straight-fall holes too would stretch every falling column by one cell per frame. Only sideways escapes flag their vacancy, for exactly one frame.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the sweep is bottom-to-top and columns are meant to fall as units; the test pins the distinction.

## structural  (88 entries)

- **`src/sim/load.rs`'s kern/bearing test, moving its origin to the footing centre; measured 2026-08-29** - **Taking the footing's eccentricity about the middle of the contact patch instead of about the cell being evaluated.** The defect is real and is demonstrated rather than argued: `bearing_moment`'s own doc states the criterion as *"is the centre of mass within a sixth of the contact width"*, and the contact width's centre is **not** `x`. Measured on an idle world (`scene=worldcrack strike=0 preset=rolling seed=7`, no verb at all), the first failure of the run: the cell at x=327 stands on a footing spanning 327..332, centre 329.5, carrying a load whose centre of mass is at **329.3** -- two tenths of a cell off dead centre -- and is crushed for an eccentricity of **2.3 against an allowance of 1**, because 327 is the footing's left *edge*. The sweep evaluates every cell of a run in turn, so the edge cells fail every time. In all **57** failures of that run the footing term bound, and by four orders of magnitude: rock whose own section could carry **165,888** was given way at **4**. **And correcting it makes the world worse, which is why this is here.** Settled at frame 7,202, two repeats, on the seed that is deterministic in every arm: shipped **−76, −76** rock (57 overload / 25 unsupported); origin corrected **−106, −106** (41 / 14); origin corrected *and* the footing's own weight added to the resultant -- both textbook-right -- **−653, −644** (25 / **902**). The full correction costs **8.5x the rock** and turns damage-free confined fissures into unsupported detachments that actually remove material. The origin-only arm is roughly neutral on damage, and its stack is *more* visibly fissured, not less. `scripts/acceptance.sh` stayed green on every arm, so the suite cannot referee this.
  *Re-test when:* The load and the footing describe the same object. That is the actual blocker and it is structural: `torque` is the moment of *this cell's subtree* while `base` is the run of rock at this row, so moving the origin makes the two **more** inconsistent -- an edge cell's subtree is a sub-part of the piece while the run is the whole of it. A per-piece footing is not available either: since ground-resting cells stopped being dependants (§6.5f) each such subtree touches ground only at its own cell, which is the knife-edge reading the run-based width was introduced to replace. Anyone retrying this needs a piece-level object carrying both its load and its contact patch, not a better formula. `Reports/structural-support-model.md` §6.5g, `open-bugs-handoff.md` §S4.


- **`src/sim/load.rs` `ground_footing_distance`'s saturating return; measured 2026-08-28** - **Refusing to hand `structural::tick` a saturated `u16::MAX`.** The reasoning is good and the measurement says no. `attached` is a worldgen flag rather than a claim about reachability, so real attached rock can carry `u16::MAX`; the footing walk's `cell.aux().saturating_add(through)` then returns the one value the whole field reads as *no path at all*, and the cell being rooted comes out reading **detached** — `evaluate_within` calls it unsupported and every neighbour relaxing off it inherits the sentinel. A false *void*, which is the worse direction of the two, and the function's own contract (*"`None` is not unsupported"*) already describes returning `None` for it. **It reverts §6.5d.** `scene=strike` goes from **289 pending sites to 7,145** against its 1,500 bar — byte-identical to `GROUND_ROOT=flat`, i.e. §S all the way back. The win on that scene *is* the sentinel: writing it is what stops the neighbourhood relaxing off a false zero, and a cell reading unreachable is re-asked rather than trusted. Pinned by `the_footing_walk_may_lend_the_sentinel_and_that_is_load_bearing`, which exists because this looks exactly like a bug.
  *Re-test when:* Only if `tick` gains a way to say "held, at an unknown distance" that is not `0` and not `u16::MAX`. Both current values are false claims in opposite directions and the field has no third one. Note the idle-world collapse that sends people to this line is **not** here — it is `dependants`, `Reports/structural-support-model.md` §6.5f.

- **`src/sim/load.rs` `support_parent`, honouring `is_anchor` instead of `own == 0`; measured 2026-08-28** - **Making a ground-resting cell a chain root in `support_parent`.** Its doc says *"`None` if it is an anchor"* and its code tests `own == 0`, which stopped meaning "anchor" when §6.5d gave ground roots a real distance — so this reads as the obvious repair. It **fixes the reported seed and breaks a quiet one**: idle `rolling 7` goes −1,756 → −416 rock, and idle `canyon 3` goes from **0 overload failures to 4,217** (rock +80 → −664) on a world that is quiet on `main` *and* on `GROUND_ROOT=flat`, so it is a new regression rather than an unmasking. The cause is over-reach: `rests_on_ground` is true for any cell with footing-grade powder beneath it, including one also welded into a cliff, and making those parentless hands them `evaluate_within`'s bearing clamp — judged as if the gravel were their only support. The `touches_bedrock` half was measured separately (`ROOT_RULE=bedrock`) and moves nothing, since those cells already store `0`.
  *Re-test when:* Only alongside a bearing clamp that is not gated on `parent.is_none()`. The load-flow half of the same repair is what shipped — `dependants`, §6.5f — and it needs no change here.

- **`src/sim/load.rs` `dependants`, the two narrower forms of the anchor skip; measured 2026-08-28** - **Restricting "an anchor is carried by nobody" to ground-resting *peers*, and to scree that demonstrably reaches rock.** Both are safer statements than the rule that shipped and **both leave most of the bug standing**, on idle `scene=worldcrack strike=0 preset=rolling seed=7` at frame 4,502 against −1,756 unfixed and −76 shipped: peers-only (skip only when the *carrier* also rests on ground) reads **−1,357**; `ground_footing_distance(..).is_some()` (the scree must bottom out on bedrock or attached rock rather than on `grain_is_footing`'s give-up branch) reads **−1,534**. They fail for one reason — the scree on these slopes is deeper than `GRAIN_FOOTING_PROBE`, so it *is* the give-up branch, and that branch is where the benefit is. A third form, requiring the stored `aux` to equal the cell's current `ground_footing_distance` ("it took the ground root"), is **vacuous**: byte-identical to unfixed, because the stored value has moved on by the time `dependants` asks.
  *Re-test when:* If `GRAIN_FOOTING_PROBE` ever grows enough to tell a hillside's scree from a mid-air pile, `is_some()` becomes the strictly better rule and should be taken — it is the one that would stop a falling pile relieving a beam. Until then the give-up branch is load-bearing and the cost is named in §6.5f.


- **`Reports/structural-support-model.md` §3, §S's sketched short-horizon gradient; measured 2026-08-26** - **Clamping the support field at a horizon and calling the saturated value "no known path".** §S's own words: *"a short-horizon field, saturating at something like 64. It is local by construction, so damage propagates 64 cells and stops."* **The premise is confirmed** — at 8192x2560 only **3.40%** of body cells store a distance at or under `load::ROOTWARD_CHECK_STEPS` (48), and 83.43% are over 256, so 96.6% of the field really is magnitude no consumer walks far enough to read. **The mechanism destroys the load DAG.** `load.rs` reads the field as a strict *order* (`support_count` is `n.aux() < own`, `dependants` is `n.aux() > own`, `support_parent` is an argmin among the strictly-lower), so a clamp creates plateaus and a cell inside one has no strictly-lower neighbour left: no supports, no dependants, `support_parent` returns `None`. Measured with `examples/support_census.rs` -- cells that have supports under the exact field and none under the clamped one, as a share of body cells: **95.38% at the sketch's own horizon of 64**, 96.42% at 48, 91.32% at 128, and still **43.29% at 1024**. The share rises with world size at every horizon (H64 reads 56.96% at 1024x320, 79.74% at 2048x640, 95.38% at 8192x2560), so a bigger constant is the wrong direction and M10 makes it worse.
  *Re-test when:* Never as a clamp of *distance-to-bedrock* -- the two requirements are contradictory, since the horizon must be small to bound the correction and small is exactly what strands. A short-horizon field has to measure distance to something **local that is itself known-good**. The hierarchical `(chunk-component level, distance-to-portal)` potential in the same report is that, is never clamped, keeps a strict order everywhere (`held->unheld` **0** on every world measured), agrees with the exact field on reachability exactly (**0** either way), and packs into the existing `u16` (max level 39, max offset 239). Its open cost is §4.7 of that report, not this.

- **`Reports/structural-support-model.md` §5, `structural::tick`'s upward writes; measured 2026-08-26** - **Forbidding `tick` to *raise* a stored distance at all** (`STRUCT_NO_CLIMB=1`) -- a relaxation that comes back worse is still judged, but is neither written nor fanned out, so every genuine rise has to arrive through `reconverge_from_damage`'s closed set instead. **Distinct from the already-recorded "make `tick` increase-aware in place"**, which *replaced* the local decision with a jump to `u16::MAX`; this removes the local decision entirely, so nothing can ping-pong. The premise -- that `reconverge_from_damage` is blind to an already-wrong field because `distance_is_achievable` asks "does a neighbour offer exactly the value I store", which a drifted region answers *yes* -- is **confirmed by measurement**: with `STRUCT_RECONVERGE=1` the `[reconv]` line reads `seeds 1 invalidated 0 repathed 0` for hundreds of frames through the cascade. **It fires and it is a trade, not a fix.** `worsened` goes 1,400/frame to 0-72, `produced` **7,491 -> 2,568**, `pending` **36,818 -> 18,217**, and paired with `STRUCT_RECONVERGE=1` frames over the 16.6 ms budget go 86.9% -> **74.2%**. **And the field gets worse: 37,629 -> 80,441 wrong cells** against the converged oracle at +1,300 frames (`scale_probe size=8192x2560 phases=1 warm=1500 frames=1600 load=blast:200:1`, `RECONVERGE_AT=1500`), all in the **stale-low** direction, which is the load-sink direction `tick`'s own ground-root comment warns about. What failed is the reasoning, not the mechanism: the 37,629 cells are a correction the field genuinely owes. The region's *true* distance is ~2,400 before the charge and after it; its stored value collapses to near zero from a false anchor at the crater (§6 of that report), and getting back is an **increase**, which Bellman-Ford performs one unit per round -- ~2,400 rounds at `STRUCTURAL_TICK_INTERVAL` being the eleven thousand frames §S measures. Suppressing it abandons the recovery part-way rather than sparing the field the work.
  *Re-test when:* Only after the field's *range* is bounded. At range 2,577 a single correction can owe 2,400 rounds; at the hierarchical potential's measured max offset of 239 it cannot owe more than 239, and suppressing the fan-out then costs almost nothing. The queue half is separable and worth keeping either way -- a rise that produces no useful information should not fan out to five sites, and that alone is the 7,491 -> 2,568.

- **`src/sim/rigid.rs`'s `settle`, and `structural::tick`'s `grounded_root`, as sources of §S's false anchor; measured 2026-08-26** - **Two ablations for "who writes the `aux 0` that a blast leaves behind".** `Reports/structural-support-model.md` §6 measures the false anchor directly: ten frames after a radius-20 charge, `stone` cells at the crater store **0 and 1** where the converged truth is **2,398** or `u16::MAX`, and by +1,300 frames the whole 327x207 neighbourhood has relaxed downhill from them and reads the true field minus a near-constant **~2,160**. **(a) `settle` writing landed body cells at `u16::MAX` instead of `Cell::new`'s default 0** (`SETTLE_AUX_MAX=1`) -- measured at 1.6% and **since overturned: it is half the fix.** The 1.6% was true of the code at that moment and false of it an hour later, because `particle::land` raised no structural check, so the cells around landed debris slept and a false 0 nothing evaluates propagates nowhere. Closing that gap woke them. Isolated by reverting only `place_landed`'s two call sites: `PARTICLE_AUX_MAX=1` alone reads **186** without the landing schedule and **27,844** with it, and both flags together read **186**. See `Reports/structural-support-model.md` §6.4a. **(b) removing `grounded_root`** (`STRUCT_NO_GROUND_ROOT=1`) -- the arm is **byte-identical** to the baseline, and a new `grounded` counter on the `[struct]` census reads **0 on every frame**, so it is a vacuous null rather than a negative result. Also ruled out by reading: `break_free` writes a `Powder` (not body material), `ParticleSystem::spawn_from_cell` carries `cell.aux()` (§Z2's fix), and every `world.set` in `explosion.rs` mutates a copy of the cell it read.
  *Re-test when:* (b) never fires on this scene and stays ruled out. **(a) is not ruled out and this entry is kept as the record of how an ablation can be right and then stop being right**: a per-source ablation is only valid while every *other* gate is held fixed, and ranking candidates by how much each moves the number assumes an independence these two did not have -- one was masking the other. **The false anchor is no longer open**: a write-seam trap in `World::set` (`AUX_TRAP=<frame>`, plus a backtrace) named `particle.rs::landed_cell` twelve times out of twelve, and landing body material at `u16::MAX` instead of `Cell::new`'s 0 takes the oracle from **37,629 wrong cells to 186**. See `structural-support-model.md` §6. Keep both entries anyway: (b) is the cleanest example in this file of a **vacuous** null caught by its own counter, and the pair is why the trap was reached for at all -- two ablations by name is the point at which to stop guessing writers and instrument the seam. **Corrected again 2026-08-27: (a) shipped, but not as `u16::MAX` -- see the next entry.**

- **A flat `aux = 0` as the value `structural::tick`'s ground root writes; measured 2026-08-27** - **The rule is right and the number was wrong, which is the same shape as the landing seam one level up.** A cell takes the ground root when relaxation found no path and it is resting on loose material -- a true statement, and the rule has to stay: a chunk landed on a pile must read as supported and must not shatter, which is what the dig cascade taught. It said so by writing `0`, and in this field `0` means *bedrock-adjacent*, so a chunk on scree told every neighbour the world's floor was one step away. Measured on `scale_probe load=strike:20:200` at 8192x2560: **8** cells stored at 0 produced **34,009** wrong ones, all in the `1k-60k` climb bucket. Replaced by `load::ground_footing_distance`, which walks the column `grain_is_footing` already walks and returns the rock under the scree plus the scree walked through: wrong cells **35,102 -> 1,337**, climb bucket **-> 0**, `pending` **39,492 -> 6,184** against a ~5,400 idle. `GROUND_ROOT=flat` keeps the arm. **A distance is a path length, not a strength** -- how much the pile can carry is `capacity`'s question and `capacity` already answers it; the `0` conflated the two.
  *Re-test when:* Never as a shipping value while any consumer reads `aux` as a distance to bedrock. Note that **removing the root instead is a separate dead end**: `STRUCT_NO_GROUND_ROOT=1` reaches 1,542 wrong cells, slightly worse than the footing value and at the cost of turning `a_cell_left_standing_on_nothing_is_asked_again` and `a_disturbance_extent_licenses_the_wound_but_not_the_chain` red. It is a diagnosis, not a proposal. And do not reach for `scripts/acceptance.sh` to judge any change here: `grounded` reads 0 through `rockdrop` and `ligament`, so the behaviour gate never fires this rule and returns a green that means nothing.

- **`u16::MAX` as the value a landing writes into `Cell::aux`, at either seam (`rigid::settle`, `particle::place_landed`); measured 2026-08-27** - **The right diagnosis with the wrong value, and it was caught by a guard rather than by a measurement.** `Cell::new`'s `aux 0` reads as *bedrock-adjacent* on an inert `Solid` and is §S's false anchor; `u16::MAX` reads as "no known path, earn one", is honest, and `tick` corrects it in a single improvement round. It recovers the whole of §S on the oracle (38,325 wrong cells -> **186**). It also turns `a_disturbance_extent_licenses_the_wound_but_not_the_chain` red and **inverts** it -- 367 cells came away with the wound licensed against 418 with a point licence -- and the settling measurement is that **`stone destroyed` inverts too** (608/638), so it was a real regression and not the fourth mode shift that count has caught. The mechanism is *timing*, not value: for one `STRUCTURAL_TICK_INTERVAL` a MAX cell satisfies `load::dependants`' `n.aux() > own` against every neighbour, so freshly settled debris hangs its entire weight on the rock beside it at exactly the moment the licence is evaluating that rock. Replaced by `structural::seed_landing_aux`, which writes the value `tick`'s own relaxation would have produced, so the transient never exists: same 186 wrong cells and guard green. **No frame-cost claim either way**: the two arms produce identical non-timing output on the blast scene -- same oracle, same `pending`, same standing census -- so their 30.74 vs 25.04 ms whole-frame means are one simulation measured twice, and that 5.7 ms gap is the machine. It is quoted in `Reports/structural-support-model.md` §6.5c as a *noise bar*, not as a result. `SETTLE_AUX=max` / `PARTICLE_AUX=max` keep the arm alive.
  *Re-test when:* Never as a shipping value while any load rule reads a *relative* order of stored distances -- the defect is not in the number but in the window before it is corrected, and that window is `STRUCTURAL_TICK_INTERVAL` wide by construction. It stays correct as the fallback `seed_landing_aux` returns when a cell genuinely has no body-material neighbour, which is a different claim: there, "no known path" is true.

- **`Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md` §S, the riser-seeded climb collapse; measured 2026-08-26** - **Boxing the cells that are *climbing* rather than the charge, and re-deriving that box with `relax_region`.** The two withdrawn attempts boxed the charge and failed because `relax_region` trusts the values just outside its box and those are stale-low for everything that routed through the crater. Boxing the risers looked like it escaped that: a cell that is not climbing is not stale, so the ring around the climbers should be correct *by construction*. **It is not, and the reason is worth the entry: a frame's risers are a sample of the climbing region, not the region.** A cell is only rescheduled every `STRUCTURAL_TICK_INTERVAL` frames, so on any given frame most of the climbing region is not rising, sits in the ring, and is trusted while being just as stale. Measured at 8192x2560, one radius-20 charge, censused against the converged oracle at +1,300 frames: **71,380 wrong cells against a 37,629 baseline**, and the error changed sign -- cells came out ~2,000 *too low* (`150 -> 2463`, `287 -> 2489`) because the ground-root fixpoint rooted cells the box could not reach. Worse than doing nothing.
  *Re-test when:* Never as a box. No box can have a correct boundary here, because "correct" is not locally observable — that is the whole content of §S. A box would have to *contain a real anchor* rather than trust one, and at ~2,400 weighted steps to bedrock that means spanning the massif. **The cause turned out not to be the framework at all** — `particle::landed_cell` carries a landed particle's `aux` only for `worth_in_aux` (food) materials, so every thrown rock lands at `aux 0`, which reads as bedrock-adjacent and acts as a load sink. See `open-bugs-handoff.md` §S's superseded architecture note for the correction, and `Reports/structural-support-model.md`. That does not revive the box: a region relaxed off a sink is still internally consistent, so a box still cannot tell a correct boundary from a stale one.

- **`Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md` §S, "Ruled out: doing it reactively, inside `tick`"; measured 2026-08-26** - **Making `structural::tick` increase-aware in place** — a distance that *rose* jumps straight to `u16::MAX` instead of writing the one-step-worse value, the invalidation step propagates but does not judge, and `grounded_root` additionally requires the cell to already read `u16::MAX` so a transient invalidation cannot root it flat. It is the textbook remedy for an increase in a shortest-path field, done per cell instead of over a closed set, and it is the cheap version anyone reaching §S will try first. **It fires and it does not converge.** Positive control: `improved` goes from ~30/frame to ~900/frame, so the field genuinely starts doing convergence-shaped work rather than climbing. Null: pending still grows linearly 23,227 to 130,403 between frames 2,400 and 13,200 on `scale_probe size=8192x2560 load=blast:200:1`, `worsened` holds at ~1,000/frame eleven thousand frames after the only event, and the whole frame is **41.19 ms against the control arm's 40.14 ms**. A monotone climb is traded for a *stable oscillation at the same throughput*, because a cell cannot tell locally whether a neighbour's `u16::MAX` is an invalidation about to be undone or a genuine dead end — so it invalidates in response, and the two ping-pong. The fan-out is the cost and it is direction-blind: `schedule_solid_neighbours` fires on `moved`, not on `worsened`, so trading 1,300 upward moves for 900 downward ones buys nothing.
  *Re-test when:* Do not retry any purely local rule in `tick` for §S. The measured alternative works and is not local: `RECONVERGE_AT` in `scale_probe` shows one `compute_world_distances` takes the scheduler from 12.49 ms to **0.25 ms** and pending from 53,077 to **6,094**, permanently — so the affected set must be *closed* (invalidate the subtree in old-distance order) and the search run from its boundary, per `Reports/structural-reconvergence-design.md` §1. The set is 67,100 cells, 0.35% of the body, against 45 on an idle control.

- **PLAN.md M17-adjacent progress entry 'Structural integrity extended to Plant'** - Reserving Cell::aux for a plant growth stage (M16's original plan) was abandoned without ever being implemented: real per-tip growth state (attractor lists, channel strength) cannot fit a u16 and belongs in per-organism state, which freed the aux slot for structural distance and later the cell-type/resource encoding.
- **README.md 'M17 status' — 'A step's cost now depends on which direction the support comes from' paragraph** - Charging a flat cost of 1 per relaxation step regardless of support direction: a 1-cell tower snapped at exactly the reach a 1-cell cantilever managed, contradicting rock being strong in compression and weak in bending/tension. Split into support_cost_below/_beside/_above (stone 0/1/3).
  *Re-test when:* All three default to 1 (the behaviour they replaced), so an un-authored material silently regresses to the rejected model — new materials need real directional costs.
- **README.md 'M17 status' — 'Confinement is what makes bulk material work, and it replaced the exemption the milestone originally shipped with' paragraphs** - Exempting terrain from structural checking entirely (because raw distance-to-bedrock condemns the interior of every mountain — a cell buried 500 deep scores 500 while being the most supported cell in the world): the whole world was structurally invalid while reading as anchored, which Reports/worldgen-design.md section 6b calls the structural-integrity landmine. Replaced by per-material confinement_radius: a cell confined on every visible side is anchored outright.
  *Re-test when:* Grounded in the world being a 2D slice of a 3D world — in-plane distance cannot observe out-of-plane support. Neither the exemption nor raw distance-only checking should return while that framing stands.
- **README.md 'M17 status' — independent-review paragraph on min_neighbour and burning neighbours** - Reading a burning Solid neighbour's aux as if it were a distance (it holds the burn timer while is_burning) can mask a real break or shatter a perfectly supported cell; and the tempting alternative — treating a burning neighbour as 'no support at all' — conflates temporarily-unusable with unsupported. The relaxation must exclude burning neighbours and defer (reschedule without writing aux) when every Solid neighbour is mid-burn.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while Cell::aux is a tagged union shared with the burn timer; reachable in ordinary play because ignite_circle force-ignites stone.
- **README.md 'M17 status' — 'Terrain is now genuinely checked, not exempt' paragraph** - Routing whole-world terrain distance seeding through the active-site scheduler: MAX_SITES_PER_FRAME would spread the computation over many frames, during which the count-to-infinity dynamic pushes cells past their span before the true anchor value reaches them — worldgen-design section 6b's predicted global collapse arriving as visible crumbling on frame one. A single converged Dijkstra run at generation instead (measured 9.1 ms for 6,616 solid cells at 512x320).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while relaxation is incremental and paced; the whole-world seeding scan becomes per-chunk under M10 streaming, at which point the cost shape changes and this decision is worth re-reading.
- **README.md 'M17 status' — 'That scene's width tracks the span, and has to' paragraph** - A 7-cell-wide bridge demo scene against a span raised past 3: cutting the anchor removed one cell and the remaining six stood (all comfortably in reach of the surviving edge), so the scene printed a healthy-looking bridge twice and proved nothing, with every test green. Caught only by looking at the output. Keep the scene wider than twice the span.
  *Re-test when:* Re-check the scene width whenever confinement or direction-weighted costs move the effective span.
- **Reports/building-rethink.md §4a "Tried once, reverted, and what it showed" (and §3a)** - Intact-until-damaged implemented as an *exemption* — `load::is_structurally_interesting` skipping intact cells and `paint_capsule_as` marking placements intact — was built and backed out the same session: `scene=ligament` stands forever because nothing ever asks, and §3a shows binary immunity means one chip cascades and levels a castle. Protection must be a large capacity multiplier (the `attached` bit's `attached_span_bonus`), not an exemption; the §1 edit that skips evaluation of intact cells is explicitly marked wrong and must not be made.
  *Re-test when:* Holds as long as protection is binary. The multiplier form (§4b) passed all six acceptance cases including ligament and is the recorded way back in — it was reverted only over three stale unit tests and two clippy lints, not on merit.
- **Reports/destruction-plan.md §"The open tension, stated precisely"** - Constant-tuning the shared span number: raising `max_unsupported_span` 16→40 while dropping `attached_span_bonus` 12→2 (holding terrain capacity nearly constant, 1536→1600) made built spans hold but stopped `scene=undercut` spalling entirely. Reverted. One number is doing two opposite jobs (player span strong, undercut shelf weak), and no setting exists — the fix is a model change, not a constant.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while player-built stone and freshly-undercut rock are both merely `attached == false`. A third state, or a capacity penalty decaying with distance from the cut, is what would justify re-testing.
- **Reports/destruction-plan.md Phase B "Turn the model on for what the player builds" (B1/B2)** - Three shipped binary cannot-fail hatches were removed: treating `aux == 0` as proof of anchorage (compounded by `rigid::settle` writing `aux = 0`, making landed debris a permanent anchor), the `is_resting_on_ground` powder exemption, and the per-frame-budget-exhaustion exemption. Each is binary immunity — precisely what the four earlier support models were rejected for — and together they switched the model off for player structures.
  *Re-test when:* Do not reintroduce any cannot-fail guard. Powder should terminate the support chain (so landed debris does not shatter) without zeroing aux or skipping the torque test — a granular pile's bending capacity is approximately none.
- **Reports/fracture-mechanics-design.md §0 "The defect, stated precisely"** - Reach/distance-to-anchor (`max_unsupported_span`) as the failure criterion is backwards: at the owner's ligament case, the two-cell neck sits at *low* distance and passes while the far tip fails — but rock fails where stress is highest, at the neck. Replaced by `load > capacity` on accumulated bending moment (two additive scalars, M and Sx, up the parent forest).
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional as a criterion. §3.7 adds: its vestigial form keeps passing vacuous tests once superseded — delete `max_unsupported_span` or explicitly reinterpret it, never leave both half-live.
- **Reports/fracture-mechanics-design.md header + §0 (also building-rethink.md §2, underground-definition.md "The deeper reason no reach works")** - Four successive support models inferred "is this held up" from shape — confinement radius, thickness, attachment-as-anchor, then reach/span — and every one was either strong enough to hold a mountain or weak enough to drop a player's wall, never both; each conferred permanent binary immunity that nothing revoked. Playtesting rejected them all. The fix was stating the difference as data (the `attached` bit), not more tuning.
  *Re-test when:* Geometry cannot distinguish a mountain from a wall someone stacked — the difference is history. Do not retry shape inference unless the distinguishing fact exists as per-cell data.
- **Reports/load-concentration-review-response.md §2 'The best parts' (section 7 of the reviewed report)** - Correcting column strength from quadratic to linear in width with a single multiplier: built, measured (scene=worked went 7 to 918 overload failures), and withdrawn. The sharp reason: the shelf case and the column case share `base`, so no single multiplier can recalibrate one without breaking the other; the load-share report §7 records four things any replacement must clear.
  *Re-test when:* Held while shelf and column capacity share one base constant; a replacement that decouples them is the recorded route.
- **Reports/load-concentration-review-response.md §6 'Smaller points' (wall=3 finding)** - The handoff's recorded suspicion that load concentration caused the non-monotonic wall=3 span=200 collapse is retired: on the load-share binary the scene loses 48 cells rather than 1,064 and is identical with sharing on and off, so concentration was not the root cause.
  *Re-test when:* Measured on load-share 43a57b7 from master bef468a; the suspicion should not be re-recorded against concentration whatever else moves that scene.
- **Reports/load-model-fit-review.md §2b 'Do not spread attachment. Read the parent's.'** - Spreading attachment cell-to-cell (the naive chain rule; equivalently generalising the B background brush, which grants full attached on placement): rejected because attachment is also what is_structurally_interesting uses to skip evaluation, so spread attachment does not merely strengthen structures, it makes them invisible to the model — an attached castle is an unjudged castle. The parent-edge keyed_bonus achieves the joint without propagating anything.
  *Re-test when:* Held while attachment doubles as the evaluation skip; if that coupling were split, the unbounded 12x-everywhere strength objection would still stand.
- **Reports/load-model-fit-review.md §2c 'The verb, and where any new state lives'** - A sparse side table on World caching load state — the thing load-model-handoff §3 asked for — was refused by the implementing commit because a derived quantity cached outside the computation goes stale. The review upholds the refusal for derived data.
  *Re-test when:* The objection does not transfer to authored data: an authored joint (mortar material, or an authored side table with BTreeMap/sorted-Vec and clear-on-set) cannot go stale by definition, and the material route is preferred as free.
- **Reports/load-model-handoff.md §2c / §5.1 (equal distances double-count)** - Deriving the support subtree by flooding to greater-distance neighbours (copying label_failing_region's >= contour) is wrong for load accumulation: two equal-cost paths count a subtree twice, and with any zero-cost step the parent relation can cycle so accumulation never terminates. Record the parent explicitly instead; label_failing_region's >= is deliberate for finding a detached piece and must not be copied for sums.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for accumulation; the contour use remains correct for detachment.
