- **Reports/load-model-handoff.md §3 'Storage — do not add fields to Cell'** - Iterating a HashMap for the structural side table: iteration order is randomised per process and determinism is required — this codebase has hit exactly this twice (issue #7's trap). Use a BTreeMap, or HashMap lookup plus a separately sorted Vec for any ordered walk.
  *Re-test when:* Held while same-build determinism is required (PLAN.md — note this was reversed from 'not required', so older comments disagree).
- **Reports/load-model-handoff.md §4 'What to delete in the same change' (support_cost_below)** - support_cost_below: 0 (free compression in stone.ron) existed only to stop towers snapping under the reach model, and created two degeneracies: the zero-cost tie (whole regions share a distance, parent relations can cycle, accumulation never terminates) and a self-consistent zero — a floating blob where every cell claims support from the one below is stable at distance 0 and never reschedules, instrumented and confirmed.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional under any distance-relaxation support model; under load a tower stands because it carries little, which needs no free compression.
- **Reports/load-model-handoff.md §6 'Four support models have been tried. Do not retry them.' (model 1)** - Confinement as an anchor — a cell buried deep enough counted as anchored outright. Made terrain stable and made thickness into immunity: nothing at or past the threshold could fail anywhere, mid-air included. Removed; its tests were deleted as vacuous (an undisturbed slab sits at a self-consistent distance and stops rescheduling, so they passed while testing nothing — §5.7).
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional: geometry cannot tell a mountain from a stacked wall. Distinct from crush-in-place, which keys confinement to the failure *outcome*, never the criterion — the two are one word apart and only this one is the dead end.
- **Reports/load-model-handoff.md §6 'Four support models have been tried. Do not retry them.' (model 2)** - Thickness scaling the span, inferred from burial: per-cell burial gives every free face a bare span, so thick rock eroded one skin at a time from every surface; taking the max over a neighbourhood fixed that and made structures too strong. Parked on branch structural-thickness-wip, never merged.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — same geometry-cannot-distinguish through-line as the other inferred models.
- **Reports/load-model-handoff.md §6 'Four support models have been tried. Do not retry them.' (model 3)** - Attachment as an anchor: right that support is a property of what the material is, but anchoring on it made attached rock immune — an undercut shelf could never fall however much was dug from beneath it. Attachment now buys reach/capacity (attached_span_bonus), never immunity.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; the fit review's §0 warning is that the same binary immunity keeps re-arriving as bookkeeping (aux==0, powder-below, budget exhaustion) and must be recognised as this dead end in new clothes.
- **Reports/load-model-handoff.md §6 'Four support models have been tried. Do not retry them.' (model 4)** - The reach model (each cell's own distance against its own span) cannot fail a worked root: a crack at a beam's root weakens a cell whose distance would never fail at any span, so a 160-cell shelf survives six blows at its join and a two-cell ligament goes unnoticed. Superseded by torque > capacity (landed in 7e13e42).
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; scene=worked is the reproduction. Note the fit review found organism_structural_tick still using max_unsupported_span as a reach limit — the superseded criterion living on is §5.7's trap, not a reason to retry it.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1a-ii 'The constant is calibrated, not derived'** - ARCH_LOAD_K extremes were swept and rejected: k=2 holds everything (100/100/61) and k=8 collapses everything identically to no arching (26/30/25); k=4 is the setting that separates ant tunnel from gallery. It is a game constant, not Terzaghi's coefficient — do not read it as the textbook number.
  *Re-test when:* Calibrated against flat-scene bores 5/9/13 at ~8 cells cover; re-sweep if bore sizes, cover depths, or the capacity arithmetic change.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1a-ii 'Three things this got wrong first, all caught by measurement'** - Arch relief applied to the mass (min(mass, Hp)) never bit: under 8 cells of cover roof-cell mass is already below the cap, A/B read 30%/39% vs 30%/40% — knob not connected. The binding constraint is the span through torque = M x arm, so relief must clamp the arm.
  *Re-test when:* Held at shallow-to-medium cover, where relief is the only place it matters; re-check if arching must ever operate where load rather than span binds.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1a-ii 'Three things this got wrong first, all caught by measurement'** - Clamping only the ceiling course of a tunnel: the cells actually failing are in the roof mass above it, so the clamp reached one row and changed nothing on two of three bore sizes. A roof cell must look down through its own cover to find the opening it roofs.
  *Re-test when:* Held while failure lives in the roof mass rather than the ceiling row; unconditional under the current criterion.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1a-ii 'Three things this got wrong first, all caught by measurement'** - A 20-cell cover probe for arch relief cost 2.7x frame time (62.56 vs 23.06 ms, over the 60 ms budget on caveshallow); cutting it to 8 cells gave 23.01 ms with the discrimination table completely unchanged. The deep half of the scan was pure cost.
  *Re-test when:* Held for the current scenes' discrimination table; reopens only if arching must discriminate at cover deeper than 8 cells.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1b 'What has been ruled out, by measurement'** - Granular capacity as a cap rather than a divisor: 899/215/0 cells lost became 25,838/51,262/0. The cap sits far below what a deep intact section resting on soil received, and generated terrain is mostly that shape. Two failed attempts on the granular term mean the term is not where the fix goes.
  *Re-test when:* Held under the flat 64x divisor and eager ground rooting; moot once the planned bearing model replaces the term.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1b 'What has been ruled out, by measurement'** - Stale distances as the cause of the one-dig worldcrack erosion: relax=1 (a converged pass immediately after the dig) was identical to the cell on eight of nine preset/seed pairs. Staleness is not this bug.
  *Re-test when:* Staleness is real elsewhere (canyon seed 3 went 5,364 to 394 with relax=1), and every figure in that section must be re-baselined against the live-churning worldgen commit before trust.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1b 'What has been ruled out, by measurement' (superseded entry)** - Gating the granular capacity divisor on parent.is_none() was recorded as a dead lever (exactly zero cells moved) — and that record was itself wrong: the gate was vacuous because structural::tick rooted any powder-backed cell at distance 0, so every such cell was parentless by construction. With ground demoted to a last-resort root the same gate is meaningful (worst case 27,409 to 70 cells) but overcorrects.
  *Re-test when:* The rejection held only while eager ground rooting made the condition degenerate. The recorded follow-up is a bearing model (compression-only bed, kern rule, depth-proportional capacity) replacing the flat 64x divisor, not re-abandonment of the gate.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1c 'Ruled out this session: it is not the failure cascade'** - Deleting the failure cascade (structural::tick's schedule_solid_neighbours after a failure) to stop chaining does nothing: bit-identical output on flat seed 24301 strike=12 (95 overloaded / 75 unsupported both ways), 18-run sweep worst case unchanged at 1,228. The distance-relaxation wavefront is what actually propagates a collapse, so 'no chaining' must be a policy (bounded disturbance radius/time), never a deletion.
  *Re-test when:* Held while tick's if-moved branch reschedules touched cells and their neighbours — the cascade is redundant with the wavefront by construction.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §3 'What has been tried and must not be retried'** - Raising max_unsupported_span 16 to 40 with attached_span_bonus 12 to 2 (holding terrain capacity constant) does make built spans stand — and stops scene=undercut spalling entirely. Rejected as trading one owner requirement for another.
  *Re-test when:* Held under the reach-based criterion; the criterion itself is superseded by torque > capacity, but the shape (buying span globally to fix built structures) remains rejected.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §3 'What has been tried and must not be retried'** - Scheduling the parent cell on settle (continuous load maintenance): pending sites climbed 26 to 4,064 and frame cost 2.5 ms to 3,160 ms. The bounded in-tick chain walk replaced it; do not maintain load continuously.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional at world scale — cost grows with settle traffic; the bounded walk is the replacement's bar.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §3 'What has been tried and must not be retried'** - Dividing torque by the section: fixed a beefy block, broke scene=undercut. Peak bending stress M/D² is already modelled (capacity carries D², torque carries M); dividing again gives M/D³ and double-counts, because a shelf's rows already chain independently.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional under the torque/capacity split as built.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §3 'What has been tried and must not be retried'** - Grading the attachment bonus as the mean over a section's cells (three loosened of seventeen costing 3/17 rather than the full 12x) took scene=undercut to zero failures — per-cell weakness is the spalling mechanism itself, not an artifact — and did not even help its target case, since at a cut the whole cross-section is loosened and the mean equals the minimum.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; recorded in capacity's source comment.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §3 'What has been tried and must not be retried'** - Treating intact/attached as an exemption from failure broke scene=ligament, which fails from geometry alone: a structure standing only by exemption has no answer when anything asks, so one chip levels a castle. Attachment must be a capacity multiplier, never an exemption.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; the ligament scene is the guard.
- **Reports/organism-substrate-design.md §2/§2's structural branch (on-demand bounded search)** - Caching anchor distance in aux for organism-owned Plant cells (the Solid incremental-relaxation strategy) was rejected because their aux is already spent on cell-type-plus-resource; organism connectivity is an on-demand bounded BFS from the organism's own anchors instead of an O(1) relaxation step.
  *Re-test when:* Acceptable because organism structures are small relative to MAX_THICKEN_SCAN_CELLS-scale spans and the search runs only on disturbance; a freed per-cell storage slot would reopen the incremental option.
- **Reports/organism-substrate-design.md §5 Connectivity: correcting a premise** - The plan's instruction to 'factor structural.rs's BFS-from-anchors into a generic primitive' rested on a wrong premise: structural.rs runs no BFS at all — it is an incremental Bellman-Ford-style relaxation over aux-cached distances, and there is no full-graph BFS to extract. 'The same function, reused' is impossible because the two callers need different storage strategies; the shared primitive is reachable_from_anchors, sharing traversal shape but not caching.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — a reading-the-code correction, not a tuning outcome.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §6d 'Roots stabilize soil — the mirror, correctly identified'** - PLAN.md's framing 'extend anchor-distance credit outward from a root into adjacent soil' does not work: Powder cells do not participate in structural.rs at all — no anchor distance, no break-free, they just fall via update_powder — so there is no distance to extend credit into. The correct mirror is a check in update_powder (a Powder cell with a root neighbour does not move).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while powders remain outside structural.rs.
- **Reports/prior-art-destruction.md §2.1 (flood-fill connectivity row)** - Connectivity-only support (the Noita/Teardown/DRG/Dwarf Fortress model) was rejected as the primary model: it cannot express "this is overloaded", so it can never make a worked root give way — the specific owner complaint that motivated load.rs. It is retained deliberately as the fallback when the load answer is unavailable (`is_supported`'s bounded flood).
  *Re-test when:* Right as a fallback, wrong as the model, for a game about mining and building; re-weigh only if overload failure stops being a requirement.
- **Reports/prior-art-destruction.md §2.1 (per-cell stress field row of "the two ideas everyone converges on")** - Representing support as a scalar per-cell stress/pressure field (The Powder Toy's shape) was evaluated and rejected: bending moment is directional and second-order, defined relative to a support point, so a scalar field cannot represent it without a companion "which way is support" field — which is the parent forest again, gaining nothing.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the failure quantity is a moment. Evaluated in the survey rather than attempted in-repo.
- **Reports/prior-art-destruction.md §4.1 "A real solver, at any point on our roadmap" (also destruction-plan.md "What we are explicitly not doing")** - A real stiffness solver was seriously evaluated via the survey and rejected as a category error, marked "Do not revisit": solver games (Poly Bridge, Besiege) run tens-to-hundreds of authored members with a build/simulate split; 512×320 growing to streamed chunks means thousands of unknowns per frame with no author-imposed structure. Nobody ships this over an open world.
  *Re-test when:* Recorded as unconditional — a category error at cell scale, not a tuning or performance question.
- **Reports/prior-art-destruction.md §4.3 "support_cost_below: 0" (also fracture-mechanics-design.md §3.1)** - A free (zero-cost) vertical support step was shipped and deleted: zero-cost ties let whole regions share a distance, "decreasing distance order" stops ordering parent before child, the parent relation can cycle, and the load walk never terminates. 7 Days to Die ships exactly this shape (free unlimited vertical, budgeted horizontal) and it is that game's most complained-about system. "Do not reintroduce it."
  *Re-test when:* Do not reintroduce a free step anywhere without re-deriving §3.1's termination argument for the accumulation walk.
- **src/sim/cell.rs const FLAG_ATTACHED doc (and world.rs paint_capsule_as "four earlier support models")** - Two named attempts to infer background anchoring from shape — "a sufficiently confined cell is anchored", then "thickness scales how far a cell can span" (four support models in all) — failed identically: any rule strong enough to hold a mountain made player constructions indestructible, and any rule weak enough to let buildings break ate the mountain. The distinction is stated as data (the attached bit), never inferred.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent: it is not a property of shape, so no tuning setting exists; only revisit if the mountain/built distinction itself becomes unnecessary.
- **src/sim/load.rs const MAX_REGION_CELLS** - The old 4,000-cell region cap was a cost workaround masquerading as a physical limit: scene=ligament's 110x40 overhang overflowed the search, which defaulted to 'still supported' and left 4,400 cells of rock hanging in open air. Do not shrink the cap to control search cost again.
  *Re-test when:* Raised to 20,000 once the per-frame anchor cache (AnchorMemo) made the support search O(region) per frame instead of per check; a small cap is only tempting while that cost problem exists.
- **src/sim/load.rs const MAX_SUBTREE_CELLS** - Unbounded per-cell subtree walks measured 6,556 ms/frame on scene=capped and 4,456 ms on scene=strike (every cell of a solid block flooding thousands of cells). Also, resolving a truncated walk by guessing high fails terrain upward — the direction this subsystem was burned by three times; the partial sum is a genuine lower bound and must resolve toward 'holds'.
  *Re-test when:* Cap size set from those measurements; the truncation direction is inherent to the model.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn arch_span body, comment on the cover probe** - Testing only the cell immediately beneath the roof for the opening was tried and was far too narrow to matter: the clamp reached one row while overload failures happened in the rock above it, and the A/B came back identical on two of three bore sizes. The whole roof beam arches, so a bounded downward cover probe is required.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn arch_span doc, section 'The problem, which is geometric and not a bug'** - Tuning the pre-arch span model to let short tunnels stand cannot work: a 140-cell corridor under 8 cells of cover collapsed at every bore size, because nothing in the model knew how big the hole was — in a 2D side view a bore is a slot whose roof genuinely spans the whole excavation. The owner's ant-tunnel-vs-mine expectation was unreachable by any tuning.
  *Re-test when:* Held while the model contained no measure of opening size; arch_span (Terzaghi with B = Ht) added that measure.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn arch_span doc, section 'Why this is the arm and not the mass'** - Applying the arch relief to the load (min(mass, Hp)) was tried first and did almost nothing — only the A/B switch showed it: bore 9 measured 30%/39% of its cave surviving with relief on versus 30%/40% off, because at these depths a roof cell's mass is already below anything the arch would cap. The span (moment arm) is the binding constraint, so the relief belongs on the arm.
  *Re-test when:* Measured at ~8 cells of cover; if load ever becomes the binding constraint (far deeper cover), re-measure.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn bearing_moment body, contact-width comment** - Sizing the granular footing from only the cells whose own underside touches grain was tried first and is wrong twice over: a pile is lumpy, so a slab on its own rubble read as several knife-edge footings and was dismantled one cell at a time — 48,109 cells lost to one dig on scene=worldcrack preset=flat versus 894 for the bug it was meant to fix — and it judges an instantaneous patchy contact the conforming grain erases within frames. The footing is the piece's run of rock with anything beneath it.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn bearing_moment doc ('This replaces GRANULAR_CAPACITY_DIVISOR') and const KERN_DENOMINATOR doc** - The flat 64x GRANULAR_CAPACITY_DIVISOR was a counterweight for structural::tick rooting eagerly on powder — two modelling errors annulling each other, so every tuning attempt made some case worse. Once rooting was fixed it became pure overcorrection (scene=roomcut fell to 2 overload failures against its bar of 5) and was deleted outright rather than left as a dead constant that reads as a live lever.
  *Re-test when:* Held only while eager powder rooting existed; the no-tension bearing (kern) model replaced it.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn capacity body, heading 'Tried and reverted: averaging the bonus over the section'** - Grading the attachment bonus as the mean over the section was tried and reverted: it broke scene=undercut (went to zero failures — per-cell weakness IS the spalling mechanism, at section 6 with 3 loosened rows the mean read 6.5x where the old rule read 1x) and did not help the case it was written for (at the cut every section cell is loosened, so mean equals minimum). Wrong on both counts.
  *Re-test when:* The thin-wall collapse it targeted actually comes from mine's detach band reaching across a wall that thin (2,516 cells lost to a radius-1 chisel in a 17-cell wall) — see Reports/next-session-handoff.md; fix there, not by grading the bonus.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn capacity body, heading 'Tried and reverted: gating this on parent.is_none()'** - Gating the granular capacity cut on parent.is_none() was recorded as a dead end because not one cell moved across six presets and three seeds (scene=worldcrack preset=flat stayed at 599/637/338 cells lost, to the cell). That rejection was vacuous, not wrong: structural::tick then rooted every powder-backed cell at distance 0, so the condition was universally true. Do not re-record a zero-delta as 'lever dead'.
  *Re-test when:* Rejection held only while eager powder rooting existed; after ground was demoted to a last-resort root the gate discriminates correctly and the granular case moved into bearing_moment.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn capacity body, keyed-attachment comment** - 'Adjacent to attached becomes attached' spreads without limit — the floor is attached, so everything built on the ground inherits the bonus; this is how all four earlier support models made player structures indestructible. The attachment bonus must be a property of an edge (this cell or its support parent is attached), which cannot chain.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn capacity doc, section 'Where base comes from, and what happened to max_unsupported_span'** - Leaving max_unsupported_span half-live after the load model replaced reach was the considered alternative and is explicitly the trap: when confinement_radius was superseded, its tests kept passing while testing nothing (an undisturbed slab sits at a self-consistent distance and stops rescheduling). The constant was reinterpreted (base = span^2/2, the reach a one-cell-deep unattached beam fails at) — one meaning or none.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn evaluate_within, anchor-guard comment** - Using aux == 0 as the 'anchored, skip evaluation' guard: player-painted cells start at aux 0 and rigid::settle deliberately writes 0 on landing, so both were permanently immune to every failure mode — the binary immunity all four earlier support models were rejected for, moved into a guard where it was invisible. scene=capped evaluated 2 cells out of 15,840 across 600 frames. The guard must ask the world (touches_bedrock), never the cached distance.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn evaluate_within, bedrock-adjacency comment** - Routing the evaluation exemption through is_anchor (which includes resting-on-powder) made the granular-capacity branch unreachable: destruction-plan B2 shipped dead by its own commit, and a few grains of rubble were a full anchor for everything above them. The exemption must be bedrock adjacency only; a powder-rested cell terminates a chain but still gets torque-tested.
  *Re-test when:* unknown; guarded by test a_cell_resting_on_powder_is_evaluated_against_a_granular_capacity.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn evaluate_within, comment 'A column is not charged the beam's moment'** - Charging columns the full eccentric moment of what they carry was the unzip: torque read an identical 76,028 from y=170 down to y=300, 140 cells below the roof that produced it, so any wall cell losing its attachment bonus failed instantly anywhere. The standing 'runaway propagation front' hypothesis was disproved by that measurement. The arm on a vertical support step is clamped to the column's half-thickness (kern) — a clamp, deliberately not an exemption.
- **src/sim/load.rs fn evaluate_within, comment 'Dividing the moment by the section was tried here and reverted'** - Dividing the moment by the section to fix single-path load concentration was tried and reverted: it fixed the holed block but broke scene=undercut — capacity already carries D^2, so the extra divide makes peak stress M/D^3 (over-strong, shelves stop spalling), and it double-counts sharing that shelf rows already have through independent chains to the cliff.
  *Re-test when:* The one-pixel-red-line concentration defect is real and open; the recorded fix direction is load sharing between genuinely parallel supports (a flow problem — destruction-plan E1), not another divide.
- **src/sim/load.rs module doc, paragraph 'This is not the thing §2c forbids'** - Re-deriving a cell's subtree by flooding to 'neighbours with greater distance' must not be done: equal-cost paths make it count a subtree twice, and a zero-cost step lets it cycle outright. Only flooding to 'neighbours whose parent is me' is safe, because parent is a function (each cell has exactly one).
  *Re-test when:* Inherent while support-step costs can tie or be zero; guarded by test a_subtree_counts_every_cell_once_where_two_paths_are_equally_short.
- **src/sim/load.rs module doc, section 'Where the forest comes from, and why nothing is stored'** - Storing each cell's support parent in a sparse side table on World (as Reports/load-model-handoff.md §3 called for) was evaluated and rejected: support_parent re-derives the identical argmin from neighbours' stored distances, deterministically and exactly, so a stored parent can only be staler, and a position-keyed side table is a determinism trap (PLAN.md issue #7). Do not add the table back.
  *Re-test when:* Settled by measurement: the largest subtree any acceptance scene produces (~1,500 cells, the 'worked' shelf ligament) floods well inside one frame's budget. Re-test if subtree sizes grow far beyond that (e.g. M10 streamed worlds).
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn shed_stranded_leaves doc (and the abscission comment in organism_upkeep's Photosynthesize arm)** - Scheduling structural checks from leaf abscission was tried and measured at 26x destruction: the organism support search is hop-bounded, so a mid-crown check reads everything past the span limit as unsupported and amputates the upper crown — 772 cells against 20,213 at the same shedding rate, the only difference being the check. It masqueraded as 'the shedding mechanism collapses the stand at any setting' across a whole eight-setting sweep. Stranded sprays are handled by a bounded leaves-only component walk instead.
  *Re-test when:* Holds until the organism support search anchors properly; Phase 3 damage work will meet the same landmine — any mid-crown disturbance today over-amputates, and Phase 3 damage results are contaminated by this until fixed.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn compute_world_distances body, edge-anchoring comment** - A first version guarded the mirror's neighbour reads with a bounds check, treating off-world as nothing — which un-anchored every cell touching the world edge and took six load tests down with it. Off-world must read as bedrock, reproducing the Cell::OUT_OF_BOUNDS convention.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn compute_world_distances doc, section 'Cost, as measured rather than as hoped'** - Running the pass through World::get cost 579 ms at 2048x640 (76.2 ms at 512x320) versus 175 ms (15.0 ms) via a flat mirror — do not route the bulk pass through hashed access. Also, an earlier version of the doc named the seeding scan as the dominant half, which is wrong at scale: true only on a one-screen, mostly-sky world; a mostly-rock world inverts it, and acting on the stale claim would have fixed a third of the cost.
  *Re-test when:* Which half dominates depends on world composition (sky-heavy vs rock-heavy); re-attribute with a probe before optimising either half.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn compute_world_distances doc, section 'Why this is a direct pass and must not go through the scheduler'** - Converging worldgen distances by scheduling a StructuralCheck on every terrain cell is wrong twice: MAX_SITES_PER_FRAME (2000) spreads it over many frames, and during partial convergence the count-to-infinity dynamic is live — a cell whose true distance is small climbs past its own span before the real anchor value reaches it, breaks, and takes neighbours with it: terrain visibly crumbling on a fresh world's first frames. Converge first, write once.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn relax_region doc, section 'Why a stroke needs this and a reactive relaxation is not enough'** - Letting the reactive per-tick relaxation converge freshly painted material was the shipped behaviour and is a scheduling artifact: paint starts at aux 0 (reads anchored) and the true distance climbs one round per ~5 frames, so a 192-cell column takes over fifteen seconds to decide whether to fall — 'I built a thing and it collapsed ten seconds later', the single most-reported building complaint. Strokes get a one-pass seeded regional re-convergence instead.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn tick, comment 'Settling alone is not enough'** - Judging only when the distance settles never judges a piece with no anchor at all: its cells climb by one each tick forever (count-to-infinity), so a blob painted in open air hangs permanently. A risen distance — support got worse — must also trigger judgment; a fallen one costs nothing.
  *Re-test when:* unknown; caught by test an_unsupported_foreground_blob_does_not_hang_in_mid_air.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn tick, comment 'The distance wavefront is computed once, here, and every early return below carries it'** - Letting early returns (out of budget, uninteresting, deferred) drop the distance fan-out froze the relaxation dead: on scene=capped the first check's support search ate the whole frame budget, and at frame 3,000 all 15,840 cells were still at aux 0 with zero load evaluations — the column 'stood' because nothing ever asked; and (next_frame, x, y) dispatch let the frozen low-x structure starve every other structural check. Every early return must carry the propagation.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn tick, end-of-function comment on the settled branch** - Scheduling the parent when a cell settles (to fix the chain-ordering bug) was tried and reverted: every settling cell raised a fresh check on its parent, which raised one on its parent, faster than the queue drained — scene=capped pending sites climbed 26 to 4,064 with frame cost tracking from 2.5 ms to 3,160 ms. failing_along_support_chain walks the chain inside the tick instead, queueing nothing.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn tick, judge-on-settle comment** - Running the full subtree judgment on every step of the distance wavefront cost 4,456 ms/frame on scene=strike and 6,556 ms on scene=capped, and it reads the support forest mid-flight (load-model-handoff §5.2's stale-parent hazard). Cells are judged once, on the tick their distance settles or worsens.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn tick, post-failure comment 'Measured inert on the big-strike scene, and kept anyway'** - Do not hunt collapse chaining in the post-failure neighbour scheduling: switching it off entirely produced bit-identical output on preset=flat seed=24301 strike=12 (95 overloaded / 75 unsupported, same regions, same cells) and moved an 18-run seed sweep's worst case not at all (1,228 both ways). The distance-relaxation wavefront is the actual route a collapse propagates by; this scheduling is kept only for disturbances the relaxation does not reach.
  *Re-test when:* Becomes load-bearing only if the relaxation ever stops rescheduling those cells itself.
- **src/sim/structural.rs module doc, opening section** - Distance-to-anchor (reach > span) as the failure criterion is superseded: reach answers 'how far out are you', which is not the question — a crack at a beam's root weakens a cell that was never near any span limit, so a worked root never gave way and an overhang on a two-cell ligament did not notice. Distance now only orders the support forest; load.rs decides failure.
  *Re-test when:* unknown; see Reports/fracture-mechanics-design.md.
- **src/sim/structural.rs module doc, same section (support_cost_below)** - support_cost_below = 0 (standing on rock is free) existed only to stop towers snapping under the reach model, and it broke the support forest outright: whole regions shared one distance, so 'closer to an anchor' stopped ordering support at all. It is now 1.
  *Re-test when:* Held only under the reach model; under the load model a tower stands because it carries little, so the zero has nothing left to do.
- **src/sim/structural.rs module doc, section 'How terrain is checked, and why it used to be exempt'** - Exempting pre-placed terrain by leaving aux at its default 0 ('indistinguishable from anchored for any cell nothing ever asks about') worked for hand-placed terrain and is a landmine at worldgen scale: the entire world structurally invalid but reading anchored, with any scheduling change causing global collapse (worldgen-design §6b). Replaced by compute_world_distances at generation, which terrain genuinely satisfies.
  *Re-test when:* Held while terrain was one hand-placed screen; worldgen-scale worlds made it untenable.
- **src/sim/structural.rs module doc, section 'Why attachment is stated, not inferred'** - Two attempts to infer support from geometry — 'a sufficiently confined cell is an anchor', then 'thickness scales how far a cell can span' — failed identically: each was tuned until terrain stood and each made everything the player built indestructible; weakened enough to break built structures, each ate the mountain. No setting separates the cases because the difference is not a property of shape; it is now stated as data (Cell::attached). crush_in_place repeats: 'confinement as an anchor' is retired and must not come back (load-model-handoff §6.1).
  *Re-test when:* None within geometry — only new per-cell data could change the calculus. Confinement may only decide what a failure produces, never whether it happens.
- **src/sim/structural.rs module doc, section 'Why one step's cost depends on its direction'** - A flat cost of 1 per relaxation step regardless of direction makes a 1-cell tower built up from the ground fail at exactly the height a 1-cell cantilever reaches sideways — rock is strong in compression and weak in bending. Replaced by support_cost_below/beside/above per material.
- **src/sim/structural.rs test a_burning_solid_neighbours_burn_timer_is_never_read_as_its_distance** - The neighbour-scanning loop once read a burning Solid neighbour's aux — its burn-timer countdown, not a distance — as structural data, so a 500-frame timer instantly exceeded stone's span and broke the cell above. Fields are separate now, but the surviving rule stands: defer any structural read of a burning neighbour, whose material may change out from under the reader.
  *Re-test when:* The specific aliasing became impossible when aux and the burn timer became separate fields; the defer-while-burning behaviour is still load-bearing.
- **src/sim/world.rs fn paint_capsule_as (attached comments)** - Cell::attached as "part of the background massif", authored through a separate brush mode with a touch-the-massif extension rule, is superseded: attached now means intact (a capacity multiplier revoked by every destructive verb), after a play report asking for effortless building that still breaks realistically. An exemption instead of a multiplier is the recorded failure shape that killed the four earlier support models — nothing ever revoked it, so one chip's cascade reaches everything.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while C1 keying gives material joined to terrain its bonus at the joint (which serves the old brush mode's purpose) and while every destructive verb revokes intactness.

## destruction  (78 entries)

- **`assets/materials/log.ron` `colors` — a desaturated grey for the piece tier** - Chosen so a landed piece would separate from `litter`'s gold and `deadwood`'s dark brown, and it did separate — as the wrong thing. Watching the fall frame by frame the owner's words were *"the trunk of the tree doesn't move it just turns gray"*: grey is what ash and stone are in this world, so a bole settling in place read as tissue **dying** rather than as a log. Replaced by pale cream timber, which separates from `deadwood` on value and from `litter` on hue.
  *Re-test when:* Never for a material meant to read as *wood*. The entry generalises: legibility in a debris pile and "reads as the right substance" are two constraints, and solving only the first picks a colour that says something false.

- **`assets/materials/log.ron` `colors` — a warm mid-brown for the piece tier** - The obvious correction to the grey above, and it fails the other way: warm mid-brown (166,132,96 to 184,152,118) is the same hue family as `litter` (132,96,44 to 182,146,78) at nearly the same value, so the piece tier vanished into the foliage it was lying in. Measured by eye at 4x on the settled `scene=fell` pile, which is the only way it could be measured.
  *Re-test when:* Only if `litter`'s palette moves off gold. The axis that works is **value** against `deadwood` and **hue** against `litter`, which is what pale cream does and neither of these did.

- **`assets/materials/log.ron` `colors` — a wide palette spread on the *piece* tier** - `log` shipped its first draft with a 44-unit spread across its four colours, copied from the convention every other debris material uses (`deadwood` (64,43,26)-(96,66,40), `litter` (132,96,44)-(182,146,78)). Wrong, and the reason generalises: a wide spread makes a field of cells draw randomly across a range, which is *speckle* — exactly right for grains and exactly what destroys the one thing a piece tier has to show, the **shape** of the fragment. The settled `scene=fell` pile read as one undifferentiated mound with 572 cells of log invisible inside it. Narrowed to 18 units and shifted to a grey timber, off `litter`'s gold and `deadwood`'s dark brown.
  *Re-test when:* Never as stated for a piece tier; the entry is here because the pull toward "match the other debris palettes" is strong and the convention is right for everything except this. It reverses cleanly for anything that *is* granular.

- **`assets/materials/deadleaf.ron` `kind: Solid` — the foliage piece tier as an immovable solid** - The obvious reading of the owner's "the leaves shouldn't turn to powder ever", and it leaves the tree **dying standing in the shape it grew in**. A severed crown converts to Solid in mid-air; a piece tier is opted out of the structural check, so the first pieces to land become a scaffold nothing can ever fail, and every later body stalls on it and settles where it stopped. Rendered on `scene=fell fell=7150` at 3x, unmistakable. **The finding underneath is worth more than the rejection: what has always *looked* like a crown collapsing was powder flowing downhill, not a fall.** Nothing in the engine makes a severed piece travel sideways, so removing the grain removes the fall. Replaced by a Powder at `friction_angle: 85` -- it falls, but does not *flow*.
  *Re-test when:* Only once a severed piece genuinely travels and comes to rest lying down (`Reports/tree-mechanics-plan-2026-08-29.md` §7 stage 4). With a real fall the scaffold cannot form, and Solid becomes the better answer.
- **`assets/materials/deadleaf.ron` `max_unsupported_span: 2` — a Solid foliage tier that holds up nothing, including itself** - The correction to the entry above, on the reasoning that dead foliage is not a load path, and it fails the opposite way just as hard: the crown comes apart on the way down and the settled box goes to **80% loose grain against a 76% baseline**, with `deadwood` 557 -> 941 as the failing foliage takes the logs' support down with it. Two settings of one knob, opposite failures, which is `CLAUDE.md`'s own signal that the knob is not the problem.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional as a *tuning* of the Solid attempt; the axis is wrong, not the value. Falling and running out are two properties and only the second one is the dust.
- **`assets/materials/log.ron` `colors` — two palette families, bark on the surface and cut timber inside** - The **fourth** attempt on this palette, after the wide spread, the grey and the warm brown above, and it fails in a way none of those did: the bark family was `wood.ron`'s band 3, one fixed tone, so **every felled tree got birch-grey bark whatever species it was**. Four failures on one axis is the tell -- all four searched for the right colour, and the owner's answer was that there is not one: *"The color should not change at all (not sure how many times I have to say this)"*, then *"and the trunks and branches shouldn't change colour either."* `log` now carries `wood`'s palette entry for entry and `rigid::settle` copies the cell's own shade across, so the colour is bit-identical. It also restores what every re-roll was destroying unnoticed: a `wood` band is the individual's bark density, chosen by its genome, and a `leaf` band is its species, so re-rolling flattened a varied stand to one tone the moment it hit the ground.
  *Re-test when:* Never as a search for a piece-tier colour. The requirement those four attempts came from -- that a piece be distinguishable from the grit it lies in -- is satisfied by `deadwood` and `litter` staying off wood's range, which they already do.
- **`src/sim/load.rs` `evaluate_within`'s bearing clamp, reinstated for `log` by removing its `capacity != i64::MAX` guard** - Tried as the fix for `open-bugs-handoff.md` §Q ("the long skiny vertical pieces should fall over"), on a correct source reading: `bearing_moment` *is* a tipping test, and the opt-out guard is what stops it reaching a landed log. **Giving logs the tipping test does not make them lie down -- it crushes them.** Measured on the identical cut: lying/upright/square 3/8/2 -> **1/11/1**, `log` 833 -> 716, `deadwood` 553 -> 645, i.e. *more* pieces standing, because the ones it condemned were the ones with a footing and the needles survived. The load model's verdicts are *holds* and *fails*, and failing means `breaks_into`: convert where you stand. So a bearing test that correctly finds an over-eccentric piece can only say so by turning it into grit.
  *Re-test when:* Only alongside a `topple` outcome -- an unbalanced piece re-promoted as a body and allowed to rotate. The predicate is right and the consequence does not exist; rotation and the tipping test are one change, and the test is worthless before the rotation.
- **`assets/materials/log.ron` — no `breaks_into`, to stop landed pieces being crushed** - Removing `log`'s grit target to keep the piece tier alive was tried while chasing T1's landed-log attrition and is a treadmill, not a fix: a structural failure that cannot convert its cell is rescheduled forever. Measured on `scene=fell fell=7150` at frame 8,750: **42,267 unsupported failures over 158,230 cells**, against 302/1,144 with `breaks_into` present. The log count did hold (1,057 standing against 431), which is what makes it tempting.
  *Re-test when:* Only if a material can be made genuinely *exempt from evaluation* rather than merely unconvertible — the cost here is the check firing, not the conversion. The underlying cause is `Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md` T1a.

- **`Reports/explosion-stone-review.md` §17e — smoke as the cause of "cracked chunks don't fall"** - Traced the owner's "chunks fully cracked all the way around stay put and don't fall into the leftover hole/crater" to `Gas` not being displaceable by a chunk body, and to `region_has_free_face` testing raw `EMPTY`. Both were real bugs and both were fixed — but on the shallow charge that actually makes a crater, before and after are **byte-identical** (79 confined failures, 1,239 cells, unchanged). A new counter showed 44 pieces genuinely cut loose, largest 292 cells, **none of them wedged**: they are judged *supported*, not confined. `depowder=300` named the real gate — `load::rests_on_ground` is "the cell below is a `Powder`" feeding `is_anchor`, so one cell of a slab over soil anchors the whole slab (promoted 972 -> 4,687 with powder removed; largest still-loose piece 292 -> 27).
  *Re-test when:* Never as stated — the smoke fixes are correct and shipped, they are simply not this complaint. The real fix is `31f06b9` on `claude/water-phase-changes-ki6g8c`, the third version of the `rests_on_ground` predicate. Do not write a fourth.

- **`Reports/explosion-stone-review.md` §17b — a containment metric that counts every changed cell** - The first draft of `filmstrip::damage_radius` measured the furthest cell whose material differed from the pre-blast snapshot, and read **296** on a single radius-20 charge — almost all of it smoke drifting to the world edge and debris grains landing. Both are real changes and neither is damage. Narrowed to "was `Solid`, is not the same material now", the same charge reads 63.
  *Re-test when:* Not a lever to retry; recorded because it is `CLAUDE.md`'s whisker rule in a new place — the metric counted what a blast *looks like* rather than what it costs. Sanity-check any new damage metric against a world with no charge in it (this one returns `-1`, deliberately not `0`).

- **`src/sim/fracture_field.rs` `pitch_at` — a smooth spatial gradient on the joint pitch** - Varying the Worley pitch smoothly with position cannot work here and the reason is structural rather than aesthetic: the severing rule is `domain(a) != domain(b)`, an identity test between two adjacent cells, and its watertightness depends on both having been mapped by the *same* lattice. Under a smooth pitch almost every neighbouring pair reads a slightly different lattice, every comparison is meaningless and the web dissolves. Quantising the pitch on a coarse lattice of its own works instead, because inside a band the pitch is one constant.
  *Re-test when:* Only if the severing rule stops being an identity test between neighbours — i.e. a different fabric mechanism entirely.

- **`src/sim/fracture_field.rs` `BAND_PITCH` at 64** - At a band pitch of 64 a radius-20 blast's halo crosses three or four grain bands, and every crossing is a welded seam (the `other_pitch != pitch` guard stops the web there). Measured 9,052 promoted cells against 11,671 for a uniform grain — a 22% loss. At 128 the same measurement clears the uniform baseline. 192 was worse than 128 again, which is seed spread rather than a trend.
  *Re-test when:* Only against the four-seed order statistic, never a single seed — the 64/128/192 readings on seed 1 alone would have picked the wrong value twice.


- **PLAN-log.md 'Live playtest feedback (screenshots of cargo run...)' item 1, explosions** - The old per-cell debris roll chance(1.0 - sqrt(dist2/r2)) vaporized ~72% of a blast (90/317 cells became debris, reproduced by temporary revert) because a circle's area is dominated by its outer band where the curve is low — it read as 'vaporizes everything, no force'. Replaced with a small deterministic vaporize core, unconditional debris in the primary radius, and a shockwave annulus that flings loose material.
  *Re-test when:* Owner-judged feel verdict ('I want to see sand flying'); unconditional.
- **README.md — 'Playtest feedback drove two more changes' paragraph (code: src/sim/explosion.rs)** - The old explosion debris roll chance(1.0 - sqrt(dist2/r2)) put the odds against debris almost everywhere a circle's area actually is (the outer band): a reproduction test confirmed only 28% debris in a dense fill, so blasts vaporized nearly everything and showed no visible force. Replaced by a small deterministic vaporize core (VAPORIZE_FRACTION = 0.12), unconditional debris in the primary radius, and a fading shockwave-pickup annulus to radius*1.8.
  *Re-test when:* Playtest-driven (satisfying-feel requirement); unknown further condition.
- **README.md 'M15 status' — 'A real ordering bug' paragraph** - Running fireball ignition before the blast-radius clearing step (or targeting it inside the clearing radius): the clearing unconditionally wiped the same larger radius and silently erased the fire it had just set. Ignition must target a ring beyond the clearing radius — also the sensible design, since a fireball inside a hole has nothing left to burn.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent ordering constraint for any explosion rewrite.
- **README.md 'M15 status' — 'Debris realism' paragraph (code: src/sim/particle.rs)** - One flat shared GRAVITY for all particles: identically-launched particles fell in lockstep forever and read as a moving block. Particle now carries its own drag/gravity_scale, drawn once at spawn from ParticleSystem's internal RNG and held for the whole flight.
  *Re-test when:* Unknown; the per-particle variation is what makes a scatter read as a scatter.
- **README.md 'M15 status' — 'Debris realism, added later (overnight run section 6)' paragraph** - Scaling debris launch jitter by raw strength: a * strength term pins every particle to particle::MAX_SPEED_PER_AXIS's clamp and makes debris more uniform, not less. Jitter must scale by each cell's own computed speed.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while MAX_SPEED_PER_AXIS clamps particle velocity.
- **README.md 'M15 status' — 'Rebuilt after a diagnosis pass' paragraph (see Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md)** - The original M15 explosion: resolved in a single frame, reused the debug force-ignite tool for the fireball (setting stone alight regardless of flammability), and had no debris pierce — so a buried charge threw nothing at all. Rebuilt to expand over Tuning::duration frames, write real CA temperature for the fireball, and give debris Particle::pierce.
  *Re-test when:* Measured in the diagnosis report; the rebuilt shape is the bar any replacement must meet.
- **README.md 'M8 status' — 'A real bug was caught while writing this module's own tests' paragraph (rigid::label_component)** - A naive flood fill over MaterialKind::Solid: Cell::OUT_OF_BOUNDS carries material BEDROCK (kind Solid), so the entire world boundary reads as one giant connected wall and any component touching an edge floods along it to max_cells (a 5-cell blob reported 1000). Both the seed check and every neighbour must go through is_body_material (Solid and not BEDROCK).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while OUT_OF_BOUNDS is modelled as bedrock — the same single-check trick structural.rs's anchor detection relies on.
- **README.md 'M8 status' — 'Also built: rigid::trace_contours' paragraph** - Classic marching squares for contour extraction: it exists to walk a continuous scalar field and needs a 16-case lookup table plus a saddle-case tie-break specifically for interpolation ambiguity — none of which applies to an already-binary occupancy grid. Directed boundary-edge stitching is the equivalent, unambiguous approach, and handles holes with no special-casing (a hole boundary falls out wound the opposite way).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the input is binary occupancy; a continuous scalar input would re-justify marching squares.
- **README.md 'M8 status' — chunk-bodies trigger paragraph ('The trigger uses a failure predicate rather than label_component')** - Triggering chunk-body promotion by labelling connected solid from a failing cell: label_component from there would sweep in the whole mountain the overhang is still attached to. The flood must be over 'solid, unowned, and past its own span', using the distances structural.rs has already written by then.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while promotion fires after structural distances exist; a promotion path that runs before distances are computed cannot use this predicate.
- **README.md 'M8 status' — independent-review paragraph on the pinch-point contour walk** - Terminating the contour walk only on returning to its own start point (n == start): a pinch point (two cells sharing only a corner, connected elsewhere) silently drops one lobe's exit edge and reroutes the walk into the other, already-closed lobe's cycle, which never revisits the dropped lobe's start — an infinite loop growing memory without bound. The walk must also break on revisiting any already-visited point (pinch points are then wrong-but-bounded, as the module doc always claimed).
  *Re-test when:* Pinch points are still not correctly resolved, only safely bounded — a correct resolution is future work, but the start-only guard must never return.
- **README.md 'M8 status' — 'Still not built' paragraph, quarter-turn rotation (rigid::ChunkBody::spin)** - Continuous (arbitrary-angle) rotation of chunk bodies: a cell grid cannot hold a slab at 37 degrees without the resampling leaking material on re-rasterization, so bodies tumble in quarter turns only — a 90-degree transform is exact and can never gain or lose a cell.
  *Re-test when:* Re-open only with a re-rasterization scheme that conserves cells at arbitrary angles.
- **README.md 'M8 status' — 'Still not built, in pipeline order' paragraph** - Adding rapier2d (plus earcutr/glam) for falling chunk bodies was deliberately rejected: what a falling chunk needs is gravity, a grid fit test and a settle rule, not a constraint solver — and rapier's enhanced-determinism feature cannot be combined with its parallel feature (coupling-research section 0.2), against the determinism requirement PLAN.md reversed to required.
  *Re-test when:* Everything except the integration step is shared with a rapier version if one ever lands; re-opening requires resolving the determinism-vs-parallel feature conflict.
- **Reports/destruction-plan.md Phase C §C3 (ROOTWARD_CHECK_STEPS doc-comment conflict; also prior-art-destruction.md §3.2)** - ROOTWARD_CHECK_STEPS = 16 was tried and was too small: it left scene=ligament's neck standing at stress ratio 1.87 (recorded in the constant's own doc comment). Do not simply shrink the 128-step rootward walk to get locality.
  *Re-test when:* The bound being a step count is the condition. The recorded alternative is a disturbance-anchored bound (distance from what actually changed), and any change must be re-verified against scene=ligament by eye.
- **Reports/destruction-plan.md Phase D §D1 "Fail the section, not the cell"** - Fixing dust-instead-of-pieces at the fragment stage cannot work: `take_fragment` was changed to BFS specifically to stop thin-pixel-line fragments and could not help, because the `NEIGHBOURS_4` tie-break decomposed slabs into independent single-row chains (measured: mass 115 torque 6555 = exactly one row of a 12-deep shelf), so `rigid::fracture`'s input was already one cell thick. The fix was D1 — union the failing cell's subtree across its section (built; bodies at first capture went 13/255 cells → 102/1,888, dissolving debris 277 → 30 cells).
  *Re-test when:* Tuning the fragment ladder is vacuous whenever the regions handed to fracture are degenerate. Related: `DETACH_DEPTH: 3` and `CRACK_DETACH_DEPTH: 2` were sized under the pre-load-model rationale ("pieces can only be as thick as the loosened rock") which the load model silently invalidated — re-derive them, not scope creep.
- **Reports/destruction-plan.md Phase F §F2 (acceptance harness)** - Judging acceptance scenes by outcome alone was how `scene=capped` came to be reported as passing while the entire 15,840-cell structure was frozen and had never been evaluated — "it still stands" was true and meant nothing. The harness must assert the mechanism fired (`failures: overloaded N`) alongside the outcome.
  *Re-test when:* A scene that passes with N == 0 when it should collapse is the vacuous case; any outcome-only check over this model can pass on dead code.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md §2/§3 (round 1) + round 3 "Result"** - Converting every cleared cell to debris in a single frame was measured and rejected: 2,513 particles for a radius-20 blast, 86% landed after one frame, refilling their own crater (43% on the first frame, fully gone by f+30); the naive full-debris prototype peaked at 7,213 in flight. Staged spawning across the blast duration plus `Tuning::debris_fraction` 0.4 gives an 810 peak, and fewer, longer-lived, faster debris is both better-looking and cheaper.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for look and cost; particle counts during a blast are the number to watch if debris volumes ever grow.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md §4 "The fireball is a stamped ring with a step-function lifetime"** - Using `ignite_circle` (the M14 debug force-ignite tool) as the blast fireball was rejected: it stamps a geometrically perfect annulus whose every cell gets the same burn duration on the same frame (measured 520 cells burning, constant f+1..f+180, then 0 — a frozen decal), and it ignores `flammability` entirely, so stone with flammability 0.0 burns and the ring becomes the whole effect. Replaced by `Blast::scorch` writing cell temperature, rolling `flammability`, with `BURN_DURATION_JITTER` (±50%) for ragged burnout.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for the player-facing effect; `ignite_circle` remains a debug tool only.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md round 1 findings + round 3 "what was built"** - The M15 single-frame detonation (trigger clears, spawns debris, and ignites all in one frame) passed its signoff criterion and was rejected on look: six stone-scene samples spanning frames 61→211 were an identical motionless orange donut for 2.5 seconds. An explosion is a sequence and needs a time axis. Replaced by `Blast`, a cavity front expanding one stage per frame (`Tuning::duration`, default 10).
  *Re-test when:* The signoff test asserted only "throws debris that re-integrates on impact", which the dead version satisfied — outcome tests cannot defend against this; the rejection was by eye at play.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md round 2 §"Prototype: a pierce budget on free particles"** - Exchange-based piercing — deposit the carried grain in the cell being left, pick up the one being entered — was tried first and rejected by measurement: it deposits at every step, riddling the pile with displaced grains and holes that read as static rather than motion; it transports material only one cell per exchange however far the particle flies; and it tripled "material disturbed" while leaving "material thrown clear" at zero — the wrong one of the two to move. Carry-through pierce (`Particle::pierce`, `PIERCE_SPEED_RETENTION` 0.82) won, and is cheaper (no CA writes per pierce).
  *Re-test when:* Exchange conserves mass locally — revisit only if local conservation ever outranks look. Note the carry version required `land`'s bounded nearest-empty ring search, or deeply buried blasts silently delete grains.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md round 2 §"The other half: the default explosion is far too small"** - Coupling blast radius to brush radius (default 6) was rejected: a 113-cell crater with zero particles in flight two frames after detonation, and even the pierce fix moved only 149 cells — invisible on a 512×320 screen. "No amount of correct physics on 113 cells will read as an explosion." Decoupled into `Tuning::radius`, default 22.
  *Re-test when:* The brush default is right for painting; the coupling itself, not the constant's value, was the error.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md round 3 "Two bugs found while building it"** - Heat-only ignition — writing cell temperature and relying on `fire::try_ignite`'s temperature path — cannot light anything: no shipped material has a finite `ignition_temperature` (every material file leaves the "never" default; oil's comment says so). A fireball that only wrote heat would have looked right and started no fires, so `scorch` also rolls `flammability` directly, the same property neighbour-contact ignition rolls.
  *Re-test when:* Holds until materials ship finite `ignition_temperature` values; `oil_beside_a_blast_ignites_but_stone_does_not` pins both halves.
- **Reports/fracture-mechanics-design.md §0/§2 (rigid::label_failing_region "whole appendage" flood)** - The "when anything fails, take the whole appendage" flood exists only because the reach model's root never fails on its own — roughly the right outcome by the wrong mechanism, and it degrades exactly on partially-cracked overhangs. It must be removed in the same change that lands load>capacity, or both fire and a single failure detaches twice.
  *Re-test when:* Tied to the reach criterion; once the root genuinely fails, the detached region is a consequence (ordinary connectivity), not a mechanism.
- **Reports/fracture-mechanics-design.md §5 "Build order" (the recorded correction to step 2)** - Shipping capacity-from-cracks alone as an independently-judgeable step was built, measured, and did almost nothing: with failure still evaluated per cell as own-reach vs own-span, a crack at a beam's root weakens a cell at distance ~10 that no span setting ever fails; six blows at the join of a 160-cell shelf left it standing (`filmstrip scene=worked`). Capacity is half of `load > capacity`, not a step — the two sides must land together (§3.6: shipping load first makes cracks decorative too).
  *Re-test when:* Held under the reach criterion; moot once load>capacity is the criterion. The durable lesson is checking which cell a rule actually evaluates before promising a step can demonstrate itself.
- **Reports/load-model-handoff.md §5.8 and Reports/load-model-fit-review.md §3** - Gating fracture on MAX_BODY_CELLS ('bigger therefore skip') was written twice — rigid::strike and rigid::try_promote_failing_region — so the larger the failure the more certainly it declined fracture and dissolved to per-cell dust. A size cap belongs on a fragment, never on whether a region breaks; the fit review notes the same shape recurring as budget-exhaustion-means-supported in is_supported.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; any 'if too_big { return }' in a failure path is a claim the largest cases deserve the least behaviour.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1 'The unzip: the previous diagnosis was wrong'** - Diagnosing the room unzip as a self-propagating evaluability front (is_structurally_interesting making each removal's neighbours evaluable, feeding rigid::fracture sub-MIN_FRACTURE_CELLS regions) was disproved on scene=room: material below the cut read 'not evaluated' at every frame, failing regions measured 80-1,573 cells, and 45 chunk bodies formed. Do not go back to the propagation front; the real cause was the column-moment defect fixed in 0b5b175.
  *Re-test when:* Disproved by direct measurement; the reproduction is three commands on scene=room. Only a change to is_structurally_interesting's adjacency rule would merit re-checking.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1a-ii 'It was a row of circles, and that invalidated the first envelope'** - tunnel= spacing bites dig*2+1 apart produced a row of separate chambers joined by thin pillars, not a tunnel, so every 'does a tunnel hold' number before the fix measured the wrong geometry; the non-monotonic bore result (24/744/14 cells for bores 5/9/13) is retracted — with a real corridor every bore size holds with zero rock destroyed.
  *Re-test when:* step= now defaults to dig (half-overlap). Any conclusion resting on tunnel= before that commit must be cross-checked, not reused.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1a-ii 'What actually governs it: roof cover'** - 'rock destroyed' as a cave-survival metric was evaluated and found perverse (64/402/0 across depths whose dumps read worst-to-best): it conflates how completely the roof failed with how much material was present to lose. Replaced by roofed_void / min_cave= (reads 10%/41%/100%, the ordering the dumps give).
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — the conflation is structural to the metric.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1a-NEW (original diagnosis) 'The fix is a design call, so ask'** - Tuning dig_yield to get the gnome through his own bore: the sweep moved ending-x in both directions (0.0/0.2/0.35/0.55 gave 178/192/189/173) because rubble ramps him over disc scallops — the knob reads the wrong quantity. Do not tune dig_yield for traversal.
  *Re-test when:* Held while bores were rows of overlapping discs; rigid::mine_swept's capsule corridor fixed the wedging outright, so this is historical unless disc bites return.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1c 'Ruled out by measurement'** - Crack rays as what scales a big strike: CRACK_RAYS 5 to 0 (a strike scoring no cracks at all) still eats 10,852 cells vs 12,283 on flat strike=12. Do not spend time on CRACK_REACH or ray counts for the lateral surface unzip.
  *Re-test when:* Held for the big-strike magnitude question; the crack pattern's look is a separate live thread (§1c-i).
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1c 'Ruled out by measurement'** - Detaching only near the impact (DETACH_REACH, crack runs full length but unbraces only within one bite): flat strike=12 improved 16,634 to 12,283 but strike=20 worsened 10,541 to 18,502. A knob moving the number in both directions is reading the wrong quantity — do not retune it.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional by the repo's own rule: bidirectional movement means the rule reads the wrong quantity; no setting exists.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1c-i (dead ends recorded in commit c709b4c's message)** - Axis-stepped (integer-quantised) crack walkers: the heading's dominant axis persists so cracks come out as long horizontal/vertical runs at right angles — the criss-cross again, from quantisation rather than the path. Sub-cell position fixed it.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for integer-stepped walkers; keep sub-cell positions.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1c-i (dead ends recorded in commit c709b4c's message)** - A complete crack lattice with no ligaments: every block became an island and therefore unsupported — 2,400 confined failures per 400 frames, still climbing at frame 2,000, with the world dead still throughout.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — fully severing blocks makes every block fail its support test.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1c-i (dead ends recorded in commit c709b4c's message)** - Straight crack rays from origins scattered through the failing piece: owner's verdict was 'criss cross irregular lines', not a spreading crack. Independent strokes crossing read as scribble; one origin with forks reads as a crack.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional owner judgement on the look; the sub-cell walker from one origin superseded it.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1c-i (dead ends recorded in commit c709b4c's message)** - Restricting the crush-in-place fissure walk to the failing region made the mechanism a near no-op: 189 confined failures wrote 29 cells of fissure, producing bit-identical images across two complete pattern rewrites — only FailureCounts::crushed_cells could see it, because an overload's region is a sparse union of subtrees.
  *Re-test when:* Held while failing regions are sparse subtree unions; caught only by a counter, so pair any retry with one.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1c-i (dead ends recorded in commit c709b4c's message)** - Crack pattern for crush-in-place as a regular lattice every sixth row and column: arithmetic fine, but it drew a perfectly regular 6x6 crosshatch reading as wireframe over the hillside. No metric said a word; only looking caught it.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — a regular lattice reads as graph paper at any spacing.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §1d 'Spoil: decided, and the options that were weighed'** - A blanket spoil-vanish hardcoded inside rigid::mine was written, measured and reverted: it breaks player::at_full_yield_nothing_leaves_the_world (yield 1.0 must never delete material) and drops roomcut to 2 overload failures against its bar of 5. Vanish is dig_yield = 0.0 (the shipped CLEAN mode), never a code change.
  *Re-test when:* Held while the conservation contract and roomcut's bar stand; the tunable already expresses vanish, so hardcoding is never needed.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §3 'What has been tried and must not be retried'** - Narrowing the detach footprint (DETACH_DEPTH 3 to 1, CRACK_DETACH_DEPTH 2 to 1) changed nothing on scene=room (2,595 to 2,540 cells lost) because one loosened cell on a load path carrying the roof's whole moment is already fatal. Constants are back at 3 and 2.
  *Re-test when:* Measured only on scene=room while the column-moment defect (since fixed in 0b5b175) dominated; §1b explicitly says this experiment is worth re-running on worldcrack/flat, where there is no column.
- **Reports/next-session-handoff.md §4 'The measurement loop'** - Censusing damage by counting every non-empty cell was tried and lies: it reported canyon gaining 167 cells on a run where nothing failed, because a Liquid cell spreading into two half-full ones is +1 occupancy at unchanged volume. Census Solid and Powder specifically, and read the rock/rubble split.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional while liquids hold continuous fill in aux.
- **Reports/prior-art-destruction.md §4.2 "Truncation that makes bigger structures safer" (also destruction-plan.md "What we are explicitly not doing")** - Size caps that gate or degrade *whether* failure happens: `MAX_SUBTREE_CELLS` truncation understated a large subtree's torque, so the larger the overhang the less likely its neck was judged overloaded — the third instance of the twice-written "a cap must bound work, never gate whether something happens" lesson, this time by degrading the evidence rather than the decision. destruction-plan records the instance fixed.
  *Re-test when:* The class is unconditional. Incidental recorded finding: `supported_subtree` tests cell count while `subtree_sum` tests pending-stack depth — not the same bound. §3.3's push-from-the-break makes the whole-subtree cap non-load-bearing.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs const SCORCH_RAGGEDNESS / Tuning::flash_temperature (docs)** - The old force-ignite path (World::ignite_circle from the blast) was rejected twice over: it ignited materials regardless of their ignition temperature (stone should glow and cool, not catch), and it drew a geometrically perfect annulus of uniform colour that read as a stamped decal. Replaced by scorch writing CA cell temperature with position-keyed raggedness, with fire::try_ignite alone deciding ignition during the sweep.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while fire.rs owns ignition decisions; a blast may only write heat, never burning flags.
- **`examples/filmstrip.rs` fn severed_islands / Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §S5; measured 2026-08-29** - **Reading "the largest severed piece in the world" as "rock the blast cut loose".** `severed_islands` counts every maximal 4-connected solid component the cracks left unconnected, and nothing in that definition says *rock*, or *near the charge*, or *not already a separate object*. On `preset=terraced seed=7` the largest such piece is a **390-cell floating ice sheet at x 23..108** while the charge is at **x=300** -- it holds because ice floats, which is correct, and it is present with no blast in the world at all. Filed as a 376-cell chunk welded to a hillside by load-budget starvation; retracted the same day. The real pieces near a charge are **8 to 108 cells**, and they split into two different problems (§S5-a supported-while-severed, §S5-b failing-but-standing). The tell was recorded and not read: the 200x budget control moved the piece **376 -> 365** while promoting 8.1x more rock elsewhere, and a lever that moves everything except the thing it is meant to explain is saying so.
  *Re-test when:* Never re-derive it; use `largest_severed_piece_near`, which excludes `floats` materials and boxes to the charge. The general rule is `CLAUDE.md`'s: the world-wide census had to be run on a world with **no blast in it** before it could be trusted about one with a blast in it, and it was not.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs fn open_seam (doc), fn seam_width** - **A uniform one-cell seam, and an opened zone bounded by a circle.** The doc argued it directly: *"one cell wide, always -- the reference's thickness comes from the lines being void rather than from their being fat, and a two-cell seam eats the world for no extra legibility."* Both halves were measured and only one held. The world-eating fear is real but small: widening rides on boundaries that were opening anyway, so it frees no extra blocks and its whole bill is the extra seam cells -- `seedsweep.sh` over 3 presets x 4 seeds on `blast=300,8,20,180,60`, run to rest, `rock destroyed` total **5,062 -> 6,831** and max **1,022 -> 1,397** for `joint_seam_width` 1 -> 3, both arms from one build. The legibility claim was simply false of the *pattern* even though it is true of a single line: one weight everywhere is a binary outcome, and the owner reported it as one (*"the cracks should be thicker near the explosion and thinner far away with variation between cracks"*). The circle was the sharper defect -- the open/scored transition sat at a fixed radius and read as a ring. Both replaced by a width read off each boundary's own headroom; `joint_seam_width: 1` keeps the uniform seam reachable as the A/B control.
  *Re-test when:* The cost was measured at 12 runs, under the sweep's own "only 12 runs" warning, and only for `joint_seam_width` 3. If a full PRESETS x SEEDS grid puts the widening materially above the +35% of rock destroyed measured here, the cap is the first thing to drop -- it is one integer and it changes nothing else.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs debris test ('Counting only particles used to be the same thing')** - Counting only particles as 'the blast turned this into something that flies' stopped being valid once blasts began cracking crater walls into coherent chunk bodies — rock that would have been grit becomes chunks, and scoring that as a loss would have the guard pushing against the feature. Debris is particles plus chunk-body cells.
  *Re-test when:* Held only before crater-wall fracture existed; any new flying-matter representation must be added to the count.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs fn shockwave_pickup_chance (doc)** - Dividing the shockwave falloff by a rounded span (rounded shockwave_radius - radius) produced negative chances in the sliver between the true and rounded outer edge; Rng::chance silently treats negative as never, so the annulus quietly narrowed below what the constant claimed. The falloff is built from the continuous radius, clamped.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while chances route through Rng::chance's silent negative handling; audit any probability built from rounded geometry.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs test 'An explosion must not turn a promoted body's reserved container cells into flying nothing'** - Routing the blast's 'is there material here to destroy' question through the managed-aware Cell::is_empty() treated a promoted body's container cells (materially EMPTY, reported non-empty) as destructible, spawning debris particles carrying material::EMPTY that landed and wrote themselves back into the world. The blast uses the raw material test; particle landing deliberately keeps is_empty because its question is availability.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while is_empty is managed-aware by design; every new destruction path must choose which question it is asking.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs test debris_velocity_varies_within_a_single_field_tile** - debris_velocity sampling the coarse field at exactly +/-FIELD_SCALE gave every cell within a field tile the same quantized gradient, launching them with bit-identical velocity — a moving block rather than a scatter (confirmed to fail with DEBRIS_JITTER_STRENGTH zeroed). Per-particle jitter (plus per-particle drag/gravity spread) is what separates them.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while launch velocity reads a FIELD_SCALE-quantized gradient; re-test if the field gains sub-tile resolution.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs test near 'an earlier version tried to ignite this same inner region'** - An earlier version ignited the inner blast region before clearing it, and the clearing step then silently erased every cell it had just set on fire — the work was ordered so the effect destroyed its own evidence. Clearing must precede the scorch ring, which lies beyond the cleared radius.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the cavity clear and heat write are separate stages; any reordering must re-run the regression guard.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs Tuning::debris_fraction (doc)** - Spawning a debris particle for every cleared cell (effectively 1.0) produced ~2500 particles at radius 20, of which 86% landed again on the very first frame. Fewer, longer-lived debris both looks better and costs less — the one diagnosis-list item whose frame cost is negative.
  *Re-test when:* Re-sweep the fraction if particle lifetime or launch velocity models change; the waste was first-frame landings.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs Tuning::radius (doc)** - App::explode used to pass the brush radius as the blast radius. The brush default (6) excavates 113 cells: measured, four faint orange smudges on an undisturbed surface with zero particles still in flight two frames later — a sensible painting default is a terrible explosion default, and no amount of correct physics on 113 cells reads as an explosion. The two defaults are now separate.
  *Re-test when:* Holds whenever one constant serves two verbs; keep blast radius its own tuned value.
- **src/sim/explosion.rs Tuning::vaporize_fraction (doc)** - An earlier version rolled per-cell debris odds of 1.0 - sqrt(dist/radius); since a circle's area is dominated by its outer band, most of the affected area vaporized with nothing to show, reading as 'a clean circle disappears, thin ring of sparks'. Replaced by a small fixed vaporize core with debris everywhere else — an explosion's visual signature is material flying outward, not a clean hole.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any radial-falloff destruction chance; check area weighting before shipping one.
- **src/sim/particle.rs step, pierce comment ('An exchange version was tried first')** - Exchange-based piercing — deposit the carried grain in the cell being left, pick up the loose cell being entered — was tried first for local mass conservation. It riddled the pile with a trail of displaced grains and scattered holes that read as static/corruption, and transported material only one cell per exchange (a bucket brigade, not ejecta): measured, it tripled 'material disturbed' while leaving 'material thrown clear' at zero. The particle carries its grain straight through instead, which is also strictly cheaper (no CA writes per pierced cell).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while pierce exists to let debris cross loose cover; mass stays conserved because the grain rides the particle until landing.
- **src/sim/player.rs fn face_toward (doc)** - Aiming a dig by clamping the cursor onto the reach circle looks right and was wrong: clicking deep inside a massif put the bite behind the rock face, carving a sealed pocket whose spoil had nowhere to go, so stone turned to rubble in place — on screen, a dig that does nothing (rubble and stone are deliberately near the same grey). Replaced by a ray to the first blocking cell, which digs the near face like a pickaxe and guarantees open space behind the bore.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while spoil must be displaced rather than deleted; any aim that can land behind a sealed face re-creates it.
- **src/sim/player.rs fn face_toward, loose-material comment** - Stopping the dig ray at the first blocking cell of any kind meant the gnome's own spoil shielded the face he was cutting: after two bites the bore held rubble, so every later bite landed on the muck and re-broke broken material — measured over 63 bites into a solid massif, the world lost only 77 cells and the tunnel could not advance. The ray now reaches past powder to the first hard cell, with loose material only a fallback target when no rock is in reach.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while digging displaces spoil into the bore; if spoil is removed from the bore instead, re-test.
- **src/sim/player.rs fn bore_slice (doc)** - Anchoring a bore stroke on the near edge of the box, so each press cuts the first `bore_bite` cells of the passage.
