The first stroke clears them and every stroke after it cuts air, so **holding the button drives the passage exactly one stroke and then stops**; the passage only advances if the player walks forward between presses, which nothing on screen states and which reads as the tool having broken. The slice advances to the first slab with something breakable in it instead. Guarded by `one_stroke_is_a_slice_and_not_the_whole_box`'s `assert_ne!`, watched failing for this fault.
  *Re-test when:* Never as written. Any face rule must advance past what the previous stroke already removed, or a held button is a single stroke.
- **src/sim/player.rs fn bore_slice, the two-level face rule** - Defining the bore's working face as "the nearest slab that is not empty". A cut leaves a `dig_yield` fraction behind as spoil, and deep in a massif that spoil has nowhere to be thrown (`displace_rect` reaches four cells and every one of them is rock or more bore), so the near slab never empties and the face never advances: measured, **80 of a 144-cell box still standing after sixteen strokes** that should have cleared it twice over. The face is the nearest slab holding a `rigid::is_tool_target` cell, falling back to any non-empty slab so a bore into a dune still works.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while mining leaves spoil in the bore. If spoil is ever removed from the bore rather than displaced within it, the simpler rule is correct again.
- **src/sim/player.rs fn bore_rect, the slide** - Anchoring the bore box flush against the gnome's rectangle, on the argument that a bore is at arm's length by construction and reach therefore never enters into it. A wall twelve cells away then got twelve cells of air cut at it and a hole reported that was never made — `Tool::Dig`'s own standing rule broken from the inside (a reach may bound *where* a verb lands and must never decide *whether* it happens). The box slides forward to the first face within `dig_reach`.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the cursor picks only a direction. If the bore ever takes a *point* from the cursor, the slide is that point instead.
- **src/sim/player.rs fn bore_rect, the slide's face predicate** - Siting the bore box at the first slab holding any non-empty non-organism cell. This is `face_toward`'s spoil-shielding failure in a second costume: the digger's own spoil sits between him and the rock, so the box is sited on the muck a cell in front of the face and **the bore grinds on its own spoil for ever**. Caught by `app::a_click_on_a_tree_shakes_it_rather_than_cutting_or_painting` on a *single* shaken-loose grain of sand, which sited a whole passage five cells short of the wall it was aimed at. `footing`'s `Hard | Scenery` sites it now — the same three-way split `face_toward` uses — with a fallback to anything at all.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for the same condition as `face_toward`'s entry above; the two rules must move together or the two dig styles aim differently.
- **src/sim/player.rs Player::swing_cooldown (doc)** - A per-tool recovery timer, one each for pick, hammer and axe. Two timers make the belt an exploit rather than a choice: strike, switch, strike again at the sum of both rates, with no cost to carrying all three. One timer charged to whichever tool struck. Guarded by `the_belt_shares_one_recovery_so_switching_is_not_a_second_swing`, watched failing for this fault.
  *Re-test when:* Never while switching tools is free. A belt with a switch *cost* could afford per-tool timers.
- **src/sim/player.rs Tuning::dig_yield (doc)** - Both extremes of dig yield are recorded rejections: at 1.0 nothing leaves the world and caves are impossible (shatter conserves cells, and inside a massif there is nowhere to shove spoil — the second playtest's 'the material breaks but goes nowhere'); at 0.0 rock simply vanishes and destruction stops producing debris, which the project already rejected once on exactly those grounds. Some fraction must leave and some must pile.
  *Re-test when:* The removed fraction is the natural hook if mined stone ever becomes something the player carries — re-derive the split then.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs struct Hinge and fn swing_on_hinge; making a severed crown sweep by imposing a shared rigid-body velocity on its fragments, rejected in play 2026-08-29** - Built to answer the owner's own words -- *"it should hinge at the trunk hit the ground and the bottom branches break off from the impact... not just unzip and fall to the ground"* -- and it does exactly that on a contact sheet: every fragment off one severance shares a `Hinge {pivot, alpha, omega}`, its velocity is `omega x r` about the stump while it holds one, the base lands first, and `landed` releases it on impact. It measured well (`log` 587 -> 772 cells with the leaf fix, more wood surviving than either arm before it). **It was rejected on sight, twice, and the reason is the mechanism rather than the tuning**: *"A still looks like everything breaking but because of an invisible force pushing things around, not because it fell and hit the ground"*, then *"you are trying to force a full motion and break up... It seems like you are trying to create an animation and not a physics system that results in realistic damage."* `Hinge` **imposes** a velocity field computed at the moment of the cut; nothing in the world causes it, so it produces the right silhouette by construction and no correct cause. The model wanted instead is causal: a severed trunk **detaches** rather than fragmenting at the cut, goes over because it is unstable, and the *impact* generates the stress that breaks limbs -- `Reports/tree-mechanics-plan-2026-08-29.md` sections 2-4 in their own order. **The condition:** this is rejected as a *substitute* for the stress model, not as arithmetic -- `angular_acceleration` and `cut_face` are sound and would be reusable if a real instability ever needs a pivot and a rate. Do not reinstate the imposed velocity; anything that draws the motion rather than deriving it will read the same way. Full account, with the numbers and both verdicts: `Reports/tree-fall-2026-08-29.md` section 9d.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn cut_face; hinging a severed crown about `load::Failure::at`, measured 2026-08-29** - The obvious pivot for "the tree swings on its stump" is the cell the support check failed, and it is not the stump. `Failure::at` is one arbitrary cell of the region: measured on `scene=fell` it is `(201, 170)`, the crown's far **left** edge, against a region spanning x 201..320 -- so the centre of mass sits **58.5 cells to the right of it and 2.0 below**, `r` is very nearly horizontal, and `omega x r` points almost straight down. The hinge fired correctly and produced a fall, and **nothing on the rendered sheet distinguished it from no hinge at all**; only `HINGE_PROBE=1`'s printed `r` did. Replaced by the region's own lowest row at its horizontal centre of mass, which gives `r = (58.5, -26.0)` and a horizontal sweep. The condition: this is the right rejection for a *trunk*, and the wrong one for a limb breaking off sideways, whose lowest row is its own drooping tip -- there `broke_at` is the better pivot, and telling the two apart wants the attachment face (region cells still touching standing tissue) rather than either.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn ChunkBody::centre_of; deriving the turn pivot from the body's current offsets on every turn, 2026-08-29** - The tidy version of "turn about the centre", and it walks. The pivot is the floored mean of the offsets, so a body whose true centre sits at a half-cell comes out of a turn with its floored centre a cell away, and the next turn pivots about *that*. Measured on a six-cell L: one clockwise turn followed by one anticlockwise left it a cell from where it started, and four clockwise turns never came home -- a position leak with no ledger that could catch it, since the cell count is conserved throughout. Compensating by shifting the offsets back and moving `x`/`y` the same amount does **not** fix it: the shift is world-neutral by construction, so it converts drift in offset space into drift in world space and the composition is still not the identity. The pivot is fixed at promotion instead, which stays the shape's centre because a quarter turn leaves the shape congruent. Caught by `the_two_turns_undo_each_other` before it shipped; the condition is that the pivot must be a whole cell for the turn to be exact on a grid at all.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn drag_through_liquid, the two spin lines; holding `spin_accel` through a submerged flight, 2026-08-29** - The fall's angular acceleration is held for the whole flight, which is right for a piece falling through air for a few dozen frames and wrong for one that never lands. A raft breaking up mid-pond wound itself to `MAX_SPIN_RATE` and **stayed on the surface**: 34 of 80 rock cells still standing at rows 84..100 after 600 frames, and identically after 3,000 -- so wedged, not slow, which is `an_unsupported_raft_sinks_through_a_deep_pond`'s own bug returning by a new route. The control that named it was forcing `spin_accel` to zero, one line, which restored the test at once. Rejected in favour of water taking the same share of the turn that it takes of the weight -- `drag_through_liquid`'s existing `carried` term, so no new constant. The condition: this only bites where a body stays in flight far longer than a fall through air, so a change that bounds the wind-up some other way (ending the hinge after the first turn was considered) would make it moot.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn topple doc; reinstating `load::bearing_moment` for `log` so the load model tips a standing piece, measured 2026-08-29** - The obvious repair for `open-bugs-handoff.md` §Q, and it does the opposite. Removing the `capacity != i64::MAX` guard so the bearing clamp reaches `log` and re-running the identical cut: settled lying/upright/square **3/8/2 -> 1/11/1**, `log` 833 cells -> 716, `deadwood` 553 -> 645. It **crushes** the pieces rather than laying them down and leaves *more* of the rest standing, because the load model's two verdicts are *holds* and *fails* and failing means `breaks_into` -- the pieces it condemned were the ones with a footing. The missing thing was never the test, it was an **outcome**; the middle-third test now lives in `rigid::topple`, on the far side of the flight, where the piece is still a body. The condition is the load model having no way to express "go over", so this stays rejected until it does.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn blocked_axis body, occupied-set comment** - Building the body's occupied-cell HashSet inside displace, per displaced cell, made one substep O(cells^2) in hashing plus a fresh allocation each time; fracture multiplies in-flight bodies roughly eightfold, so a collapse paid that cost a hundred times a frame — reported from play as 'when something big breaks into lots of little pieces, the performance gets bad'. Build the set once per call and lend it downward.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn fracture doc, section 'Why a distribution and not a single body'** - Promoting the whole region as one ChunkBody if large enough and converting every cell to rubble if not was rejected in playtest: 'everything either disintegrates into powder or breaks off as a large piece; there needs to be more rubble.' Both outcomes were individually plausible; the binary was the problem. Fragment sizes now come off a power-of-two ladder so the distribution falls out of the draw rather than being authored.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn fracture_failing_region doc ('No upper bound, deliberately') and fn strike body (same note)** - Gating breakage on MAX_BODY_CELLS — written twice in one session, in fracture and in strike — meant anything bigger than ~400 cells fell through to per-cell dust conversion (reported from play: a thick column cap where 'part breaks into chunks ... the other parts just crumble to dust') and a big strike loosened several hundred cells then threw nothing. The bigger the event, the less happened — exactly backwards. A size cap bounds a fragment, never whether a region breaks at all.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn fracture_failing_region doc, section 'Why this no longer decides which cells fail'** - label_failing_region's flood ('connected, unattached, distance above zero' — take the whole appendage) is superseded: it existed solely because the reach model's cantilever root never failed on its own, produced roughly the right outcome by the wrong mechanism, and degraded exactly where it mattered (a partially cracked overhang has no single distance-defined appendage). Reintroducing it beside a root that now fails on its own makes one failure detach twice — both had to change in the same commit.
  *Re-test when:* Held once load::failing_region made roots fail properly; only a return to a reach-style criterion would motivate it.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn fracture_shell body, annulus comment** - Scanning the whole disc instead of an annulus swept up the crater's interior — material the blast was still working through — and converted it before the front reached it, leaving later blast stages nothing to clear. The wall is what comes away, not the hole.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn fracture_shell doc** - Explosions that cleared their crater and spawned only single-cell debris particles were rejected for rock: right for sand, wrong for stone — a blast produced a clean hole and a spray of grit, never a piece. Owner: 'you explode a rock to make a cave and pieces should crack.' The freshly exposed shell is now fractured into thrown chunks.
  *Re-test when:* Single-cell debris remains the right treatment for sand; the rejection is specific to rock.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn fracture_with_impulse body, impulse-radius comment** - Sizing the collapse air impulse from the region's cell count left the far end of anything long and thin outside the impulse entirely — a 22-cell cantilever shoved no air at its own tip. The radius must be sized to reach the region's extent.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn mine doc, section 'This is a parameter and not a constant on purpose'** - A blanket vanish of the bite hardcoded into mine was measured and reverted: it breaks player::at_full_yield_nothing_leaves_the_world, whose contract — at yield 1.0 a dig may move material but never delete it — is the promise keeping the eraser from creeping back in through the mining verb. Vanishing is spoil_yield = 0.0, a setting, not a rule.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn mine_swept doc, section 'Why a bore has to be swept and not stamped'** - Driving a tunnel as a row of stamped discs was shipped and failed on sight and in function: the bore's usable height is the scallop between discs, not the disc — the pinch between two radius-7 bites measured 13 cells against the gnome's height of 14, wedging him at the mouth of his own bore; the owner: 'why are you digging tunnels a row of circles instead of a tunnel.' Bites are swept as capsules from the previous bite, giving constant 2r+1 height.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn score_cracks body, scored_now comment** - Counting cracks made by the current call toward the tip bonus let every strike max out its own bonus immediately — rays from one blow cross each other near the origin — so reach was identical after one blow and after two, leaving nothing for the next blow to build on. Only pre-existing damage may earn a ray extra reach.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn score_cracks body, site-keyed jitter comment (also recorded at structural.rs crush_in_place)** - Jittering crack-ray directions per call sent every hit off in new directions, so a second blow on the same spot scored fresh fissures beside the old ones instead of driving them deeper — the crack-tip bonus could almost never find anything to extend, so damage repeated instead of accumulating. Directions are keyed on the site via rng::jitter (a position hash), which also keeps blows out of the replay RNG draw order.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn score_cracks doc, edge-orientation paragraph (the same trap recorded at structural.rs crush_in_place)** - The edge a crack cuts must be the one perpendicular to its direction of travel, so the crack separates rock along the ray. Get it backwards and the cracks run across the ray, cutting the rock into rings instead of wedges — both crack writers record this as a trap.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn strike body, MIN_STRIKE_RADIUS comment** - Scaling the blow entirely off the brush radius with no floor — a deliberate choice ('the tool the player is already sizing decides how hard they hit') — was wrong at the low end: at radius 2 the arithmetic is correct and the result invisible on a cliff face, reported from play as 'striking a cliff does nothing'. Drawing and hitting want different sizes; the brush still scales the swing, from a floor that always lands.
  *Re-test when:* The brush-scaling half is still right at the top end (a wide brush should calve slabs); only the unfloored bottom was rejected.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn thin_to_spoil doc, section 'Why an even stride and not a random subset'** - Thinning spoil by random subset was rejected: it clumps, so a bore comes out with lumps of surviving rubble in places and clean holes in others (reads as the cut having missed), and it consumes world.rng draws, so a replayed input sequence diverges — determinism is required. The Bresenham-style even stride does neither.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs fn thin_to_spoil doc, section 'Why the removed cells are not thrown as particles'** - Throwing the removed bite as ParticleSystem particles was tried first, for exactly the right reason (material blinking out is the rejected 'no debris, no consequence' failure), and it does not work: particles land and write themselves back into the grid, quietly undoing the removal — measured on scene=tunnel, 941 cells dusted and only 119 actually gone from the world. The debris requirement is met by the kept spoil fraction plus the pressure impulse.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while ParticleSystem particles write back into the grid on landing.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs struct ChunkBody, spin field doc** - Free rotation for chunk bodies is rejected: a cell grid cannot represent a slab at 37 degrees without resampling, and resampling a rotating body is where the classic re-rasterization leaks and holes come from (PLAN.md records the pitfall and the inverse-mapping fix). Quarter turns are exact — offsets map to offsets with no interpolation — so a tumbling chunk can never gain or lose a cell.
  *Re-test when:* Would need a resampling scheme proven leak-free before revisiting.
- **src/sim/rigid.rs test a_pinch_point_terminates_rather_than_hanging (fn trace_contours)** - Terminating trace_contours' inner walk on 'n == start' alone hangs on two cells touching only at a corner: the start-point collision at the pinch silently drops one lobe's exit edge and reroutes the walk into the other, already-closed lobe's cycle, which never revisits the dropped lobe's start — unbounded ring growth. Found by independent review; do not simplify the termination guard back to the single check.
  *Re-test when:* Inherent to pinch-point inputs, which the module doc already documents as producing (bounded, wrong) geometry.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn crush_in_place body, comment 'Confining it to region was tried first'** - Confining fissure walkers to the failed region was tried first and was very nearly a no-op that only a counter could show: 189 confined failures wrote 29 cells of fissure between them, producing bit-identical images across two complete rewrites of the crack pattern — an overload's region is a sparse union of subtrees that the walker leaves on its first step. Fissures must run through rock and stop at free faces.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn crush_in_place body, single-origin comment** - Scattering several crack origins through the piece, each throwing straight spokes, was rejected by the owner: 'it doesn't really look like a spreading crack from a boulder, it just looks like criss cross irregular lines.' Everything must descend from one origin — a few primaries like a windscreen star, every other stroke a fork — so it reads as a trunk with branches, not scribble.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn crush_in_place body, sub-cell path comment** - Stepping crack walkers to whichever axis neighbour the heading pointed most nearly at was tried and produces a staircase: the heading turns slowly, so its dominant axis persists for many cells and the crack comes out as long horizontal/vertical runs meeting at right angles — the 'criss cross' look. No amount of extra wander fixes it, because the quantisation causes it; the path must be carried at sub-cell precision and rounded only to pick the cell.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn crush_in_place doc, paragraph on rigid::score_cracks** - Reusing rigid::score_cracks for confined crushes was evaluated and rejected even though it is the same idea: it unbraces the rock either side of every fissure (telling the load model the inside of a mountain has come free) and schedules structural checks at each (turning a crush into a treadmill). Both behaviours are right for a blow and wrong here.
- **src/sim/structural.rs fn crush_in_place doc, section 'Fissures, not graph paper'** - Cracking confined failures on a fixed world-coordinate lattice (every sixth column and row, with keying ligaments) was built and looked absurd: preset=rolling seed=1 strike=20 drew a perfectly regular 6x6 crosshatch reading as wireframe mesh over the rock. No metric said so; one contact sheet did. Cracks must radiate from the failed cell.

## plants  (155 entries)

- **`src/sim/rng.rs` `stream` doc (the "both research reports name the wrong culprit" note); `src/sim/plant.rs` `seed_genotype` doc** - **Re-keying the plant genotype off position and onto `organism_id`, to remove the "position is a hidden inherited variable" confound, is a fix aimed at a mechanism that does not exist and is half-shipped already.** Plants never touch `Chunk::rng` — it is reached only by the CA sweep through `CellSurface::rng()`, and `plant.rs`'s single `world.rng` use is one litter shade at `:5775`. The real coupling was *draw order*, and the per-organism stream that fixes it **is live**: `rng::stream(organism_id, x, y, frame)` (`plant.rs:1388`). Position keying of the *founding* draw is deliberate and defended in source: it survives planting order, worldgen edits, slot reuse (a 4-bit generation wrapping at 16) and save/load, none of which an id-keyed draw does — and an id-keyed genotype makes a plant's character a property of the world's whole event history, so planting one extra sapling earlier re-rolls every plant after it. **Five reports carried the wrong mechanism** (`plant-simulation-research.md` §7d, `population-dynamics-research.md` §7d, `ecological-lod-design.md`, `plant-evolvability-facts-2026-08-27.md` §6, `plant-morphology-evolvability-2026-08-26.md` §7 gate 4); `rng.rs` had named the first two as wrong for some time and the correction never reached them, so the claim propagated into two later documents and into a session plan.
  *Re-test when:* The residual confound is real but **binds founders only**, because `seed_genotype` returns early on `inherited` — a lineage's genetics flow parent to child and position never re-enters (`plant-evolution-design.md` §1c). What reopens it is a *measurement*, not an argument: the founder-versus-descendant fraction over a long run. If founders are a large share of a radiation run's population, a favourable cell re-sown repeatedly draws the same founding genotype and can read as "this allele wins here". Note that `FOUNDER_VARIANT_CHANCE` (2026-08-28) widened that surface from two loci to six, since all six discrete loci are now positional at founding.

- **`src/sim/decay.rs` `tick`, the "Left dry, deliberately" comment (first paragraph)** - Copying a neighbouring soil cell's moisture into the soil cell a decayed litter or ash cell becomes was built and reverted: it **duplicates** water rather than moving it, because the donor keeps its own, so every litter cell that rots adds a cell's worth of soil water from nothing. That closes a pump the plant economy then runs: tree sheds litter, litter rots into damp soil, tree drinks it, grows, sheds more. Decayed soil ships **dry** instead, which is also the more honest physics -- soil formed by weathering is not wet *because* it weathered -- and `update_soil_water`'s capillary term wets it **conservatively** over the following visits. The visible cost is that a seed reseeded onto brand-new soil may wait before germinating, which is the model working, not a defect.
  *Re-test when:* Rejected as a **copy**, not as an idea. A true transfer that debits the donor by exactly what the new cell gains is conserved and is *not* covered by this rejection; any version that leaves the donor whole is the same dead end whatever cell it reads from. The rejection also leans on capillary flow being able to reach the new cell, so if `SOIL_FIELD_CAPACITY` (620) or `SOIL_CAPILLARY_REST` (380) ever move far enough that a dry cell beside damp ground no longer fills, the fallback is gone and the question reopens.
- **`src/sim/decay.rs` `tick`, the "Left dry, deliberately" comment ("Deriving it from the humidity" paragraph)** - Deriving the new soil cell's moisture from the **ambient field humidity** that licensed the decay is worse than copying a neighbour's, and went the same way. The field moisture channel is deliberately *outside* the water ledger -- `field::apply_moisture_sources` forces it up from a source level every step and debits nothing -- so reading it into `Powder` aux converts an unconserved air channel into ledger-visible soil water. Same pump as the copy variant, and this is the one that was measured: the standing-biomass probe went from **1,718 cells to 2,652 and still climbing**.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the field moisture channel is unconserved and force-written from sources; only a humidity channel that is itself inside the water ledger would change the argument. **Two things must be rebuilt before a retry is even measurable.** The instrument is gone -- `a_tree_eventually_stops_growing` was retired 2026-08-22 -- so this needs a fresh *paired* standing-biomass comparison rather than the old two-instants bar. And the sibling entry's own "capillary will wet it" fallback now has company: the worldgen climate baseline puts every soil cell the generator places above the damp gate, so a retry starts from wetter ground than the version measured here and would pump *harder*, not softer. Note the distinction that cost time to establish -- **soil** reads damp almost everywhere, but the **litter lying on it** mostly does not, because the field is sampled at the litter's own block, which is air: measured at frame 10,800, only 2-7% of standing litter sits above the gate, while 66-76% of decay *events* are damp ones because the damp chance is 25x the dry one.

- **`assets/species/tree.ron` RootTip `Grow` (`branch_chance: [0.0]`), `OrganismCell::primed`, `Reports/plant-genome-design.md` §8a** - A root tip's *in-tick* branch roll: root branching was a second `Grow` in the same tick as the primary step, so the tip had to hold two steps' carbon at once. Measured, it cleared that bar **twice in twelve thousand frames** and the 0.04 roll fired **zero** times. The economy was not the defect — a root tip cannot photosynthesise, lives on allocation and spends at first affordance, which is intended — the *shape of the purchase* was. Replaced by `branch_priming`: the tip marks a site for free as it passes and the site buys its own lateral later. `branch_chance` left at `0.0` rather than its old value so nothing reads as a live knob that no longer runs.
  *Re-test when:* Only if root tips gain an income that can fund two steps in one tick; the zero here is the "vacuous, not wrong" shape `CLAUDE.md` warns about — the gate was unreachable, so the roll was never really tested.
- **`assets/species/tree.ron` `branch_priming` comment (swept 1/2/3/6/12/24)** - `branch_priming: [1]` was swept and rejected: at 1 it stops being an oscillator and primes *every* cell, and the walk then re-scans **82,487 primed cells over a run against 1,160 at 3**, for a stand-scale cost the mechanism does not need. Root mass falls monotonically as the interval widens (755 cells at 1, 336 at 6). Landed at 3, which also puts genotype slot 1 on the responsive part of the curve.
  *Re-test when:* Only alongside a cheaper primed-site walk; the rejection is about scan cost, not about root mass.
- **`src/sim/plant.rs` `render_step` / `Behavior::Grow::stem_stiffness`** - **Sharpening the direction sample to straighten a stem was tried and measured *worse*.** The obvious repair for a wobbly shoot is to make the straight-ahead neighbour likelier — raise `continuation_weight`, or raise every candidate score to a power. Modelled on the order-0 trunk's own authored weights, exponent 4 moved lateral drift over a 145-cell trunk from 32 cells to **43** at `heading_inertia: 0.0` and from 50 to **74** at `0.5`. The mechanism is that sharpening makes the walk more *persistent*, and a persistent random walk is super-diffusive: it commits harder to whichever way it already erred. No exponent exists that fixes this, because the defect is not the width of the distribution — it is that consecutive steps are drawn independently, so their errors add instead of cancelling. *Re-test when:* never on this argument. What is left to try is a different way of turning a continuous heading into lattice steps, which is what `stem_stiffness` does.
- **`src/sim/plant.rs` `render_step`** - **Perpendicular error *feedback* — pushing a strayed tip back toward its axis by biasing the candidate scores — was tried and fixes the wrong half.** It does bound the drift (13.7 cells to 4.0 over a 145-cell trunk), and it makes the *visible* jitter worse: mean straight run 1.50 cells to **1.26**, direction changes 66.5% of steps to **79.0%**, because a correction applied after every departure is itself a zigzag. Rejected in favour of error *diffusion*, which decides the step from the accumulated debt rather than penalising it after the fact and measures 0.41 RMS departure from the local best-fit line against the feedback form's 0.65 and the baseline's 0.88. *Re-test when:* the quantity being corrected is a whole-plant one (a lean, a centre of mass) rather than a per-step one — feedback is the right shape for a slow variable and the wrong shape for a per-cell one.
- **`src/sim/plant.rs` `render_step`, and the metric that nearly killed it** - **"Mean straight run length" is not a smoothness metric and reading it as one rejected the correct fix.** A perfectly drawn line at 17 degrees is a *staircase* — it has to be, on eight neighbours — so it scores ~1.4 cells of mean straight run, indistinguishable from random jitter, and the first pass at this work read error diffusion's 1.19 against the baseline's 1.50 as a regression. What the eye separates is *regularity*, not run length: RMS departure from the local best-fit line scores the baseline at 0.88 and the drawn heading at 0.41, against ~0.3 for a perfect line. This is `CLAUDE.md`'s "ask what your number counts when nothing is wrong" with the control being *a known-good artifact* rather than a known-good world. *Re-test when:* never — but the trap generalises to any lattice-rendering question, where the discrete path is supposed to be a staircase.
- **`src/sim/plant.rs` `plagiotropic_here`, `Reports/plant-evolution-design.md` §4a register** - Order-0 plagiotropy (dropping the `order > 0` guard so a plant's first-order shoot could also run plagiotropic) was tried and reverted, back to `order > 0 && alleles[LOCUS_TROPISM] != 0`. A knob whose only justification is a form class that pure data already reaches is not paying for itself.
  *Re-test when:* **Do not re-derive it from the same argument.** What would change the answer is evidence that order-0 plagiotropy produces something `heading_inertia` and a low turgor budget cannot — and that is a sheet, not a syllogism.
- **`src/sim/organism.rs` EMBEDDED species list, `Reports/plant-evolution-design.md` §4a register** - Two authored probe species were shipped and then **retired against the owner's verdicts**: `weeping.ron` ("same plant" as `tree`) and `prostrate.ron` ("Not that different" from `creeper`, 2/5). Both files were deleted rather than left embedded — a condemned probe does not outlive its verdict here. `creeper.ron` survived the same round and stayed.
  *Re-test when:* A form probe is worth re-authoring only against a silhouette lever that texture and colour actually move (`Reports/plant-appearance-design.md`); relabelling which cell is which will reproduce the same verdict.

- **PLAN.md 'AUDIT: what the scene discovery invalidates, and what survives' and 'The environment is now fixed'** - Every plant judgement made in 20- or 40-row-sky scenes is invalid: trees reach 41-47 rows, press the ceiling and can only spread sideways (widest row 56 cells at 40 rows of sky vs 7 at 70), and LIGHT_DECAY=0.997 made light attenuate through empty air so headroom cost illumination (median height 21→65 and biomass 1,271→15,362 from the decay fix alone). LIGHT_DECAY moved to 0.9997 and the 96-row grove scene exists for tree judgement. The audit's own first conclusion — 'the slab is substantially a ceiling artifact' — was then itself found wrong: with light and headroom fixed the mass is real; the ceiling was a confound hiding it by starving growth.
  *Re-test when:* forest and default plant_probe (40-row) scenes remain valid for root and soil work only; never re-tune canopy behaviour against them.
- **PLAN.md 'Bud break — built, measured, and reverted. Read this before rebuilding it.'** - BudBreak as designed (§2e: resource_threshold + crowding_threshold + per-cell chance on MatureBody) was built, measured, and reverted — three claims falsified: surplus resource carries no information (transport fills every cell to RESOURCE_SCALE, so threshold 0.75→0.99 moved the ensemble only 17,181→17,365); 'self-limiting without a cap' is false because when a tree stops growing every local signal (crowding, conductance) equalizes at once, so per-cell chance makes budding proportional to volume and the tree fills into a solid mound; and a one-roll-per-organism rate cap only converts exponential to linear filling (lumpy at frame 12,500 instead of 3,000). The size ceiling itself is real (0 active sites by frame 16,000 without budding).
  *Re-test when:* A future attempt needs a real bound on absolute size, which the engine currently has nowhere. Two untried candidates recorded: growth cost rising with distance from roots (hydraulic limit), or budding triggered by disturbance rather than idleness (self-limiting by construction, and the more satisfying verb).
- **PLAN.md bud-break section, claim 3 (shoot allometry)** - MAX_SHOOT_FRACTION (the mirror of MAX_ROOT_FRACTION) does not bound size and was reverted: a ratio bound lets roots and shoot both grow without limit while staying in band. Measured with budding off it cost ~13% biomass (7,484→6,529 at frame 24,000) and changed nothing about saturation — a tax with no benefit.
  *Re-test when:* Only worth revisiting if something else is producing frontier that a ratio could meaningfully constrain.
- **PLAN.md bud-break section, 'Lowering pipe_ratio ...' paragraph** - pipe_ratio 10.0 → 6.0 was measured and reverted: it restores the rows>1-cell-wide metric to baseline (35%→43%) by making the basal pancake worse — biomass 5,806→13,795 with a slab spreading the full width of the ground. The real fix is thicken()'s geometry (grow around the stem, fix width on diagonal stems), not this knob.
- **PLAN.md bud-break section, 'one constant the migration invalidated' paragraph** - 4-bit quantized canopy density cannot decay: with a halving decay, 0.267*0.5 rounds straight back to 0.267, giving a permanent one-quantum floor on every cell ever deposited — the mechanism meant to let later growth reclaim space could never release it. As f32 it reaches zero. Its earlier decay constant had been tuned around the quantization half-step.
  *Re-test when:* Any re-quantization of a decaying per-cell scalar must check the decay step against the quantum.
- **PLAN.md bud-break section, 'rows >1 cell wide mostly measures the basal pancake' and the eight-variant A/B table** - 'rows >1 cell wide' was abandoned as a tuning target after eight A/B runs chased a 42→35 'regression' that was the metric, not the trees — it is dominated by the basal slab. All eight candidate causes (re-quantizations, per-frame transport, old duty cycle, reversed iteration, dirtying writes, Gauss-Seidel instead of Jacobi, combinations) landed at 35-37 and none reached 42; the combination was worse than Gauss-Seidel alone, so they interact. Judge on stem thickness above the base, establishment rate, and the picture.
- **PLAN.md 'Phase 2 landed' bullets ('Roots grow into soil')** - Using the existing density field as penetration resistance (the design doc's cheaper suggestion) was rejected: conflating them breaks wet sand and pumice. A real per-material penetration_resistance (MPa) field against a per-species penetration_force was added, calibrated to the 2-3 MPa bound where real root elongation stops.
- **PLAN.md 'Plant substrate v2 — started...', 'Decision 2 step 2c landed — and it was a prerequisite for polarity' paragraph** - Building polarity on the packed 8-bit fixed-point resource in aux is arithmetically impossible, not merely inelegant: the §7h Y-junction gate turns on a share difference of 0.0048 while one quantum of the packed representation is 0.0157 — the gate resolves 0.31 of a step — and per-substep rounding exceeds the per-substep signal in both directions, so exact conservation is unreachable. Trap recorded: the chain canalization test still passes under packing (flux saturates the Hill function), so a green guard suite hides an unmeasurable mechanism.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any fixed-point resource representation coarser than the gate's signal; the f32 sidecar (OrganismCell) is the fix.
- **PLAN.md 'Polarity (Decision 6) — landed'** - Two deviations from the design doc, both measured: canopy density cannot run through the general pairwise transport form and stay bit-for-bit with the symmetric average (the mean and pairwise rules differ by neighbour count) — density keeps its own tested rule; and the §7h Y-junction test cannot pin stem hunger at 1.0 while capping delivery at Q=0.3 in a running sim — pinning drives flux to ~19x J_REF, saturates the Hill function on both faces and collapses the conductance ratio to 1.00. The test asserts the claim supply-limited instead (1.26 vs the illustration's 1.38).
- **PLAN.md Progress log, tree wind-lean entry (independent review notes)** - Scaling the growth-time wind lean by raw field velocity magnitude was rejected: field.rs clamps pressure but never velocity, so a nearby explosion's shockwave (several times the combined weight of every other input) dominated tip growth direction. The lean is direction-only at fixed WIND_LEAN_MAGNITUDE, gated by WIND_SPEED_THRESHOLD. Separately, a one-shot add_pressure_impulse as test 'wind' produces a decaying sign-flipping oscillation (vx crossed negative by step 27) that passes only in lucky windows — tests must use continuous per-step forcing.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while field.rs leaves velocity unclamped; a velocity clamp would allow revisiting magnitude scaling.
- **PLAN.md 'Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md — done, not started (session handoff)', decision 1** - The sub-cell turgor/extension growth scalar (the project's own preliminary lean) was rejected in the v2 design: the resource scalar already is that accumulator, and the liquid-fill trick's load-bearing property — conserved, transferable fill — belongs to soil moisture, not growth rate. Accretion plus one-cell root displacement was accepted instead. Also recorded there: the tiny-tree problem's real cause was thicken()'s counting bug, not the growth-mode question at all.
- **PLAN.md same design entry, decision 3 (soil moisture)** - Modelling waterlogging as reduced absorb efficiency was rejected as backwards, and as slowed growth rejected as indistinguishable from drought — the literature-grounded mechanism is necrosis of RootTip specifically on a duration gate (O2 collapse kills tips within 1-2 hours while mature tissue survives, Evans 2004).
  *Re-test when:* Grounded in cited waterlogging physiology; retry only against new evidence.
- **PLAN.md same design entry, 'Decision 6, now written in full' (§7b)** - A packed direction-only encoding for polarity was rejected: conductance itself, not just direction, is what has to update under the Hill-function ratcheting rule. Four per-face f32 carbon_conductance scalars are required.
- **PLAN.md same design entry, 'Revised after landing' paragraph** - Deferring polarity to its own future pass (the design doc's first draft) was reversed: sidecar storage restructures the exact code polarity must change, and tuning the resource economy once against isotropic diffusion and again after polarity is the double-tuning waste the owner flagged. Polarity moved between retrofit steps 1 and 2, before the leaf/reserve re-tune.
  *Re-test when:* Sequencing knowledge: never tune constants ahead of a scheduled mechanism change that redefines what they mean.
- **PLAN.md 'The economy pass — three things landed', item 1, plus the 'Single-run tuning is unsound here' finding and the plant_probe metric caveat** - Tuning plant knobs at n=1 is invalid: debug_tree_variants' n=1 read 'highrate is 3x baseline', but at n=8 that variant spans 15-488 and every variant's range overlaps every other — any decision from the old harness would have been wrong. Attendant rules found the same way: outcomes are bimodal (13-21 or 100-500 cells, nothing between) so report establishment rate, never a mean; replicates must be different planting positions (rng::stream is position-seeded, so re-running one scene draws identical numbers); growth converges by ~4,000 frames so 20,000-frame runs waste 3x the time; and plant_probe plants on the ground so it was structurally blind to a total falling-seed growth failure (-1% biomass reported while filmstrip's forest scene was dead) — always shoot a picture too.
- **PLAN.md 'The economy pass', item 2 (canalization contrast)** - Canalization contrast 30:1 was rejected by measurement (54/96 established, 4,906 cells) in favour of 10:1 (70/96, 6,321) — the coupling is the mechanism, since lowering contrast raises the permitted TRANSPORT_RATE and unpolarized tissue conducts at RATE*CONDUCTANCE_MIN. 5:1 was also tried and rejected.
  *Re-test when:* All numbers were measured in the 20-row-sky scene later found invalid; the direction may hold but the values must be re-derived in the grove scene before reuse.
- **PLAN.md 'Three bugs the work exposed rather than caused' (plant Phase 2) and CLAUDE.md's matching thicken() note** - reachable_from_anchors traversed 4 neighbours while Grow places children at 8, so any diagonally-grown tree read back as disconnected fragments and thicken() counted a fragment of the canopy. Fixing the traversal changed what the count means, and pipe_ratio had to be recalibrated 2.5→10.0 because its input grew by an order of magnitude — re-deriving dependent constants is part of such a fix.
  *Re-test when:* A traversal must always use the writer's neighbourhood; transport's 4-connectivity is the one deliberate exception (face-sharing exchanges).
- **README.md 'M16 status' — 'A real bug in root growth' paragraph** - Letting a root advance only into Empty or Powder and treating everything else — including water — as blocked killed the tip at the edge of any wet ground without ever drinking, which is backwards: water is what root growth is for. Liquid targets need their own case: absorbed on the spot, tip advances into the emptied cell, no wood left behind.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent for the root-advance rule.
- **README.md 'M16 status' — 'A real bug, caught by a test that expected growth and got almost none' paragraph** - Moss growth requiring a candidate cell to have a solid neighbour specifically: once a cell's only non-empty neighbour was already-grown moss, every growth front dead-ended one step after starting, leaving a frozen single-cell line instead of a spreading patch. Existing moss must count as a growable surface (real moss thickens over its own earlier growth).
  *Re-test when:* Permanent; guarded by the damp-vs-dry moss regression tests.
- **README.md 'M16 status' — independent-review list, 'Tree tips tunnelled through wood' item** - Checking a growth target against the shared wood MaterialId ('is this any wood') instead of 'my own wood': a tip could pass through another tree's trunk or curve back into its own, spending energy on steps that created nothing. Fixed by blocking growth on any non-empty cell, full stop.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while wood is one shared MaterialId across all trees.
- **README.md 'M16 status' — test-coverage-gaps item, channel-decay tuning bug** - Decaying a tip's auxin channel on every tick spent waiting for energy, not just genuine dead ends: energy waits happened 2-3x more often than successful growth at the original constants, so channel could practically never reach the branch threshold and trees essentially never branched. Decay only on a true dead end (no attractors in reach).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while energy waits outnumber growth steps; re-derive if the energy economy changes those odds.
- **README.md 'The coarse field grid' — wind-lean paragraph ('Two of the plan's lower priority extras are done too')** - Scaling the tree-growth wind term by raw field velocity magnitude: field.rs clamps pressure but never velocity, so a nearby explosion's shockwave dominated the growth formula outright. Replaced with a fixed-magnitude, direction-only wind term. The original test was also wrong: a one-shot impulse produces a decaying oscillation, not a steady breeze.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while field velocity is unclamped; a magnitude-scaled wind read would need velocity clamping first.
- **Reports/design-philosophy.md §3 'Chosen over full biophysical simulation'** - A literal plant model — real auxin-transport PDEs, distinct xylem/phloem systems, hormone signaling — was considered and rejected, explicitly so it does not get reinvented: real hormone timescales get compressed past relevance in a game, the fidelity must then be fought for playability, and it is not visually distinguishable from the cell-typed tier at pixel-art resolution. Model the signals (light, gravity, wind, water), not the biochemistry carrying them.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional at pixel-art resolution and game timescales.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md 'CLOSED — the three the polarity review raised' (root retirement)** - Fixing the allometry-gate root retirement by marking the 'not now' gates as found_candidate (the review's suggested frame) breaks a_tree_eventually_stops_growing immediately — the staleness counter is the only thing that makes growth terminate. The real defect was that ageing out had no landing site for RootTip.
  *Re-test when:* Held while staleness is the sole growth-termination mechanism; if termination is re-based on something else, the gate marking could be revisited.
- **Reports/organism-substrate-design.md §1 Species as data, parallel to materials** - Folding species into MaterialRegistry was rejected: a material answers a question every cell asks on the CA sweep's hot path (millions of lookups per frame), and species fields would ride along meaninglessly for sand, water and stone. SpeciesRegistry is parallel, looked up only for cells with nonzero organism_id.
  *Re-test when:* Unknown — recorded as settled from the commissioning planning session.
- **Reports/organism-substrate-design.md §3 Transport: explicit per-cell diffusion** - Using the coarse field grid for organism resource transport was rejected: a FIELD_SCALE=8 block would smear a one-cell branch's resource level into seven unrelated neighbouring cells — the same resolution mismatch found independently by emergent-world-architecture.md §6 and the explosion-debris analysis. Transport is per-cell diffuse_resource between same-organism 4-neighbours, fire.rs's diffuse_heat shape.
  *Re-test when:* Holds at FIELD_SCALE=8; note the 4-neighbour choice is the deliberate exception to the 8-neighbour traversal rule (exchange crosses a shared face).
- **Reports/organism-substrate-design.md opening ('Why now, not at M16/M18 time')** - Private per-organism structs as the organism model are a superseded pattern: TreeState and CreatureState each solved 'per-organism state too big for a Cell' privately and structurally unrelated; extending CreatureState again would have been the third private solution. The organism substrate replaces both; creature-direction.md §3a orders it retired, not extended, and design-philosophy.md §3 records the deep-sim pattern as why trees read as scripted.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional going forward — any new per-organism state goes in OrganismState on the shared substrate.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §2 'Every global bound becomes a visible artifact'** - Hard, stand-uniform bounds were written three times by different doors (world ceiling, turgor ceiling, hard cutoff at the turgor line) and every one produced the same artifact: a straight edge in the render — trees pinned at one row, crowns fused into a slab, or flat horizontal plates.
  *Re-test when:* Any new bound must be graded (turgor_taper: extension ∝ P − Y, per Lockhart, turned plates into rounded crowns) and per-individual (genotype_variance spreads arrival times). The failure signature to watch for is always a straight edge.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §3 'A stand is not a plant, and a light-optimal stand is a slab' (measured in tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md Phase 2 status)** - Fighting canopy fusion by raising upward bias or adding branching along the trunk made the slab worse (fused runs 120, 118, 71) by pushing more growth into the ceiling. Only lateral crowding opposes the slab, and it must be strong: crowding_weight 0.5 → 6.0 took the thickest fused run from 136 to 39 cells at 57-cell spacing. Nothing else tried has worked.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while income is per-column sky visibility (grows with lit width) and a height bound exists — at any ceiling the light-optimal stand shape is a one-cell slab, so the light model rewards the artifact.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §4 (branch_chance variance narrowing)** - branch_chance jitter at ±60% starved seedlings: a seedling is order 0 and sees only the trunk tier, and branching is how a small plant builds leaf area, so wide low draws gave 35% establishment failure. Narrowing to ±35% took it to six trees in eight.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while there is no juvenile/adult axis — a species whose juvenile form differs from its adult form needs an age axis that does not exist yet.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §4 'Structure comes from tiers, not from tuning'** - Having a rule decide which cells are 'trunk' from local shape: a cell cannot know this locally, and four successive support models failed trying to infer held-up-ness from shape. Tiering comes from data — ByOrder<T> per-branch-order parameter lists — and a bare bole falls out of plastochron: [12,5,2,2] with no trunk-detection rule at all.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional: when a rule must tell apart things that look identical, state the difference as data (branch order is position, not age).
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §5 'Extension makes structure; thickening only makes mass'** - Measuring pipe-model cross-section from a cell's immediate neighbours: a run's growing end always reads 2, so thickening spreads without limit. Rejected alongside the row total; the denominator must be this stem's own contiguous run of woody cells, and leaves must never count as cross-section.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for pipe-model gating in 2D.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §6 'Nothing creates frontier except the bud bank' (bud siting paragraph)** - Flushing the brightest bud builds a flat cap, because the brightest bud is always on top of whatever the plant has already built. Siting was changed to light per unit crowding, which moves flushes to buds with room.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while light is per-column sky visibility (top cells always brightest).
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §7 — global sweeps** - One-knob-at-a-time global sweeps: every one tried on the branch came back monotone, meaning no interior optimum exists for such a search to find, and the method cannot see interactions. Sample the population instead — one run of N plants is a response curve over N genomes including interactions.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for the current parameter surface; a knob that develops an interior optimum would be visible in the population sampling anyway.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §7 — jitter width** - A flat ±15% genotype jitter width left three of four traits measuring |r| ≤ 0.05 — not inert, under-sampled — and integer quantization made ±15% on a plastochron base of 2 round back to 2 across the entire range, killing that dimension exactly where the crown is built.
  *Re-test when:* Set jitter width per trait from what it moves, and check integer-valued traits actually vary across the drawn range.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §7 — pattern-edit trap** - A blind sed on a field name in tree.ron varied more than its knob: the file holds two crowding_weight lines (shoot, and the root's deliberate 0.0), so a 'shoot crowding' sweep silently varied root behaviour at every point and had to be discarded.
  *Re-test when:* Prove the edit touched only its target (count matches, or edit by occurrence index) before trusting anything downstream.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §7 — 'Salts are positional'** - Retiring a dead genotype trait by removing its slot silently renumbers every genome ever measured (and re-keying genotypes has already broken a termination test). Retire a trait by setting its width to 0.0, never by deleting the slot.
  *Re-test when:* Holds as long as genotype draws are keyed positionally.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §7 — single-plant assertions** - Unit tests that plant one plant and assert on what it grew: twelve individuals from one genome span 31–153 cells, and three separate tests built this way all broke the moment genotypes varied. One plant is one draw from a wide distribution, not a result.
  *Re-test when:* Assert on populations or paired comparisons; this became binding as soon as per-individual genotype jitter existed.
- **Reports/plant-species-authoring.md §7 'Measurement discipline specific to plants' — include_str! trap** - Sweeping a species parameter by editing the .ron and re-running a prebuilt harness sweeps nothing: species files are compiled in via include_str!, and three bit-identical 'runs' at three shade_death values produced an entire invalid sweep before anyone noticed.
  *Re-test when:* Rebuild between sweep points; identical output across settings is the tell. Only the app's F5 reload reads the directory.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §2c (the displaced-soil simplification, named)** - Relocating soil displaced by root growth to the nearest empty cell (instead of deleting it) was evaluated and not adopted: it makes root growth a two-cell write with its own failure mode, and roots are the least live-verified part of the system.
  *Re-test when:* Explicit revisit condition: if soil visibly disappearing under a mature tree reads badly in play.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §2d '(iii) A sub-cell turgor/extension scalar — rejected'** - A sub-cell turgor/extension accumulator (PLAN.md's lean, reusing the liquid-fill trick) was rejected: the resource scalar already is that accumulator (Grow's resource < cost gate integrates over ticks), so a turgor scalar is a second accumulator of the same thing with two gates that can disagree; and the liquid-fill analogy fails because extension is neither conserved nor transferred, which is the trick's load-bearing property.
  *Re-test when:* The fill trick was redirected to soil moisture (conserved, transferable, drains, fits Powder aux) — the right tool pointed at the right problem. Re-open only if extension ever becomes a conserved, transferable quantity.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §2e 'The fourth element PLAN.md's lean omits: bud break'** - Fixing 'tiny tree, growth stops for good' by growth-mode changes plus economy retuning alone: PLAN.md's own diagnosis records that the absence of any frontier-creating mechanism is the actual ceiling on total size, 'not the resource economy just retuned' — the lean implemented in full would have left the headline symptom exactly where it was.
  *Re-test when:* Any plan for plant size must include a frontier-creation mechanism; economy work alone is established as insufficient.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §3a 'The current layout, verified against code' (removal noted in tree-architecture-variety-review-verification.md §3.2)** - Packing canopy density into 4 bits of Cell::aux forced tuning a behaviour constant around storage: decay rates smaller than the quantization half-step (≈0.133) got quantization-locked, so CANOPY_DENSITY_DECAY_PER_TICK had to be raised to 0.5 for the mechanism to work at all — the tail wagging the dog.
  *Re-test when:* The 4-bit packing was removed by the sidecar migration, so the 0.5 setting's rationale is gone — the decay constant is re-derivable once storage is a plain float.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §3b 'Is CreatureState the precedent? Partly — and it is not the one to copy'** - Copying CreatureState's side-storage pattern for organism cell data: evaluated and rejected — it is per-entity not per-cell, and has no generational check, so a stale index silently reads another creature's state (a bug shape world.rs already fixed on the organism side). The precedent to copy is World::organisms' generational OrganismSlot scheme.
  *Re-test when:* Holds as long as indices can go stale; a generational check is the discriminator.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §3d — option 2** - Split-brain storage (resource stays on Cell::aux, only new scalars in the sidecar): rejected — every write site must know which half of the layout each field lives in, and the bit budget stays full so the next scalar reopens the whole question.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional given the migration's purpose (four new scalars into zero bits).
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §3d 'The hard part, named rather than discovered later' — option 1** - Extending CellSurface/ChunkView with organism-data access so diffusion could keep running in the CA sweep: rejected — ChunkView is deliberately shaped around what update.rs's CA rules need, and organism state is a nontrivial new surface neither implementer carries (the same wall organism.rs already documents for TransportChannel).
  *Re-test when:* Superseded by moving diffusion to a per-organism pass over OrganismState::cells; would only reopen if the per-organism pass proved unworkable.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §3f 'Explicitly rejected: a global HashMap<(i32,i32), CellData> sidecar'** - A global position-keyed HashMap sidecar for per-cell organism data: rejected — a hash lookup on the diffusion path, positions unstable under a world that streams chunks, and an overwritten cell leaves an entry nobody owns or reclaims (issue #8's leak shape reintroduced at cell granularity).
  *Re-test when:* The per-organism Vec has an obvious owner for every entry; the HashMap only becomes arguable if per-cell data ever needs to outlive its organism.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §4c 'What "too much moisture" actually costs a root'** - Two softer waterlogging responses were explicitly rejected: reduced absorb efficiency has the mechanism backwards (oxygen is the scarce resource in waterlogged soil, not water — a root drinking less has no physical story), and slowed growth makes waterlogging observationally indistinguishable from mild drought, throwing away the asymmetry that makes the mechanic interesting. RootTip necrosis via a duration-gated anoxia counter was chosen.
  *Re-test when:* Grounded in threshold-then-death literature; necrosis also yields the emergent capillary-fringe root boundary the soft options cannot.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §5c 'The starting reserve, derived rather than guessed' (tuning consequence paragraph)** - Carrying tree.ron's pre-Decision-4 cost/rate tuning forward: the 6-way comparison found cost dominant only because a fresh cell's first Grow check read resource=0 and tips photosynthesized for themselves — both halves of that rationale are removed once seeds start with a reserve and photosynthesis is leaf-only. The existing values carry no authority over the new economy and must be re-tuned.
  *Re-test when:* Triggered by Decision 4 landing; the re-tune is the single tuning pass for the phase and must sweep TRANSPORT_SUBSTEPS and the canalization contrast alongside cost/rate/reserve.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §7b 'Data layout — four per-face conductances, not a packed direction'** - The research sketch's packed 3–4-bit 8-direction polarity enum was rejected: the bit budget that motivated packing no longer exists; transport is an exchange across shared 4-neighbour faces (no membrane on a diagonal, and diagonal transport breaks the ≤0.25 explicit-diffusion bound); the biology is per-face; and a single stored direction cannot represent a branch point — the data structure would decide apical dominance before the rule ran. Per-face [f32;4] conductance instead.
  *Re-test when:* Reason 1 lapses only if storage ever returns to a packed bit budget; reasons 2–4 are structural.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §7c 'What replaces diffuse_resource's symmetric average' (and §7j)** - The in-place, sweep-order symmetric-average diffuse_resource is superseded: each cell moved itself toward its neighbours' mean in visit order, so the total was only approximately conserved and order-dependent; and once leaves are the only sources, isotropic diffusion makes allocation distance-dependent for numerical rather than biological reasons (extremities starve because the solver converges slowly, which 'will look like a biological result and won't be one'). Replaced by pairwise, exactly-conserving per-face exchange with substeps.
  *Re-test when:* The every-frame execution cadence was also an accident of the CA sweep (~45x per organism tick); TRANSPORT_SUBSTEPS makes it an explicit tunable.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §7h 'Worked example' — Run 1** - Allocation split by hunger alone (the isotropic rule, c_A = c_B always): worked through, the lead flips by tick 5 — hunger is negative feedback, the tip that grows becomes less hungry and hands the advantage back, so nothing accumulates and sibling branches alternate instead of diverging. This is the arithmetic behind rewrite-design §3's honest 'genuinely emergent and variable' outcome.
  *Re-test when:* Fixed by flux-ratcheted per-face conductance (Decision 6), which converges to a stable ~1.38 conductance ratio rather than running away; the regression signature to test for is the lead flipping, and VEIN_GAIN = 0 must reproduce the isotropic results exactly.
- **Reports/plant-substrate-v2-design.md §8a 'Polarity — no longer out of scope; this section records the reversal'** - Deferring polarity until after the whole substrate phase (adopted on explicit instruction to avoid tuning against a mechanism about to change) was reversed: deferral does not avoid the double tuning, it guarantees it, because the leaf/reserve step re-tunes the entire resource economy and that re-tune is worthless if transport changes afterwards. Polarity became Decision 6, sequenced before anything re-tunes tree.ron.
  *Re-test when:* The original reasoning ('don't tune against a mechanism that will change') was right — it is the sequencing conclusion that was wrong. Also recorded: the 'two more f32s, no migration' seam estimate was right about cost, wrong about shape (four per-face f32s, not a vector).
- **Reports/tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md 'Deferred, with reasons' — child provisioning bullet** - Child provisioning (seeding a newborn tip with carbon to close the born-holding-zero conservation hole, 52.8% of starvation refusals) was reverted: +32% in one scene and −66% in another — scene-dependent, not a model.
  *Re-test when:* Revisit once Phase 4 makes allocation explicit; explicit income shares may subsume it entirely.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md 'Deferred, with reasons' — self-pruning bullet (also 'Why this is one design and not a list of fixes')** - Flat per-cell maintenance respiration was tried and impoverished rather than shaped: cost linear in mass against income linear in leaf count balances at any size, so a flat upkeep bounds nothing (Takenaka's exponent is 1.5).
  *Re-test when:* Re-attempt self-pruning only with superlinear upkeep — cheapest is upkeep proportional to girth, which Phase 3 already computes.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md 'Deferred, with reasons' — self-pruning bullet (source caveat in tree-procedural-prior-art.md 'Shedding — and a warning that applies directly to us')** - Combining a graded shed rule with a binary space/light signal makes branches vanish the instant they stop extending (Palubicki's own caveat, and a recorded failure mode in this repo). 
  *Re-test when:* If self-pruning lands, the light signal feeding it must be graded — this became moot for occupancy only after the per-column graded transmission work.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md 'Deferred, with reasons' — struck-through canopy_density bullet (refuted in tree-architecture-variety-review-verification.md §3.3)** - The claim 'canopy_density reads max 0.000 at end of run, so crowding_weight is inert and crown shyness landed on a dead channel' (originating in tree-extension-audit.md) was withdrawn: the channel is read at candidate-evaluation time during growth when deposits are fresh, and an end-of-run standing readout of a per-tick-decaying signal necessarily reads ~0. The sweep 0.5→136 vs 20.0→13 cells is direct proof the knob is live. Do not spend a session resurrecting a channel that is not dead.
  *Re-test when:* What is genuinely wrong with the term is its scale (normalise into [0,1] by GROW_CANOPY_DEPOSIT), not its liveness. Probe the channel while trees are still growing (reads ~1.5), never at end of run.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md 'Landed since the plan was written' — leaf clusters row** - One cell per leaf was under-scaled against wood: a 2D cross-section shows every twig, so single-cell leaves rendered as a bare skeleton with specks. Replaced by five-cell clusters per node.
  *Re-test when:* The cluster is a visual-scale correction only — income is held per node (LEAF_INCOME_PER_TICK per cell), and the variety review's normalisation makes size invariant to leaf_cluster.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md 'The queue, in the order I would take it', item 1** - Any fixed instantaneous shade-abscission threshold against the raw day/night light channel is a nightly extinction event: measured at 0.25 and 0.6, both took the stand to exactly zero leaves even after the light fix, because every leaf reads near zero at night on a 3,600-frame 20:1 oscillator.
  *Re-test when:* Requires a per-cell running mean over at least one cycle (or the noon-equivalent normalisation CLAUDE.md records as since built) before any shade-gated economics can work.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-research.md §0 'The vocabulary — and a framing this document got wrong first'** - The first framing blamed the blob on the tree being decurrent and would have built apical control first. Owner correction: decurrent oaks still have a clear bole and a shell crown; the missing mechanism is crown recession / interior die-back, and apical control first answers the wrong question.
  *Re-test when:* Held for the 'canopy is a mass' complaint. Excurrent-vs-decurrent remains a real axis worth a species parameter later — just not the fix for the blob.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-research.md §0b 'Everything here must be per-species, not per-tree'** - TransportChannel was specified as a per-species behaviour and cut before implementation for want of a caller; the shared DIFFUSION_RATE that replaced it has been sufficient through three subsequent passes. Generalising before one point on the parameter surface demonstrably works produces knobs that turn out to be the wrong knobs.
  *Re-test when:* Re-test only when a second plant form actually needs per-species transport — i.e. when a caller exists.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-research.md §7b 'The reframing: a real crown is a solid mass — of leaves'** - 'Stop the crown filling' was the wrong target: in 2D a filled crown silhouette is correct. What is wrong is composition — wood 12,039 vs 253 leaves (48:1). Self-pruning was originally proposed to serve the wrong framing.
  *Re-test when:* Held once the 2D-projection reading was adopted; the corrected target is bounding wood by the pipe model and letting leaves be abundant, with self-pruning re-scoped to crown recession/clear bole.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-research.md §7c 'Crown shyness — one real mechanism, both merge problems'** - candidate_crowding filtering the density read to the tip's own organism_id (with a test asserting it) is the opposite of crown shyness — a phytochrome cannot ask whose leaf reflected the light. The filter was replaced by an owner-blind read, converting self-avoidance into stand-level spacing for free.
  *Re-test when:* The filter was a reasonable call only under the original self-avoidance framing; under the far-red shade-avoidance reading it is wrong.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-research.md §7d 'What this means for the order' (closing caution)** - Judging a tree-shape change against the previous frame: an intermediate that got less blobby was described as 'a genuine tree' and the owner overturned it ('better but still blobby'). A time series that improves is not the same as a tree.
  *Re-test when:* Judge against the absolute criteria — a clear bole and a foliage crown — never against the prior artifact.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-variety-review.md §2.2 'The crown-shyness cliff'** - Pushing crowding_weight high to bound the canopy hits a collapse cliff (20.0 → median tree 26 cells; the sweep 0.5→136 … 20.0→13): under subtract-then-filter (score > 0.0), past a threshold the term does not bias the choice, it empties the candidate set, the tip ages out and the tree strangles. The cliff is a code artifact of the score form, not a property of crown shyness.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the score is additive-subtract-then-filter; a multiplicative form has no cliff at any weight — but per the verification report §2.2, do not land the multiplicative form before the light under-attenuation fix, because dead-ending tips is currently the only mechanism stopping lateral spread.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-variety-review.md §3.2 'Orthotropic vs plagiotropic'** - upward_weight measured inert across 1,024 genomes (a listed do-not-relitigate finding). Extension, not dispute: jittering the magnitude of a bias whose direction is fixed and shared by every axis moves nothing, because the term is the same for all candidates' competitors.
  *Re-test when:* Re-measure only after a per-order tropism direction (orthotropic/plagiotropic reference) exists — a per-order direction is a different quantity and should be measured before upward_weight is written off.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-variety-review-verification.md §2.3 '"pipe_ratio is ~20x its daily-mean value" — no, ~2.8x'** - The claim that noon-latched q_peak inflates pipe_ratio ~20x over a daily-mean convention was rejected: the daily mean of sky_light_amplitude is ~1.41 against noon 4.0, so the inflation is ~2.8x. 20:1 is the night:noon range, relevant to instantaneous gates only, not to a latched maximum.
  *Re-test when:* Direction of the argument (q_peak is a noon-only quantity) survives; only the magnitude was wrong.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-variety-review-verification.md §3.1 'phototropism_dir cannot point sideways' (with plant-species-authoring.md §1 and the implementation plan's queue item 2)** - light_weight measured completely inert across 1,024 genomes, and the inertness is by construction: phototropism_dir's entire codomain is {(0,−1),(0,0)}, collinear with upward_weight, so no scene (slope, clearing, neighbour shadow) can make it respond — horizontal gradients have no component to express. The earlier advice to just re-test it on a slope scene was itself testing a lever that cannot respond.
  *Re-test when:* Re-run the genome study only after the probe is reshaped to a real gradient (the moisture_pull form: sample ±offset in x and y, return normalised gradient). Until then it is correctly parked at variance 0.0.
- **Reports/tree-diagnosis-review.md §1 '"Extension is one-shot" is wrong — the binding constraint is the world's top edge' (resolved in tree-shape-problem-statement.md §8)** - Diagnosing and tuning trees in ceilinged scenes: the sub-critical-branching diagnosis was measured at ground=96 where the ceiling manufactures tip deaths; adding sky gave 4.6x more tissue (3,142 → 14,459 cells). This was the third recurrence of the same failure (forest at 40 rows, grove at 96). Never re-tune against forest or the default plant_probe ground.
  *Re-test when:* §8's resolution: both effects are real — offspring per tip stays sub-critical (0.9983) even at ground=200 with only 9% boundary deaths — so the scene must have headroom (ground=200) before any growth-rate conclusion is trusted.
- **Reports/tree-diagnosis-review.md §2 'The whip/blob dichotomy rests on two non-comparable harnesses' (fixed per tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md Phase 0a)** - Comparing plant_probe and filmstrip grove as if they were one scene: they differed in tree count, spacing (56 vs 140 at 8 trees), seed placement and soil depth, which is why the crown-shyness commit was tuned at one stand density and eyeballed at another. Also from the same section: 'whip ~40 cells' was the leaf median, wrong by ~12x as a tree size.
  *Re-test when:* Fixed by the single scene builder in examples/common/mod.rs; the dead end returns if any harness stops calling it.
- **Reports/tree-diagnosis-review.md §3 'The thicken() row-total gate has a real and common failure case' (retired per tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md Phase 3)** - The pipe-model gate measured on the organism's row total: 53% of occupied rows hold more than one separate run, so a limb elsewhere on the row silently suppresses trunk thickening; leaves were counted as stem cross-section and the same cell appeared on both sides of the ratio. It fixed over-reading in a blob and introduced over-reading in the branched tree being aimed for. Do not fix the gate — it was replaced by BFS-derived girth (q_peak).
  *Re-test when:* The row-total gate was itself a genuine improvement over the two local probes before it (12,039 → 2,817 wood) — that improvement is the bar any replacement keeps.
- **Reports/tree-extension-audit.md '`max_active_tips: 14` — not binding' (and 'Corrections to the problem statement')** - Treating max_active_tips (or the scoring function) as what stops tree growth: 0 of 2,700 evaluations were rejected by the cap at 8 trees (0.4% at 16); trees spend most of their life at 1–2 tips. Conflating the concurrency throttle with a lifetime tip budget was a stated defect; the real limit was birth rate at 0.994 offspring per tip.
  *Re-test when:* Measured at ground=96 and 16-tree ensemble; the concurrency throttle at ~14 is fine, the lifetime count must be unbounded and supply-driven.
- **Reports/tree-extension-biology.md §1 'Meristem senescence is not real, and we implemented it' (also 'Explicit negative results')** - ORGANISM_STALE_LIMIT retiring a tip permanently implements meristem senescence, which grafting experiments specifically falsify (10-fold elongation recovery on young rootstock; bristlecone shows no age trend). Any timer-based permanent tip retirement models the disproven mechanism.
  *Re-test when:* Replacement is soft reversible dormancy (Palubicki's n = ⌊v⌋). The acceptance test is that dormancy is reversible — cut a limb and neighbouring dormant buds must restart, else the mechanism is ORGANISM_STALE_LIMIT with extra steps.
- **Reports/tree-rewrite-design.md §0 'The decision, stated first, corrected from revision 1'** - Revision 1's forced unification — one Divide struct covering both moss's uniform-random moisture-gated pick and a tree's score-then-sample resource-gated growth — did not survive review: they are different selection algorithms, and cramming both onto one struct either breaks RON's all-fields-required deserialization or silently changes moss's shipped behaviour. A second named behaviour (Grow) was added instead, with Divide untouched.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; the pre-sanctioned fallback in organism-substrate-design.md §1 was taken.
- **Reports/tree-rewrite-design.md §0/§1 (deletion target and what replaces it)** - The continuous-position space-colonization tree implementation (Tip/RootTip/TreeState with private float-position state, tree_tip_tick/root_tip_tick) is superseded by the per-cell Grow behaviour dispatched from real grid cells on the M16 schedule. Nothing may reintroduce private float-position organism state.
  *Re-test when:* Settled repo direction (design-philosophy commits to cell-typed organism modelling over TreeState-style structs).
- **Reports/tree-rewrite-design.md §1 'What gets deleted, and what replaces it — corrected retrofit order'** - Revision 1's retrofit order deleted the old tree implementation before the visual-verification gate that would catch a broken result — removing the rollback path exactly when it is most likely to be needed. Corrected order: old code stays reachable on its own key until live screenshots confirm the new system, and only then is it deleted.
  *Re-test when:* Accepted duplication cost is deliberate; the dead end is deleting the fallback before live verification, in any similar rewrite.
- **Reports/tree-rewrite-design.md §2 'Direction and self-avoidance: two separate local signals, not one'** - Revision 1's single score() blend of continuation/light/wind/upward terms silently deleted the old algorithm's actual space-filling mechanism (attractor consumption) with no replacement, risking tangled overcrowded growth. Self-avoidance needs its own channel (deposit-diffuse-decay), not a weight in the direction blend.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional: direction and self-avoidance are two separate local signals.
- **Reports/tree-rewrite-design.md §2a 'Direction: local neighbour-average, not "the parent"'** - 'Grow away from the single parent neighbour' is undefined the moment a tip has more than one same-organism neighbour — every branch point by construction, and whenever 2D growth curves back on itself. Replaced by the vector average over all same-organism 8-neighbours (reduces to the old rule at one neighbour, falls back to straight up at zero).
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional in 2D growth.
