- **Reports/tree-rewrite-design.md §3 'Resource: growth-rate limiter, not a dominance mechanism — walking back revision 1's central claim'** - Revision 1's central claim that apical dominance emerges from plain diffusion was withdrawn entirely: diffusion is an equalizing operator and cannot turn small early variance into a sustained dominant leader — that requires active suppression or a positive-feedback (self-reinforcing) transport mechanism. Resource-gating honestly limits growth rate only.
  *Re-test when:* The stronger mechanism (canalization / flux-reinforced conductance) later became Decision 6 in plant-substrate-v2-design.md §7; the withdrawal stands for plain diffusion permanently.
- **Reports/tree-rewrite-design.md §6 'Cell::aux layout' — correction from the second review** - Reading a coarse FIELD_SCALE=8 grid block-nearest for per-candidate organism decisions: all 8 of a tip's candidates sit in one field block and would read the same value, silently disabling directional self-avoidance — the exact resolution mismatch organism-substrate-design.md §3 already rejected field.rs's grid for (diffuse_resource).
  *Re-test when:* Fixable, not fatal: field_at_bilinear sampling (already used for phototropism/wind/moisture) is a required part of any choice that stores an organism-read signal on the coarse grid, not a later optimisation.
- **Reports/tree-shape-problem-statement.md §2 'Why tuning cannot escape it' (also plant-species-authoring.md §5, tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md 'Why this is one design and not a list of fixes')** - Tuning SecondaryThicken/thickening knobs to fix tree shape: measured across unbounded/bounded/off (12,039 / 2,817 / 1,623 wood), every setting is either blob or whip with structure absent at both ends. Thickening can only fatten what extension drew; it cannot make structure.
  *Re-test when:* Structural property of which mechanism the knob drives — permanent. If a form looks wrong in shape, the lever is in Grow, not SecondaryThicken.
- **Reports/tree-shape-problem-statement.md §4 (also tree-architecture-research.md §1 'The missing mechanism')** - Ratio bounds (root:shoot, shoot:root) were investigated as the absolute size bound bud break needed and found not to provide one — they bound proportions, not size.
  *Re-test when:* The size bound question was dissolved by the carbon-balance/maintenance framing (size bounded as a side effect of shape) and the turgor height bound; ratio bounds would only be worth revisiting if a proportion, not a size, is the target.
- **Reports/tree-shape-problem-statement.md §4 'Why the obvious fix failed before' (also tree-extension-biology.md 'Explicit negative results', tree-extension-audit.md 'Corrections to the problem statement')** - Bud break v1 — converting a MatureBody back to a GrowingTip on surplus carbon + low local crowding — was built and reverted: when a tree stops growing every local signal (carbon at clamp, crowding decayed, conductance basal) saturates simultaneously, so the rule fired on every mature cell at once and budding became proportional to volume, running away. The audit adds that a surplus-keyed rule also fires on the wrong cells because the frontier is the one place surplus never appears; the biology report generalises it: any resource-state/'am I idle' formulation of the bound has this saturation intrinsically. Do not retry local/volume-gated reactivation.
  *Re-test when:* Superseded by the bud bank (DormantBud deposited by extension only, whole-plant break_buds gate on supportable = floor(intercepted/cost)). Caveat from tree-diagnosis-review.md §4: the signal-equalization premise was substantially a consequence of the then-current LIGHT_DECAY tuning (mature cells pinned at the 4.0 cap), so a carbon-balance discriminator deserves re-evaluation under a properly attenuating light field.
- **Reports/tree-shape-problem-statement.md §4 'Why the obvious fix failed before' (reframed in tree-extension-biology.md §R5)** - Capping bud break to one bud per organism per tick converted exponential growth into linear growth that still filled the world — a rate cap is not a size bound.
  *Re-test when:* Rejected specifically as a size bound. tree-extension-biology.md §R5 records that a per-flush activation budget is legitimate for shape (which buds win) once size is bounded elsewhere: 'caps were never wrong per se; they were wrong as bounds.'
- **Reports/tree-shape-problem-statement.md §8 'CORRECTION: the size prediction in the first version of this section was circular'** - Claiming 1/(1−m) predicts tree size 'from a model with no free parameters' was withdrawn as a tautology: m was computed as born/(born+seeds), so 1/(1−m) equals born/8+1 — tree size expressed in tips. It matched every run to four significant figures because it was the same expression.
  *Re-test when:* A non-circular version needs offspring per tip measured per generation, or branch and death rates tracked separately over time — never a ratio of cumulative totals against a constant seed count.
- **Reports/tree-shape-problem-statement.md §8 'Standing conclusion'** - Tuning any knob for tree size: every parameter ever tuned on the branch moved the branching ratio m by fractions of a percent while size swung hundreds of percent — near criticality there is no stable middle, which is the whole whip/blob oscillation. Do not tune for size.
  *Re-test when:* Tune, measure and report m (tips born vs tips aged out); bound the plant by making m respond to crowding and light, not by capping anything.
- **src/sim/decay.rs test a_reseeded_organism_keeps_growing_after_its_first_tick (window comment)** - The test's 20,000 + 20,000 frame windows had to move: measured on the exact scene, the reseeded population grows 71→176→225 by 15k and is then flat to 40k with zero growable neighbours left, so the old second window sat entirely in the saturated regime and asserted growth where correctly none could. It passed anyway until the plastochron change (more standing income → earlier frontier ceiling) and the per-organism RNG moved saturation below 20k. Now 5k/5k, both ends inside the measured growing regime.
  *Re-test when:* Re-measure the saturation point if standing income, frontier retirement, or the scene changes; the assertion itself (reseeded growth advances past its first cell) is unchanged.
- **src/sim/organism.rs Behavior::Grow field crowding_weight doc (also plant.rs organism_tick 'Crowding divides' comment)** - Subtracting crowding from the direction score was tried: combined with the positive-score filter it stopped biasing and started emptying the candidate set past a threshold (density > 0.058 at weight 20), banking stale ticks into permanent retirement — the measured cliff in tree.ron's old sweep, median tree 2,620 cells at weight 12.0 against 26 at 20.0. Crowding now divides the score, which is monotone at any weight.
  *Re-test when:* Verified by putting the subtractive form back: a_crowded_tip_takes_its_least_bad_direction_instead_of_dying fails on it. Arithmetic, not tuning — do not retry any strength of the subtractive form.
- **src/sim/organism.rs Behavior::Grow field genotype_variance doc** - One shared jitter width for all genotype traits was wrong and was measured wrong: at a flat ±15% across a 64-genome population only turgor moved any outcome (height correlation −0.675, against |r| ≤ 0.13 for the rest), and ±15% on the integer plastochron's outer-order base of 2 rounds back to 2 across the entire range — a third of the genome dead on arrival. Widths are now per trait, sized to clear each trait's own quantization.
  *Re-test when:* Re-derive widths whenever a trait's base value or its quantization changes.
- **src/sim/organism.rs Behavior::Grow field genotype_variance doc (slot 1 paragraph)** - Jittering upward weight (genotype slot 1) is measured inert: across 1,024 genomes even at ±40%, quintile means were flat (1310/1460/1396/1388/1457 cells). Held at width 0.0 rather than deleted, because slots are positional and renumbering silently rewrites every genome ever measured.
  *Re-test when:* Re-test if upward weight gains real leverage over outcomes (e.g. a phototropism/gravitropism rebalance); never retire a trait by removing its slot.
- **src/sim/organism.rs Behavior::Grow variant doc (Divide/Grow split)** - Merging Divide and Grow into one parameterized behavior was evaluated and rejected: they share no real code (uniform pick + chance roll vs scored 8-neighbour sampling + resource gate), and forcing one struct either breaks RON's all-fields-required deserialization or silently changes moss's already-shipped behaviour. organism-substrate-design §1's pre-sanctioned fallback (two named behaviors) was taken.
  *Re-test when:* Re-visit only if the two mechanisms ever genuinely converge on shared code.
- **src/sim/organism.rs const VEIN_GAIN doc** - Canalization contrast tuning: 30:1 (VEIN_GAIN 2.9) was superseded by 10:1 (0.9) on measured establishment — 54/96 vs 70/96 at n=16, re-confirmed at n=12 after RESOURCE_SCALE was made to bind (56/72 vs 68/72). Going further to 5:1 was tried and rejected: it edges establishment (70/72) but produces fewer fully vascular cells and halves the ceiling a strand can reach, buying germination by making differentiation impossible, and it broke the chain test's own threshold.
  *Re-test when:* The 10:1 choice was re-derived once already when the transport clamp changed the economy; re-derive again if RESOURCE_SCALE, the carbon substeps, or the transport rule changes.
- **src/sim/organism.rs OrganismCell::heading doc** - Re-deriving a shoot's direction each step from supply_direction (a one-cell, 8-quantized local read) was the shipped behaviour and was rejected: the estimate was mostly noise and every step re-rolled it, so trunks wandered instead of rising and limbs zig-zagged instead of sweeping. Heading is now stored and carried forward with inertia; the local read remains only as the no-history fallback.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — real shoots have momentum; a lignified stem cannot turn sharply.
- **src/sim/organism.rs OrganismCell::q_peak doc** - The geometric row scan ('leaves in rows above me') as the support quantity was replaced: a limb on one side of the plant counted toward a stem on the other side that does not supply it, and a leaf below a cell it feeds counted for nothing. Support is a property of the vascular graph (basipetal accumulation), and per Palubicki it must be a monotone high-water mark — trunks do not thin in autumn, and the memory is what distinguishes lost foliage from never-had.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for the support quantity; the monotonicity is the prerequisite for the recovery fix noted at break_buds.
- **src/sim/organism.rs OrganismState root/shoot count fields doc (and plant.rs MAX_ROOT_FRACTION gate in organism_tick)** - Unbounded root growth was the shipped state once soil gave roots income everywhere: they proliferated until they had converted an entire soil bed to root tissue. Bounded now by a root:shoot allometric fraction — roots are built from carbon the canopy fixes, so a small canopy cannot fund an unbounded root system.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional in principle; the fraction itself is a tunable, and the aged-out-tip phantom entry shows what the gate does to blocked tips if retirement regresses.
- **src/sim/organism.rs OrganismState::cells doc ('A position-keyed map, deliberately')** - The design doc §3c's slot-indexed Vec<Option<OrganismCell>> addressed by an index written into Cell::aux was available (step 2c freed the bits) and still rejected, for three recorded reasons: every one of plant.rs's ~60 pack_cell_type writes would have to preserve a slot it doesn't know it carries (the enumeration-must-stay-complete failure class this project keeps rediscovering); 12 bits is a 4,095-cell ceiling against an already-measured 2,127-cell tree on a world that will grow; and the only hot consumer (transport) builds a flat topology once per tick, so both layouts index contiguously where it matters. The position-keyed per-organism map keeps §3c's real benefit (a home for scalars) without the encoding.
  *Re-test when:* Reason 2 only worsens as the world grows (M10 streaming); re-evaluate only if O(1) cell→data from a bare Cell ever becomes a hot path that the per-tick topology build cannot serve.
- **src/sim/organism.rs OrganismState::genotype_draws doc** - Genotype as a pure function of organism_id was the previous version: free of storage, but it made a plant's character a property of world event history — planting one sapling earlier redraws every organism after it, and slot-reuse generation wrap (at 16) hands out bit-identical ids whose repeats cluster spatially. Replaced by draws stored at germination, keyed on position.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional once free_organism/slot reuse exists — the wrap makes id-keyed genomes collide by design.
- **src/sim/organism.rs struct OrganismCell doc (plastochron parenthetical)** - Adding plastochron to the per-cell sidecar (as plant-substrate-v2-design §3a lists) was evaluated and rejected: it turned out to be lineage state, not cell state — a retired cell has no use for it and a fresh tip needs its parent's value — so it rides on ActiveKind::Organism, whose parent→child hand-off already exists for stale_ticks. Recorded in PLAN.md as the one place §3a is wrong.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional while the ActiveSite hand-off exists; do not re-add it to OrganismCell from the design doc's list.
- **src/sim/organism.rs test transport_never_pushes_a_cell_past_the_resource_scale (doc)** - Relying on transport's intrinsic boundedness after the move to the pairwise carrier rule was wrong: the old symmetric rule could never exceed the largest value present, but the pairwise equilibrium is R_j = R_i·c_ij/c_ji, so an unclamped sink settles at the canalization contrast times its source — measured on a 24-tree ensemble at 92.0 against a scale of 4.0 (enough to fund 460 growth steps). Separately, the first version of the guard test used symmetric conductances, under which the overflow is unreachable — it passed with or without the clamp and was worthless. The test must impose the asymmetry each tick.
  *Re-test when:* Any new transport rule must be re-checked for boundedness explicitly; a symmetric test fixture cannot detect this class.
- **src/sim/organism.rs transport section comment ('Resource and canopy-density transport')** - Dispatching organism transport from the CA sweep was rejected on measurement: it ran only on awake chunks — 22.8% of frames on the standard settled single-tree scene — against consumers that read it every ORGANISM_TICK_INTERVAL regardless, a correctness problem once Photosynthesize is Leaf-only. Extending CellSurface to carry the needed scalar was rejected (same reason TransportChannel was cut), and a split layout was rejected because the bit budget stays full and the next scalar reopens it; the coarse field grid was rejected earlier for resolution mismatch (organism-substrate-design §3). The pass moved to per-organism dispatch.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while transport must read OrganismState and ChunkView deliberately carries no organism state.
- **src/sim/parallel.rs ChunkView field organism_moves and src/sim/world.rs fn reindex_organism_cell** - Treating World::set as the single complete organism-bookkeeping seam ("complete by construction") was wrong twice: the parallel worker's same-chunk writes land directly in its own Chunk, and remote writes replay through set_owned — both bypass set. A falling seed vanished from its organism's cell list (the boundary-crossing variant had a one-row-in-64 window and grew a single sterile cell); the guard test ran only the serial driver so it could not see it.
  *Re-test when:* Every new write seam must replay the bookkeeping, and organism tests must run both drivers. Also: scalars cannot ride a remove+insert pair — the moment a carbon-carrying cell can move, a move-aware seam is needed.
- **src/sim/plant.rs Behavior::Germinate arm (resting check) and test helper plant_tree_on_ground** - Germination without requiring material underneath was the shipped behaviour and the first thing the owner reported ('the tree just starts growing in mid-air, no matter where you place it'). The resting check makes it structurally impossible; separately, every tree test used to plant into mid-air and relied on mid-air germination, encoding the bug — tests now put a stone floor under the seed.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; the check reads raw material, not is_empty, because managed-empty cells hold nothing up.
- **src/sim/plant.rs comment below const SEED_TICK_INTERVAL ('SEED_FALL_SEARCH lived here')** - A bounded search cone (SEED_FALL_SEARCH) for relocating a fallen seed was removed: it was a reach limit that needed tuning and had gaps a seed could fall through. relocated_seed now reads the organism's own cell list, which has neither.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while OrganismState::cells enumerates every cell; a cone would only return if the cell list went away.
- **src/sim/plant.rs const CANOPY_DENSITY_DECAY_PER_TICK doc (final paragraphs)** - Lowering pipe_ratio from 10.0 to 6.0 to restore the 'rows >1 cell wide' metric (42%→35% shift after the sidecar migration) was evaluated and rejected: it restores the metric to 43% but takes biomass from 5,806 to 13,795 by drawing a slab across the whole ground — tuning the artifact, not the tree. Eight candidate causes were A/B'd and ruled out (table in PLAN.md); replaying the old quantization moved the metric only one point of seven, and measured above the base stems actually got thicker (11.8→13.3).
  *Re-test when:* The metric is dominated by thicken()'s basal pancake; the real fix is the thicken() change PLAN.md lists as known-open. Re-tune pipe_ratio only against a metric that excludes the base.
- **src/sim/plant.rs const CANOPY_DENSITY_DECAY_PER_TICK doc (top half)** - Decaying canopy density once per CA frame was tried: a fresh deposit decayed to zero within ~10 frames at the per-frame rate, long before any neighbour's 45-frame Grow check could read it — found by live verification when growth still read as a dense round clump after the separate erasure bug was fixed. Decay now runs once per organism tick, multiplicatively.
  *Re-test when:* Decay cadence must match the read cadence (ORGANISM_TICK_INTERVAL); re-derive if the tick interval changes.
- **src/sim/plant.rs const CANOPY_DENSITY_DECAY_PER_TICK doc + fn write_carbon doc + organism.rs struct OrganismCell** - Packing organism scalars into Cell::aux was rejected on three measured failures: a 4-bit density field cannot decay below one quantum (0.267×0.5 rounds back to 0.267 — decay silently dead after two steps, and the constant's old justification claimed the opposite); every scalar update was a World::set that dirtied a chunk, so an organism kept the sweep awake merely by existing (sleep test failed at 70+ cells, forcing a write-only-if-changed guard); and cell-type writes needed the pack_aux_preserving_density patch to avoid erasing scalars. The sidecar makes the whole bug class unrepresentable.
  *Re-test when:* Do not move per-cell scalars back into aux; the design doc's §3c list of further aux scalars is superseded by the sidecar.
- **src/sim/plant.rs const INCOME_PER_NODE doc (leaf-count weighting paragraph)** - Weighting frontier income by leaf count instead of intercepted light was tried: leaf count grows with the plant, so every tip's share never fell and the stand fused into a slab. Intercepted light self-bounds via self-shading.
  *Re-test when:* Holds as long as leaf count scales with plant size while interception does not; unconditional in practice.
- **src/sim/plant.rs const INCOME_PER_NODE doc (predecessor paragraph)** - Denominating leaf income per leaf cell in raw field-light units (LEAF_INCOME_PER_TICK) was a re-tuning treadmill: 0.05→0.01 when leaf_cluster went to five cells (income quintupled, eight trees fused into a hedge), 0.01→0.004 when sky transmission became graded (mean tree 793→4,983, stand fused). Neither change touched biology yet both invalidated the constant. Currency is now per-node (L_node = MAX_LIGHT × leaf_cluster).
  *Re-test when:* Do not reintroduce constants denominated in raw summed field light; any light-model or cluster-size change invalidates them by construction.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn allocate_to_frontier (first-version comment) and fn break_buds known-defect doc** - Dividing the plant's stored carbon (stock) among tips — allocate_to_frontier's first version, and the 'obvious' recovery fix of adding stock to the supportable numerator — was tried: stock grows with mass, so every tip's share stays high forever; the stand fused into one solid canopy, 38,605 cells against 1,723. The quantity divided must be income (bounded by intercepted light).
  *Re-test when:* Stock may only enter the equation once a monotone high-water memory (q_peak girth memory) can distinguish a plant that lost foliage from one that never had any; until then mobilising reserves refuses fusion-free tuning.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn break_buds (and organism.rs Behavior::BudBreak doc)** - A bud-break gated on local 'am I idle' signals (carbon fill, crowding decay, conductance relaxing to basal) was built and reverted: every local resource signal saturates on every mature cell simultaneously the moment growth stops, so budding became proportional to volume and ran away. Replaced by a whole-plant gate on intercepted light (Palubicki's supportable count).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while resource signals equalize at growth stop (the transport clamp fills carbon to cap everywhere, crowding decays within two ticks, conductance relaxes without flux). Only a local signal that provably does not saturate plant-wide would justify retrying.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn break_buds doc, property 2** - A bare rate cap ('one bud per organism per tick') as the bound on frontier creation was considered and rejected: it converts exponential growth into linear growth, which still fills the world. It survives only as a rate limit on top of the supportable-count bound.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — a cap bounds rate, never total; the bound must come from income.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn break_buds payment comment (and organism.rs BudBreak::cost doc; test a_flushing_bud_keeps_its_standing_carbon_and_the_richest_cell_pays)** - break_buds used to assign bud_cost onto the flushing bud (write_carbon as an assignment), silently destroying whatever the bud already held — up to ~3.8 of the 4.0 cap on every flush — while the behaviour doc claimed the bud paid. Fixed contract: the richest cell pays, the bud keeps its stake floored at bud_cost. Found by the second independent review.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — conservation; the floor stays because a fresh tip must afford one growth step.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn break_buds, supportable computation comment** - Enforcing max_active_tips only inside Grow let a bud flush push a plant past the species cap — under-enforcement the tripwire test caught (16 tips against a cap of 14) the moment multiplicative crowding let crowded tips live long enough for the cap to matter. Both creators of frontier now share one gate.
  *Re-test when:* Any new creator of frontier must run through the same cap; the leak was invisible until an unrelated change made it reachable.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn candidate_crowding doc ('The organism_id filter this used to carry has been removed')** - Filtering the crowding read to the plant's own cells shipped and was removed: the channel models shade-avoidance signalling (red/far-red off nearby foliage) and a phytochrome cannot ask whose leaf it bounced off — the self-avoidance framing was the wrong reading of the mechanism. Cross-organism crowding is also what 2D needs, since crowns merge ~R times more readily than 3D-calibrated models expect.
  *Re-test when:* Rejected on citation (tree-architecture-research §7c); reinstate only if the channel is re-framed as something other than shade avoidance.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn displace_soil_water doc** - Letting Grow write a root cell straight over penetrable soil silently deleted the water in that soil's aux — SOIL_FIELD_CAPACITY (620) units per cell, roughly 62 saturated cells' worth for a hundred-cell root system in the forest scene, unseen because no conservation tally covers held water. Found by independent review. Crediting the water to the root as resource was also rejected: it double-counts against Absorb, the behaviour that already takes water up. The water is displaced into neighbouring soil instead.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while liquid tallies only know Liquid cells (Material::water_capacity's recorded caveat) — nothing else will catch a regression here.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn organism_tick, 'A RootTip retires too, and it must' comment** - Keeping RootTip as RootTip on retirement was harmless only because roots could not grow at all (Grow required an empty neighbour and a buried root never had one). Once roots could enter soil and had income, every root cell stayed an eligible tip forever and the frontier multiplied instead of advancing — sprawling horizontal mats across the whole soil bed within 20,000 frames, identical to the canopy hub failure.
  *Re-test when:* A textbook vacuous-condition dead end: the 'harmless' judgment expired the moment Decisions 1(ii)/3 gave roots a way to grow. Re-check any non-retiring frontier type whenever it gains a new way to grow.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn organism_tick, Divide arm (new-cell comment)** - Seeding a divided child with the parent's post-cost resource (resource − cost) was an earlier version's bug caught by independent review: the parent already pays cost, so handing the child the leftover too manufactures resource − cost from nothing on every division. Children start at 0.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — cost is what a division consumes, not what it transfers.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn organism_tick, gravity/up term comment (near phototropism/wind reads)** - Defaulting the gravity term to (0.0, 1.0) (down) for every cell type meant GrowingTip's positive upward_weight actually rewarded downward growth, and MIZ1 moisture pull could steer the canopy (scoped to roots only by the design). The doc's own claim that the term 'contributes nothing in practice' was wrong — the weight was small, not the direction correct. Caught by independent review; canopy now gets a true fixed up reference, MIZ1 stays RootTip-only.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn organism_tick, Grow arm comment above the resource gate** - Marking the resource/turgor/tip-cap gates as 'had somewhere to try' (setting found_candidate) was tried and reverted: it stops the staleness counter, so a resource-starved tree never retires its tips and grows forever. Staleness converting 'temporarily short' into 'permanently short' is the entire termination mechanism.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while tip retirement via stale_ticks is what terminates growth (a_tree_eventually_stops_growing depends on it).
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn organism_tick, leaf-placement comment (and test near self_type_after_grow)** - Converting the retiring parent itself into a Leaf (plant-substrate-v2-design §5a, prized for 'no new cell creation') shipped and was rejected: the stem read as wood-wood-leaf-wood layers (owner-reported), MatureBody-only thicken() gave patchy thickening, leaves in the stem carried structural load (forbidden by the design's own §6a), and leaf abscission would have cut the trunk into pieces every plastochron. Leaves now spawn laterally beside the always-wood retiring cell.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — three independent failures recorded; the §5a design text itself is wrong on this point.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn organism_tick, tip-retirement comment (and test a_growing_tip_retires_to_mature_body_after_successfully_growing)** - Leaving a GrowingTip equally eligible to grow again after a successful step was the shipped behaviour and was rejected: live-verification logs showed 78% of Grow-evaluated positions revisited 3+ times (one cell 8 times), radiating growth from a small static set of hub points and producing a dense round clump instead of a tree. It was not a scoring-weight tuning problem. Tips retire to MatureBody the tick they grow.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; also the reason 'count simultaneously-alive tips' stopped meaning anything (see the branching-proxy entry).
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn organism_tick, turgor gate comment (turgor_taper)** - A hard step cutoff at the turgor height bound (no taper) shipped and was superseded: every lineage ran full speed to one row and stopped, piling growth under the bound, and all eight measured trees capped with a flat horizontal plate. Lockhart's (P−Y)-proportional taper makes the last stretch stochastic so crowns fade over a band.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — the step form was rejected on visual measurement (eight trees, all flat-topped); any replacement must keep termination graded.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn thicken (flood-fill comment) and fn step_organisms doc** - Deriving each cell's leaf count by flood-filling the whole organism per MatureBody cell per tick was quadratic in tree size and measured at roughly half of all simulation time for a six-tree stand (41s vs 20s over 6,000 frames with thickening disabled). One per-organism row histogram produces the identical number in O(cells + rows).
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — same quantity, three orders cheaper; also note the fill must traverse 8 neighbours (4-neighbour version counted fragments and mis-calibrated pipe_ratio, per CLAUDE.md).
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn thicken (pipe-model comment, 'Counting the entire organism instead')** - Counting the entire organism (the undirected flood fill before the 'above' filter was added) as the pipe-model numerator thickens every cell equally and produces a slab. Supply is directional: a trunk carries the whole canopy, a twig almost nothing.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — the pipe model specifies foliage supplied, not organism size.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn thicken side-choice comment** - A flat [(-1,0),(1,0)] side order with a break made every thickening cell choose left when both sides were open, so trunks fattened entirely to one side (owner-reported as 'kind of weird'). Same class as commit 68371d7's liquid body shedding bug: a two-option loop with a break is a directional bias unless something breaks the tie. Coin flip from the organism's own RNG stream now.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — named as a bug class; check any new two-option loop with a break.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn thicken width history (first bullet) and test a_trunk_already_at_its_pipe_model_width_refuses_to_widen_further** - Measuring stem width from immediate neighbours was tried: the growing end of a run always has one neighbour behind and open air ahead, so it read width 2 however wide the trunk was, passed the gate forever, and spread sideways without limit — the unbounded lateral slab.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn thicken width history (second bullet)** - Local width probes — the run perpendicular to supply_direction, and tissue density in a disc — both under-read inside a porous blob and ran away; the disc was worse than the axis walk (31,591 wood against 12,039). The current per-stem run only became viable because the allocation change means the plant is no longer a blob.
  *Re-test when:* stem_run inherits the same weakness: if blobs ever return it reverts to under-reading, and the failure signature is runaway thickening. Re-evaluate the width measure if growth ever produces blobs again.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn thicken width history (third bullet) and tests a_separate_limb_on_the_same_row_does_not_suppress_the_trunk / foliage_is_not_counted_as_trunk_cross_section** - The row-total denominator fixed under-reading but systematically over-read on branched trees: an independent review measured 53% of occupied rows holding more than one separate run (worst: 23 cells across 9 runs read as one 23-wide stem), so every limb suppressed every other limb on its row — the trunk hardest. It also counted leaves as cross-section, putting foliage on both sides of the ratio.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — retired by a constructed case where row-total and per-run give opposite answers.
- **src/sim/plant.rs organism_upkeep, Photosynthesize/shade_death abscission comment** - Hard-threshold leaf shedding was evaluated fairly (after the structural-check confound was removed) and rejected: totals are comparable (20,044 cells at cutoff 0.5 vs 20,213 graded), but the graded cube pressure wins on crown separation (fused run 37 vs 55), standing-canopy light (median 2.68 vs 2.15), no synchronized culls when a shadow arrives, and robustness to transient dips that a line converts into same-tick loss.
  *Re-test when:* The first sweep's 'any setting collapses the stand' reading was the confound, not the mechanism — re-judge thresholds only with the structural check stripped and on those four paired measurements.
- **src/sim/plant.rs stale-limit retirement comment ('Covers RootTip as well…') and test a_root_tip_that_ages_out_retires_instead_of_becoming_a_phantom** - Staleness retirement covering only GrowingTip left an aged-out RootTip matching no branch: never rescheduled, never retired, skipped by upkeep — a phantom still counted in root_cells and against max_active_tips, tightening the very allometry ratio that blocked it. Found by independent review.
  *Re-test when:* Any new frontier cell type must be covered by the stale-limit retirement branch or it becomes a phantom the same way.
- **src/sim/plant.rs test a_lateral_starts_the_next_branch_order_and_a_continuation_does_not (doc)** - Testing branching on one organism for 400 ticks passed until genotype jitter landed, then that organism drew a low branch chance plus a high turgor cost and never branched at all — branching is ~100 rolls at chance 0.03, so a single individual inside any fixed budget is a coin flip. Twelve individuals test the rule; one tests one draw.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while outcomes are stochastic per individual; the same reasoning is measured in the ensemble entry below.
- **src/sim/plant.rs test a_tree_can_branch_into_more_than_one_lineage (comment)** - Counting simultaneously-alive tips as the proxy for 'did it branch' is dead: the tip-retirement fix means tips essentially never coexist, by design, so the old proxy no longer measures anything. Branch points are now detected structurally (a cell with 3+ same-organism Plant 8-neighbours).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while tips retire the tick they grow.
- **src/sim/plant.rs test a_tree_eventually_stops_growing (doc)** - Asserting termination as equality between counts at two fixed frames (3,000 and 24,000) was a bar fitted to one individual's growth curve: when genotypes were re-keyed to position, the tree at that spot became a different individual still growing at the first sample (14 cells, reaching 295 by 27,000). The mechanism was fine; the number was a fossil. The test now steps in windows until the count holds still, inside a budget set from a measured plateau (565 cells around frame 50,000).
  *Re-test when:* Any termination claim must be phrased as stability across consecutive windows, not two instants; the fixed-frame form breaks on any redraw or retune.
- **src/sim/plant.rs test grown_trees_produce_leaves_and_a_trunk_thicker_than_one_cell (doc)** - Setting the leaf bar at >= 5 from a single run that measured 18 was found wrong the hard way: swapping to a per-organism RNG — which changes which numbers a tree draws, not their distribution — dropped the same scene to 4 and failed the test. Twelve identical trees span 31–153 cells and 10–33 leaves from one species file; bars are now set under the ensemble minimum with headroom, to catch the mechanism going inert (baseline: 0 leaves, run of 1), not to pin a moving shape.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while per-tree spread is this wide (measured 5x); any single-run bar in this subsystem will flake.
- **src/sim/plant.rs test the_turgor_gate_caps_height_independently_of_how_much_sky_there_is (doc)** - Every height bound tried before the turgor gate was either an arbitrary cap or a ratio, and ratios bound proportions rather than size — a fact that cost a session to establish. Before the derived bound, 80 extra rows of sky bought 80 extra rows of tree, every time, in every scene tried.
  *Re-test when:* A height bound must be derived from species numbers and scene-independent; retest only a bound with that property.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn update_cell (organism-transport comment before the material dispatch)** - Organism resource/canopy-density transport used to be dispatched per cell from the CA sweep; moved to one pass per organism in plant::step_organisms because sweep dispatch tied transport to chunk wakefulness, and the scalars moved to OrganismState where a CellSurface cannot reach them. Do not reintroduce a per-cell call at this site.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while organism scalars live in OrganismState and transport must keep running for settled (asleep) chunks.
- **src/sim/world.rs field seed (struct World doc)** - Keying plant genotypes on organism_id was replaced: ids are assigned in planting order, so an id-keyed genotype makes a tree's character a property of the world's event history — plant one extra sapling earlier and every later plant redraws (a fixed-frame growth test broke on exactly this re-keying). Genotypes are drawn from world seed plus germination position instead.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while ids are assigned in planting order; position keying is stable under save/load by construction.
- **src/sim/world.rs fn organism_active_tip_count** - Counting active tips by scanning the schedule heap undercounts by whatever is in flight (a dispatched site is off the heap), so the max_active_tips cap under-enforces — the tripwire fired at 19 simultaneous tips against a cap of 14. Count the organism's own cell list, maintained at the World::set seam under both drivers.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while dispatch pops sites off the heap before ticking. If the cell-list count ever shows in a profile, cache the type in the sidecar rather than returning to heap scans.

- **src/sim/plant.rs `organism_upkeep` die-back block (full account: `Reports/plant-economy-rederivation-2026-08-23.md` §2.1)** - Crown recession as a *per-cell* starvation roll, gated on `q_now == 0` so it could only take tissue carrying no living foliage, was built and reverted: it took the stand apart, 4-6 of 8 founders establishing against 8 of 8 with the charge off, median plant 704-2,569 cells against 3,437, and one seed ending at 94% root mass. `accumulate_support` walks a spanning tree over what is, for a thickened trunk, a blob rather than a tree graph, so `q_now == 0` means "not on the arbitrary path this tick's walk took" — true of most of a trunk's girth — and the rule was reading a traversal artifact as a biological fact.
  *Re-test when:* Only if `q_now` is ever computed over the true topology rather than a spanning tree. `q_now` itself is kept and is correct for whole-plant questions; it is per-*cell* conclusions from it that do not hold.
- **src/sim/plant.rs `organism_upkeep` die-back trigger (same report, §2.2)** - Triggering die-back on the summed per-cell shortfall (`maintenance_unpaid`) was tried and chewed a healthy growing tree continuously — 90 of 1,124 cells still reachable from the base. `organism::transport` is deliberately slow (undifferentiated parenchyma conducts at 0.008 against the flat 0.2 it replaced), so distal cells in a plant with plenty of stock run momentarily short all the time. Whether a plant is starving is `maintenance > income`, a whole-plant quantity.
- **src/sim/plant.rs die-back candidate exclusions (same report, §2.2)** - Selecting die-back candidates without the two connectivity exclusions was tried twice and left the plant in fragments both times: with neither, a bare branch carrying a leafy twig was all candidates *behind* the twig and connectivity fell to 52% of a 1,601-cell tree for four thousand frames; with only the `path_len` exclusion, seven cells of 1,321 still came adrift, because a thickened cell inherits its neighbour's `path_len` rather than incrementing it. The shipped rule adds the standard simple-point test, so die-back is a topology-preserving erosion.
- **src/sim/plant.rs die-back foliage exclusion (same report, §2.2)** - Excluding foliage from die-back by `cell_type == CellType::Leaf` was tried and deleted a grass sward: grass photosynthesises from `MatureBody` and declares no `Leaf` stage, so every blade read as inert structure — 0 of 12 blades standing where the guard expects 12, and the sod that holds a river bank went with it (+5% crest retention against a recorded +27%). `is_foliage` asks the species; this is the same trap P3 recorded for abscission, in a second mechanism.
- **src/sim/plant.rs `break_root_tips` (full account at the call site; `Reports/plant-economy-rederivation-2026-08-23.md` §6)** - Gating root re-initiation on whole-plant solvency — the fix §U asks for, "`water_status` scaling what a plant can *afford*" — was built and withdrawn. Under a maintenance economy a plant at its maximum sustainable size is insolvent *by construction*, so the gate shut the root amplifier on essentially every mature plant and produced the exact death spiral `allocate_to_frontier` documents: water-limited income starves the roots that would fix it. Four seeds at 28,800 frames, gate on against off: **6-8 founders established of 8 against 8 of 8 on every seed**, median root cells 156 against 305, median plant 1,979 against 2,729.
  *Re-test when:* Only with a gate that can tell "this plant is at its ceiling" from "this plant is in trouble" — the same distinction `break_buds`' own defect note needs and which `q_peak`'s high-water memory exists to make. `ROOT_TIP_POOR` still reads 0 and the exit is still structurally unreachable while a trunk holds carbon; §U is not closed.
- **src/sim/plant.rs `SecondaryThicken` arm (same report, §8)** - Charging secondary thickening one growth step per cell of wood laid, gated on whole-plant solvency, was built and reverted. It closes a real treadmill — a starving plant re-lays almost exactly what die-back removes, measured at 5,914 cells shed over 60,000 frames for a net loss of ten — but it cost establishment (6-8 founders of 8 against 8 of 8) and, at the pressure that restored 8 of 8, made trees *woodier* than the baseline (wood:leaf 2.35 against 2.19), which is the opposite of what crown recession is for.
  *Re-test when:* Free thickening is a real hole and the treadmill is real; a replacement wants the charge to come out of the same allocation pool extension draws from rather than out of the thickening cell's own carbon, which transport refills. Belongs with P5's reserve-mobilisation work, where the `q_peak` memory that distinguishes "lost foliage" from "never had any" already lives.
- **src/sim/plant.rs `MEAN_NIGHT_INCOME_FACTOR` (same report, §5)** - Comparing a plant's standing maintenance bill against its *instantaneous* night-scaled income was shipped and caught before landing: the bill is charged every tick and does not vary with the hour, so the comparison read four times worse at midnight than at noon and a stand shed on a nightly cycle. The tell was in the numbers first — one build reported a median bill-to-income of 0.6 at 28,800 frames (exactly 8 days, so noon) and 2.6-5.6 at 45,000 (12.5 days, so midnight). Decisions divide the oscillator out; only the pool and the photosynthetic credit carry it.

- **`src/sim/plant.rs` fn allocate_to_frontier, the donor sort; measured 2026-08-24** - **Caching the sort key to cut the donor re-sort's world lookups.** The per-frontier-cell `order.sort_unstable_by(|a, b| world.carbon_at(b.0, b.1).total_cmp(&world.carbon_at(a.0, a.1)))` calls `carbon_at` twice per *comparison*, so the sort alone costs `O(donors log donors)` world lookups and the loop `O(frontier x donors log donors)` — measured as the one superlinear pass in `step_organisms` (0.35 us/cell at 202 cells ticked rising to 1.94 us/cell at 969, while every sibling pass stays flat). Reading each donor's carbon once into the element and sorting on the cached `f32` looks exactly equivalent and **is not**: it changes the element type from `(i32, i32)` to `(f32, (i32, i32))`, and Rust's `sort_unstable_by` (ipnsort) specialises its small-sort strategy on element type and size, so the two take different code paths and order **equal** elements differently. Donor carbon is equal constantly — mature cells sit pinned at `RESOURCE_SCALE`, which the function's own comment describes as "the trunk sits at the cap" — so the tie order decides which donor is drained, and the stand diverges: measured on `plant_probe frames=6000 trees=4`, tree heights 101 -> 103, stem thickness 9 -> 6, root depth histogram [49, 43, 7] -> [47, 38, 13], cells shed to starvation 27 -> 56. Verified as a real divergence and not harness noise by running the baseline binary twice, which was byte-identical.
  *Re-test when:* The bit-identity half is permanent while the sort's tie order is left to the standard library — **the lookup win and bit-identity are mutually exclusive here**, because the key can only be cached by putting it in the element. It reopens the moment an explicit positional tie-break is added to the comparator (`.then(a.cmp(b))`), which makes the order a function of the comparator alone and therefore independent of both the element type and the library's implementation. That is a behaviour change in its own right and is an owner decision, not a free one — but note the standing risk it would remove: **as written, a Rust upgrade that retunes ipnsort can silently change how every plant in the world grows**, and nothing in the suite would catch it.

- **`src/sim/plant.rs` `Behavior::Ripen` arm, the "charged from the reproductive budget" note; measured 2026-08-29** - **Charging an organ's ripening cost against the organ cell's own carbon, the way construction is charged, was built and reverted.** It looks like the consistent choice and it is unpayable: `allocate_to_frontier` classifies every non-frontier, non-leaf cell as a **donor**, so carbon flows out of an organ toward the tips and nothing ever puts any back. A flower is therefore permanently poor and can never pay to set fruit. Measured on six plants, standing census: **35 flowers against 2 fruit**, where the two authored clock rates predict about 58; moving the charge to `OrganismState::reproductive_budget` gave 69 against 22 on the same scene. Every event counter read healthy through the broken version, and the picture showed a stand that flowers and does not fruit -- "a flower waiting" and "a flower stuck" are indistinguishable in both, which is why `World::organ_ripening_blocked` now exists.
  *Re-test when:* The rejection depends entirely on organs being *donors* in `allocate_to_frontier`. A fruit is a strong sink in real plants, and if that function ever learns to fund organs -- the source-sink model `plant-equilibrium-costs-2026-08-27.md` §10b gestures at -- then charging at the cell becomes payable and this reopens. Note the distinction that makes the split principled rather than a workaround, and which survives either way: **construction** (building the cells) is charged at the decision from the acting cell, per the owner's 2026-08-27 ruling; **provisioning** a fruit is reproduction and belongs on the reproductive account regardless.
- **`assets/species/herb.ron` header, the turgor-bound paragraph; measured 2026-08-29** - **Setting a determinate axis's `after_metamers` without checking it against the species' own turgor ceiling.** `turgor_source: 0.32` with `turgor_per_cell: 0.006` and `turgor_yield: 0.1` puts the height ceiling at `(0.32 - 0.1) / 0.006 = 36` cells of path length, and 9 metamers at a plastochron of 4 needs exactly 36 -- so the axis reached its ceiling as it reached its count, with `turgor_taper` slowing it for a long way before that. Measured: **9 axes terminated across 32 established plants**, and a six-plant contact sheet showed one flower. The symptom is the one the determinacy trap warns about -- a low counter with no visible cause -- and the cause is outside the fate table entirely.
  *Re-test when:* Not a rejection of a mechanism, a rejection of setting the two independently. `after_metamers x plastochron` is a path length and it must sit well inside `(turgor_source - turgor_yield) / turgor_per_cell`; 0.46 against a terminal at 40 is the margin that works. Anything that changes the turgor model, `turgor_taper`'s shape, or how `path_len` accumulates re-opens the arithmetic for every determinate species at once.
- **`assets/species/herb.ron` `max_active_tips`; measured 2026-08-29** - **`max_active_tips: 1` as a way to express "one axis" is not one axis, it is no growth at all.** `plant.rs`'s cap gate reads `world.organism_active_tip_count(..) >= max_active_tips` and the tip asking the question is itself in that count, so at 1 every tip refuses to grow on every tick. Measured: a whole stand germinated, staled at one cell, went senescent for want of a vital cell and rotted away inside 8,000 frames -- **population 0**, which reads exactly like a broken species file rather than like a tuning value one too low. 2 with `branch_chance: 0` is one leader and nothing else.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent while the gate is `>=` against a count that includes the asking cell. A gate written as "tips *other than me*" would make 1 mean what it looks like -- worth doing only alongside re-deriving every species' cap, since they were all authored against the current meaning.

## creatures  (55 entries)

- **README.md 'M18 status' — 'A worm is one MaterialKind::Creature cell' paragraph** - Creature identity as a raw index into a parallel World::creatures vector (with push_creature and a wraparound debug_assert): the scheme is gone entirely — a creature is now an organism like any other (OrganismState, Cell::organism_id, species .ron, slot returned on death), per Reports/creature-direction.md stage 1, which retired CreatureState. Do not rebuild bespoke parallel creature vectors.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the organism substrate (generational allocator, 12-bit index with generation-wrap counter) is the identity mechanism.
- **README.md 'M18 status' — independent-review paragraph on worm_tick and FLAG_BURNING** - Rebuilding a creature's cell from scratch (Cell::new) on every move: it silently cleared FLAG_BURNING and the burn-timer aux, and since WORM_TICK_INTERVAL (6) is far shorter than burn_duration (60), a burning worm survived every fire simply by moving — the ordinary case, not an edge case. A per-organism tick must defer (reschedule without acting) while its own cell is burning, the structural.rs precedent, leaving fire.rs to decide the cell's fate.
  *Re-test when:* Applies to any new organism tick path; guarded by a_burning_worm_keeps_burning_even_when_its_movement_tick_comes_due.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §0 Decision log, D1** - Life-Engine-style rigid multi-cell bodies (translating/rotating a shape through a falling-sand world) and Noita-style sprite entities atop the grid were rejected as the creature body plan in favour of cell chains: rotation on a grid is unsolved, and sprites stop being made of the world (no genome-as-anatomy, no partial damage as cell loss).
  *Re-test when:* §13h narrows this: D1 rejected rotation, not width — translation-only Rigid(offsets) bodies were later built (beetle) and work, buying the tunnel-refuge property for free. Rotation remains the do-not; a walking creature needs only mirrored left/right templates.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §0 Decision log, D4** - NEAT-style topology evolution (variable-length graph genome, speciation machinery) was rejected for the creature brain: hours-of-noise bootstrap, illegibility, and every downside traced to topology mutation, so topology got caged in a fixed scaffold with evolvable weights. Crossover compatibility across one shared scaffold is the entire reason.
  *Re-test when:* Unknown — recorded as settled with the owner (2026-08-17); re-litigating requires an owner decision, not new measurement.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §0 Decision log, D5** - The absolute rule 'never creature-to-creature queries' (emergent-world-architecture.md §7/§12-Q5) is superseded: fields are the default for at-a-distance sensing, but contact-range cell-grid reads are always fine and bounded local neighbor queries are legitimate when a field buys nothing visible. Do not re-impose the absolute rule, and do not 'fix' the old document as part of creature work.
  *Re-test when:* The hard line still holds at colony scale, where the field is the mechanism.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13b The homing half does not close** - The Jones/Physarum three-forward-sensor lateral steering (right−left at offset SO) is identically zero for a creature walking a surface in side view — both lateral sensors sit in open air while the trail is in the walker's own row (measured: cell holding A=27 read pheroA_lr=0.000) — and the three-forward-candidate rule cannot express reversal. No tuning or hidden-unit gating can fix it because the gated signal is zero; the fix was a PheroAlongHeading input plus run-and-tumble.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for surface-walking creatures in the side-view world; the triad works for followers in open 2D (which is what sensor_offset was originally fitted on).
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13c double_bridge measured the terrain** - The double_bridge assertion (mean channel-B higher on the short route) passed while measuring terrain, not path selection — with lateral steering dead, ants simply used the only route a ground-dweller can and deposited on it. Demoted to a printed note; a guard that passes for the wrong reason is worse than no guard.
  *Re-test when:* Re-promote to an assertion only once genuine path selection exists (post-§13e homing landed; assertions were still printed notes at last record).
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13d Corrections (action gating)** - Gating actions on a brain output crossing 0.5 cannot work with the fast sigmoid: squash(0.9)=0.474, so plainly-authored instincts sit just under the gate and the verb never fires. Actions are probabilities instead (which the moisture-gradient drop-bias multiplication requires anyway).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while outputs go through squash(x)=x/(1+|x|).
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13d Corrections (eat gate)** - 'Eat if energy below full' makes every creature permanently hungry and deletes carrying behaviour entirely. Replaced by a hunger_fraction (0.5) branch between eat and carry.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for creatures that both eat and carry.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13e (found_colony spacing)** - Founding ants shoulder-to-shoulder gridlocks the colony: a creature is neither a foothold nor passable, so a dense line simply stops (27,386 blocked ticks, an unbroken wall on screen). found_colony now spaces founders four apart — colonies need more corridor than they occupy.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while creatures are mutually impassable; letting ants pass over one another would reopen it.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13e Homing, built (gate pair offset)** - A symmetric hidden gate pair with gate magnitude 5 leaked 0.27 and emptied the foraging range immediately; magnitude 12 leaked 0.09 and the colony still drifted home over 12,000 frames. 30 puts both units at -0.967/-0.971 and costs nothing — do not retune the gate shallower.
  *Re-test when:* §13l adds the flip side: at ±30 the gate is mutation-fragile (a +10 perturbation collapses the swing to 0.217), so Stage-4 mutation must be per-weight scaled or the gate re-expressed at O(1).
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13e Homing, built (tumble)** - Tumbling on every failed move destroys the persistent run that finds food (33 pickups fell to 1). 'How often do I step' and 'how often do I change my mind' are different questions; TUMBLE_ON_FAILED_MOVE=0.35 was the fix.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for run-and-tumble chemotaxis with the current move economy.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13f (Crowding->Move) / §10 P-12** - Removing the Crowding->Move negative-feedback gain looked free in the flat corpse-pile world and was measured load-bearing in a real economy: ablating it cost 69% of deliveries. More evaporation will not fix ossification; the crowding input exists for that.
  *Re-test when:* Measured in the regrowing-leaves economy; the flat-world 'inert' reading was an artifact of the broken economy (§13f).
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13f (zero-genome control)** - Two of the first five ablation metrics ('range 118', 'left the nest 0.63') were measuring where ants were placed, not what they did — a provably immobile zero-connection genome scored on both. Only the zero-genome control exposed it; every behaviour metric needs one.
  *Re-test when:* §13n adds the limit: a control cancels a handicap shared by both arms (the plant_ant placement bug survived it), so pair the control with an arithmetic sanity check of the absolute number.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13f The landscape was flat because the economy was broken** - Running the genome ablation in the finite-corpse-pile economy produced conclusions that were all wrong or unfalsifiable: eight of ten authored instincts were bit-identical, Persist's apparent 2.2x terrain leverage was a hand-built-profile special case, and Crowding->Move read as inert. An ablation in a broken economy measures nothing.
  *Re-test when:* The economy was repaired by regrowing food (herbivory) — ablations are meaningful only in an economy where the loop can actually run.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13g Scenes must run in the environment the mechanism was verified in** - Two hand-built stand-in scenes both gave wrong answers: a flat floor is degenerate (an ant has exactly one legal step; 248 cells visited vs 1,670 on generated terrain) and a hand-built ridge produced one-column cliffs ants fell off constantly (6,985 falls, 1 pickup, still 6,269 after smoothing). Third time a hand-built stand-in was corrected by a generated-terrain arm — prefer the real generator.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for behaviour scenes; hand-built geometry remains fine for claims that are genuinely geometric (and then one creature per world, per §13h).
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13i Readiness check** - Sampling random genomes in an environment where doing nothing wins (immobile 0.554 vs forager 0.237) would faithfully measure which genome moved least and look like a result. Four environment attempts (abundant food; start energy 260; 150; 90 with beetles moved in) all failed the smoke test before this was understood.
  *Re-test when:* Repaired by §13n/§13o: with placement fixed, an 18,000-frame horizon and moss, foraging pays (+0.187 to +0.343); the genome sweep is legitimate only in that regime.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13j The synapse tax was 80% of a life** - Sweeping the energy economy with synapse_cost fixed in absolute terms while start_energy was cut 10x made every brain ten times as expensive per life (0.002 × ~24 synapses × 1500 ticks = 72 of 90 energy), so 'forager vs immobile' was really 'thinks vs does not think'. The sweep was invalidated and restarted with the tax held at ant.ron's authored ratio (0.002 × start_energy/900).
  *Re-test when:* synapse_cost only means anything relative to start_energy; any species with a different budget, or a future body-size/metabolism parameter, must re-derive it.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13k / §13n (eat_energy columns)** - §13k's diagnosis 'identical eat_energy 300/700 columns mean the knob was never connected (ants are not eating)' was wrong: ants were eating (30% by a species-specific detector); the knob was saturated — one meal of 120 already outlasted the remaining run against a 90-point budget. Identical sweep columns have two causes, disconnection or saturation, distinguished by asking what one unit of the knob is worth relative to what it feeds.
  *Re-test when:* The diagnostic that misled was a global eats counter shared with beetle predation; at the 3,000-tick horizon (§13o) the knob visibly resolves again.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13l (EnergyLedger)** - Reading EnergyLedger balance as conservation is a mistake: granted/eaten/died_holding are free terms defined as whatever happened, so a beetle bite books the eater's eat_energy constant with no relation to the victim's energy and the identity still holds while 300 joules are conjured. The real property is 'no lineage extracts unbounded energy from a cycle it controls' (a_sealed_world_with_no_food_source_runs_down, ~~currently #[ignore]d and failing: a starved ant leaves 240 joules of corpse~~ — **CORRECTED 2026-08-23: closed by S3, `src/sim/creature.rs:3327`. Un-ignored and green: a corpse is worth the body it was made of plus what was left unspent, granted and booked at spawn rather than conjured at the far end. Horizon 80,000 frames, measured-with-headroom — the box empties at 50,000 (12 alive at 15,000, 3 at 20,000, 2 at 40,000, 1 at 45,000, 0 at 50,000), so the bar carries 60%. The lesson above is untouched by that and still stands: a balanced ledger is still not a conservation proof, and `a_sealed_colony_never_grows_its_own_biomass` is the guard that actually watches for creation.**).
  *Re-test when:* ~~Fixing it means food cells carrying the energy they are worth, the same work as putting plants (the sun) into the ledger — do predation-only accounting and you do it twice.~~ **Condition met and the re-test done (S3, 2026-08-23): food worth now lives on the material (`Material::food_energy` / `worth_in_aux`) rather than on the eater, which is exactly the "food cells carrying the energy they are worth" this line asked for — and the prediction held, in that it was done once for eating rather than twice.** ~~Two seams are still open and are the live re-test: nothing books a *destroyed* corpse (`world.rs`'s `meat_lost`), so `max_standing_meat` is an upper bound and not a bound;~~ **the destroyed-corpse seam closed later the same day (WP-6): `EnergyLedger::meat_lost` books fire, explosion and the brush at the three call sites that destroy a stamped cell, and `max_standing_meat` subtracts it — a bound now, with only meat in flight on a `Particle` keeping it a `<=`. Decay needed no hook: `corpse.ron` declares no `decays_into`.** One seam left: the living-flesh stamp waits on S6, whose sink (a parent paying stamps) does not exist yet. Plants (the sun) are still outside the ledger entirely.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13l (GENOME_LEN growth table)** - Growing brain outputs 6→9 (commit 055ee23) broke the append-only genome law: the layout is row-major g[input*BRAIN_OUTPUTS+output], so appending an output changes the row stride and renumbers every weight with input ≥ 1. Only inputs may ever be appended; outputs never can be without a migration or an output-major layout — and the two look identical in the .ron and the enum.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for the row-major layout; ~~lawful only while nothing persists a genome (after Stage 4 any growth is a migration)~~ **CONDITION MET 2026-08-29: something persists a genome now.** `src/sim/species_export.rs` writes an individual back out as a species `.ron`, so a stored genome exists outside a running process for the first time (Reports/creature-export-design.md). The layout law itself is unchanged and the reserved-slot layout still makes an append lawful in all three directions -- what changed is that a *stale* file can now exist. An exported file is mostly name-addressed and survives a lawful append, but `hidden_wiring`, `hidden_outputs` and `recurrence` index hidden units positionally, so `SpeciesDef::genome_manifest` stamps `brain::genome_manifest()` on every export and both load sites refuse a mismatch. That is a provenance stamp with one load-bearing axis, not a migration: it cannot see a slot whose *meaning* changed under an unchanged name. The day a reserve fills, an actual migration is owed.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13m The renewable food was not renewable** - Routing an eaten cell's owner through reconcile_chain regardless of organism kind killed plants: a tree has an empty chain, which trivially satisfies the 'head gone' predicate, so eating one leaf freed a 789-cell tree's slot, left 160 orphan cells carrying its id, and stopped all regrowth — silently underlying §13k's 'foraging cannot pay'. Chain reconciliation has no meaning for a chainless organism; it must return alive and let the plant's own connectivity check decide. Reclaiming a dead plant's slot from inside a bite is exactly how this happened.
  *Re-test when:* Guarded by eating_one_leaf_does_not_kill_the_tree_that_grew_it (fails against old predicate); plant slot reclamation stays a separate BFS-from-roots liveness check, still open.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13n (spawn layout / denominator)** - Scoring survival as mean live population divided by requested ANTS while plant_ant silently no-ops on unfit cells put a per-seed constant on the outcome ceiling (23 of 52 placed on wetland; max survival 0.44). The zero-genome control could not catch it because both arms carried the same handicap; an arithmetic sanity check (immobile ant must starve at ~tick 900, score ~0.90) caught it in one line.
  *Re-test when:* Fixed by scanning for a placeable cell; also 23 ants is below the population stigmergy needs (feeding rate doubled 0.30→0.58 when fixed), consistent with P-15.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13n Moss, and three ways the economy sweep was lying (terrain)** - Running moss-economy sweeps on the 'rolling' preset was vacuous: moss is poikilohydric, and on dry sunlit rolling terrain ten moss cells never divided once in 6,000 frames. Only 'wetland' (86 → 1,194 cells) makes moss a food supply; the sweep moved there.
  *Re-test when:* Correct M16 moss behaviour, not a bug — any moss-dependent experiment must run on damp shaded terrain.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13o (beetles)** - Treating beetles as predation pressure was reasoning about a mechanism that never ran: every beetles:0 row equals its beetles:9 row bit for bit over 6,000 frames (no beetle ever touched an ant), because a beetle has no pheromone instincts and run-and-tumbles at random. The guess that predation capped foraging was falsified.
  *Re-test when:* Predation cannot be a selective pressure until a predator can find prey — the recorded argument for a fear-scent channel. **RE-TESTED 2026-08-29 and the parenthesis is now false: `examples/predation_probe.rs mode=ab` on this build separates the arms on every seed it was run on.** Seed 0 over 6,000 frames: ants alive 52 -> 50, injuries 0 -> 1, deaths 0 -> 2, and 83 samples of a beetle walking around holding a piece of ant; seeds 1 and 2 likewise (29 prey-carry samples each, one injury and one death respectively). `deaths` reads 0 in every beetles:0 arm, so every death in a beetles:9 arm is an ant a beetle killed. What changed is the food model, not the beetle: S5 replaced `food: ["ant", "corpse"]` with `gut_bias` against `MaterialDef::food_class`, and `act`'s eat branch now clears the victim's cell and calls `reconcile_chain`. **The conclusion the entry draws still stands** — 2 ants of 52 against 3,161 pickups is not selection pressure — but "the mechanism never ran" is no longer the reason, and quoting it as such is now wrong.
- **Reports/creature-evolution-plan.md §5 / creature-review-2026-08.md §T6 (the beetle's channel-B wiring)** - Wiring the beetle's instincts to the existing channel-B along-gradient (`brain::BrainInput::PheroBAlong`, one `.ron` line) was **declined on its own pre-flight**, before the edit: `examples/predation_probe.rs mode=preflight`, 8 world seeds x 6,000 frames on the scene the beetles:0/beetles:9 null was measured in. The trail exists and marks the prey — total channel-B mass 274 over 31 nonzero cells, and 0.752 of ant heads sit within a sensor offset of one — but **the beetle's two sensor reads hold the same value in 99.4% of samples, and on six of the eight seeds they are identical in every sample of the whole run**, so mean |PheroBAlong| is 0.0038 and a weight authored against it multiplies exactly 0.0. Not the coarse-field trap: `PheromonePlane` is at CA resolution and `sample` is nearest-cell, so the two reads are genuinely six cells apart. The cause is the **sampler**: the world holds 31 nonzero B cells, a sensor neighbourhood is 169, and the sense reads two of them (underfoot, and one cell at offset 6 along the heading). On seeds 0/2/3 the beetle is within six cells of the trail 28-52% of the time and *still* never samples it. Widening the sensor does not rescue it — the pooled reach histogram at 6/16/32/64 is [0.162, 0.267, 0.353, 0.598], so going from the authored 6 to 32 buys 19 points.
  **The wiring was then actually built rather than only reasoned about, because `differs` is *sampled* and a beetle ticks faster than the sampler** (`every=200` sees one tick in 25 at `tick_interval` 8), so a zero reading is observed rather than proved. `(PheroBAlong, Move, 2.0)` was authored into `beetle.ron`, rebuilt, and the identical 8-seed pre-flight re-run: **it moved on exactly seeds 0 and 3 — the two whose sampled `differs` was nonzero — and on none of the other six, which came back byte-identical across every column.** The movers are what prove the knob was connected (an identical-everywhere result is exactly what a stale `include_str!`ed binary looks like); the byte-identical six are what prove the gradient was zero at *every* tick, since one differing tick diverges a chaotic run. On the two live seeds the effect counters did not move at all — 302 carry-samples holding ant, 5 injuries, 6 deaths in both arms — and `beetle within sensor` went 0.280 -> 0.300 on seed 0 and 0.517 -> **0.450** on seed 3, i.e. both directions. A reshuffled random walk, not a hunt. The `.ron` line was reverted.
  *Re-test when:* Either the trail gets thicker where predators are (channel A is already 12.7x the mass over 9.1x the cells, with a nonzero along-gradient 15x as often — that is the arm to try first and it costs the same one line), or the sense stops being a two-point read. A concentration read over a neighbourhood, or a diffusion setting that smears channel B the way `DIFFUSE` was raised to 0.25 for the ant, would change the input this was rejected on. Carrion does **not** meet the condition: a corpse emits nothing on either plane, so it raises a blunderer's encounter rate without making anything findable.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13o Foraging pays, and the thing that was wrong was the horizon** - Judging forager-vs-immobile at a 1,000-tick horizon (1.1 idle lifetimes) was the single most flattering horizon for doing nothing — survival-over-run is itself a function of run length when strategies have different shapes over time. At 3,000 ticks the sign flips (advantage -0.112 → +0.187; +0.247 with moss). Three sessions believed the wrong answer; frames now defaults to 18,000.
  *Re-test when:* Any comparison between a hard-ceiling strategy and an unbounded one must sweep or justify the horizon; the binding quantity is the fraction of ants that find food, now reported beside advantage.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §2c Per-creature RNG stream** - Keying fitness-relevant RNG draws on position (the plant genotype is keyed on germination coordinate, plant.rs) makes location a hidden inherited variable and manufactures spurious 'evolution' results (plant-simulation-research.md §7d). Do not copy that pattern; key on the organism handle.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for anything fitness-relevant; the plant instance is on the shared-genome work's fix list.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §3b New cell types and organism state** - Packing new per-cell or per-creature scalars into repacked Cell::aux bits was tried twice at measured cost (the 8→12-byte Cell widening and the canopy-density 4-bit packing); the rule is the next per-cell scalar goes in the sidecar, and per-organism state goes in OrganismState. All 8 flag bits are taken and Cell is asserted 12 bytes.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while Cell stays a 12-byte tagged union with aux fully committed; a widened Cell or freed aux slot would reopen it.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §4a (blocked-move rule) / §10 P-11** - Depositing pheromone on a failed/blocked move was ruled out (the omission naive implementations make, per stigmergy-research.md §6): blocked agents reinforcing means congested dead ends accumulate trail. Deposit only on successful movement.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for trail deposit.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §4a choose_candidate doc comment / §10 P-10** - Replacing the Deneubourg probabilistic choice (p_i ∝ (k+s_i)^2) with deterministic selection (min_by/argmax) in movement is a named regression in stigmergy-research.md §2: it kills exploration and the trail mechanism collapses. The noise is load-bearing.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for stigmergic movement.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §4c Authored instincts + §13b (additive approximation)** - Approximating the carrying-conditional channel switch additively (both taxis gains active in a single-layer net) was tried and failed exactly as §4c predicted — a pure single-layer network cannot express 'follow B when not carrying, A when carrying'. The authored hidden gate units are in ant.ron and are the mechanism.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any conditionality that is a product, not a sum, of inputs.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §7a Representation and the contract with plants** - One mutation width for all genome slots was measured wrong on plants: at a flat ±15% a third of the genome was dead on arrival. Per-slot mutation width lives in species data; brain weights want absolute (~0.05) not proportional width, because proportional mutation of a 0.0 weight can never grow a connection.
  *Re-test when:* Measured on the plant genotype; holds for any positional fixed-length genome.
- **src/sim/brain.rs BrainInput::Crowding (doc)** - Fixing colony ossification (locking onto the first path found) by turning up pheromone evaporation does not work — P-12 is explicit that more evaporation will not fix it. The negative-feedback crowding input is the mechanism; a zero crowding gain is the first suspect when trails stop adapting.
  *Re-test when:* Per Reports/creature-direction.md P-12; holds while trail-following lacks another negative-feedback term.
- **src/sim/brain.rs BrainInput::PheroAAlong (doc)** - The Jones/Physarum lateral sensor pair (PheroALateral/PheroBLateral at full sensor offset) does not work for surface-walking creatures in a side-view world: both sensors land in open air, measured with examples/creature_probe.rs at exactly 0.000 over a cell holding A=27. Replaced by the along-heading gradient scalar (run-and-tumble chemotaxis). The lateral inputs are kept but unwired.
  *Re-test when:* Correct for anything moving in open space (a flier, a swimmer) — rewire and re-test the laterals then. Removing the slots is forbidden by the positional genome law.
- **src/sim/brain.rs fn genome_from_wiring (doc)** - The additive approximation of 'follow B when empty-handed, A when laden' was tried first on the report's own reasoning that the two gains would rarely compete. They compete constantly: 28 ants picked up food and all 28 were still holding it at run's end, mean position x=208 against a nest at x<120 — carriers walking away from home following their own food trail. A gated product needs the authored symmetric hidden-unit pair; a linear layer has no product and a single sigmoid cannot AND a signed signal with a gate.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the brain is the fixed single-hidden-layer scaffold; the fix is authored in species data, not code.
- **src/sim/brain.rs module doc, heading 'Why the topology is caged'** - NEAT-style topology evolution was rejected (Reports/creature-direction.md D4): every downside traced to mutating the graph — a variable-length graph genome needs speciation machinery to protect innovations, hours of noise to bootstrap, and produces unreadable networks. Topology is caged; weights evolve freely on one shared scaffold.
  *Re-test when:* A settled design decision; re-open only if speciation/bootstrap/readability costs are accepted deliberately.
- **src/sim/creature.rs colony founding constants (COLONY_ANT_SPACING doc)** - Founding a colony with ants shoulder to shoulder gridlocked it: a creature is not a foothold and cannot be walked through, so a dense line simply stops moving — measured at 27,386 blocked ticks against a single pickup, with the picture showing an unbroken wall of ants. Four cells of spacing for a two-cell body leaves each somewhere to go.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while creatures block each other's movement; re-test if creatures gain pass-through or climb-over.
  *Re-tested and concluded, 2026-08-23 (WP-9 arm 1) — and the entry survives, narrowed.* `CreatureDef::climbs_over_kin` makes a living nestmate count as a **foothold** — footing only, never passability, so two chains still cannot pass through each other and the "pass-through" half of the condition stays untested. Measured with WP-4's `forage_probe`, 8 seeds x 24,000 frames, both arms from one binary via `climb=` so no rebuild sits between them. **The first run of this re-test was done at the probe's own 2-cell ant spacing and reported a 22x range gain; that overstates it**, because 2 cells is shoulder to shoulder for a two-cell body — this entry's own gridlock — while a founded colony is spaced at `COLONY_ANT_SPACING = 4`. Medians, spacing 2 then spacing 4: round trips 18.5 -> 272.5 / 50 -> 161; deepest 23.5 -> 82.5 / **46 -> 84**; `>=32` bucket 0 -> 25 / **4.5 -> 17.5**; `>=64` 0 -> 6.5 / **0 -> 4.5**; blocked/moves 0.538 -> 0.060 / **0.311 -> 0.033**; deliveries 3 -> 10.5 / **6.5 -> 6.0**. So **the gridlock this entry records is real and spacing 4 is what fixes it** — spacing alone, flag off, moves deepest 23.5 -> 46 and the `>=32` bucket 0 -> 4.5 — and climb-over is a further, smaller win on top of it rather than the thing that dissolves the bubble. **Landed default-on for ants**: at colony spacing reach still roughly doubles with deliveries flat (6.5 vs 6.0 against a per-seed spread of 0-49), which is the work package's stated success condition, and the owner picked this arm on a blind A/B of the jammed crowd (card `20260823T164147144Z-e1ef72`). Two costs recorded, not hidden: falls roughly double on uneven ground (deaths stay 0), and on `ascii`'s foraging scene — one seed — deliveries fall 643 -> 270 with range unmoved, which every multi-seed instrument contradicts but which is the gated scene. **That `ascii` scene is the outlier, not `ascii`:** its sibling double-bridge scene, real terrain at the same spacing, goes forage trips 22 -> 233, deepest 16 -> 74, `>=32` 0 -> 21, `>=64` 0 -> 5, blocked 24,764 -> 4,264 and pickups 41 -> 218 on the same paired run.
