Two further costs recorded from that sweep: the excavation scene digs *less* (1,064 -> 726 cells of soil on fewer blocked moves — climbing is an alternative to digging past a nestmate), and the double bridge's summed channel B on the short route falls 28.42 -> 2.00, a thinner trail per cell from a colony spread over more ground (ungated; that scene already records route selection as undemonstrated). **The founded colony's look was then asked twice more and came back indifferent both times**, which closes it by eye without moving the decision: `20260823T180052569Z-c73d21` (24-frame sequences) answered *"Pretty similar"*, and `20260823T233741804Z-31974a` (20 frames, `seed=12`, a colony dug into a hilltop, the two arms differing by ~140 cells per frame so the knob demonstrably fired) answered *"No major difference"*. Only the **jammed-crowd** card divides the arms for a human eye; at `COLONY_ANT_SPACING` there is no jam to dissolve and the multi-seed numbers are the whole case. Do not post a fourth card asking it — the third existed only because its author acted on the owner's *"need an animation to tell"* from an earlier card without checking whether anyone had already answered the question. Guarded by `a_climbing_ant_is_never_its_own_foothold` — an animal's own tail is permanently inside its head's neighbourhood, so `self` must be excluded or every ant becomes its own ladder in mid-air.
  *Also recorded, because it invalidates a quoted baseline:* `forage_probe`'s 55-ant scene was built for the range instrument and its 2-cell spacing was never chosen as a jam. The "deepest 12-18, `>=32` at zero" known-good that WP-9's success condition was written against is therefore substantially a property of **that spacing**, not of the colony: at spacing 4 the flag-off arm already reaches 46 and puts 4.5 excursions per seed past 32. `spacing=` now exists on the probe so the confound can be varied instead of assumed.
- **src/sim/creature.rs comment where TUMBLE_ON_FAILED_MOVE used to be (above the failed-move reorient constant)** - Re-orienting on every failed move roll (rate 1.0) collapsed food discovery from 33 pickups to 1 by destroying the persistent run that covers ground — 'how often do I step' and 'how often do I change my mind' are different questions and conflating them yields a diffusive random walk. 0.35 preserved runs; the knob is now genome-owned (BrainOutput::Tumble) but the lesson stands.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any creature whose move probability is below ~1; re-testable only if movement stops being probabilistic.
- **src/sim/creature.rs const PERSIST_MAX / step scoring comment ('Persistence and caution come from the brain, not from literals')** - Hardcoding the straight-ahead score (an anonymous 0.15) and the foothold preference (a FOOTING_BONUS const, 0.6) meant those literals decided essentially everything a creature did: an ablation found eight of ten authored instincts produced bit-identical behaviour. Moved to genome outputs (Persist, Caution) with silent defaults, so measurement can pick the values.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while candidate scoring is dominated by those terms; any new steering constant should be a genome output, not a literal.
- **src/sim/creature.rs fn choose_weighted (doc)** - Deterministic selection (Iterator::min_by/argmax) shipped here before and is a published failure (Reports/stigmergy-research.md §2, P-10): the noise is load-bearing, and removing it removes exploration invisibly — agents still move, they just stop finding anything. min_by additionally returns the first minimum on a tie, so a worm whose neighbours read equal always fled west. Never replace the squared-weight sample with an argmax.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any trail-laying or gradient-following decision; the exploration floor k is part of the mechanism.
- **src/sim/creature.rs fn head_has_foothold (doc) and step_chain ('No purchase anywhere ahead counts as blocked')** - Two attempts to stop ants walking into the sky by leaning on the same support predicate both failed the same way: asking the whole-piece 'am I still standing' question about a prospective step passed while the body was still grounded, so the head led into open air and the chain fell a tick later — falls at 59% of moves, and pushing the bonus harder reached 80% while cutting deliveries from 90 to 4. The fix was a different predicate: per-step footing is about the head, and no footing anywhere ahead counts as blocked (turn instead).
  *Re-test when:* Accepted cost: ants cannot cross gaps (which real ants do not). Two fixes failing identically meant the predicate was wrong, not the tuning.
- **src/sim/creature.rs fn head_has_foothold ('The edge of the world is not scenery')** - Counting World::get's out-of-bounds BEDROCK sentinel as a foothold turned the invisible world boundary into an infinitely tall ladder: the probe found ants parked at x=0 heading permanently north, walking up the side of the world forever. The sentinel is correct for sand (keeps material in-world) and wrong for anything with legs — foothold checks need an explicit in_bounds test.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while World::get returns a solid sentinel outside bounds by design.
- **src/sim/creature.rs fn tumble ('Re-rolled among headings that have somewhere to go')** - Re-rolling a blocked creature's heading uniformly over all eight directions landed back in the blocked state over a third of the time (three of eight headings point into open air on flat ground): measured 29,344 blocked ticks against 41,843 moves — a colony spending more of its life turning on the spot than walking. Re-roll among viable (body-fitting, footholded) headings only.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for surface walkers; the extra scan of eight cells runs on a path that was about to do nothing anyway.
- **src/sim/creature.rs step scoring ('Added, not multiplied')** - A multiplicative footing discount was tried first and was far too weak: choose_weighted's exploration floor k dominates every small score, so a 20x discount on a 0.15 base still left a step into thin air at 16% probability and the colony spent 59% of its moves falling. The footing term must be an additive bonus that puts the supported candidate an order of magnitude clear of the floor.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the exploration floor exists in the choice function.
- **src/sim/creature.rs worm tick, fleeing branch (bilinear comment) and test near 'Swept over eight world seeds'** - Block-nearest field reads (field_at) for candidates one cell apart put every candidate in the same FIELD_SCALE-sided block, so all compared temperature-equal and the tie-break made the worm deterministically 'always flee west'. Bilinear sampling plus normalized scoring replaced it; the guard needed an eight-seed sweep once the choice became probabilistic, because under a weighted sample the same bug becomes a fair coin and a single run agrees with the right answer half the time.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while FIELD_SCALE exceeds the candidate spacing; any coarse-grid read compared across sub-block distances re-creates the tie.
- **src/sim/organism.rs BodyPlan doc ('Decision D1 rejected rotation, not width')** - Rotating a rigid creature body through the grid was rejected (D1): a rotated shape does not land on the grid cleanly — aliasing, self-overlap, cells appearing from nowhere. Gravity gives a walking creature a canonical up, so facing-left/right is a mirror of the template, not rotation maths. Width/translation was explicitly NOT rejected — translating by one cell is just a passability check.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while creatures have a canonical up; arbitrary-orientation creatures (swimming, flying) would reopen the hard problem.
- **src/sim/organism.rs CellType::Head doc (and scheduler.rs ActiveKind::Creature doc)** - The parallel CreatureState / raw-index-in-Cell::aux creature storage was retired rather than extended: no generational handles, no reclamation, and a u16 overflow guarded only by a debug_assert, so an active site outliving its creature read whatever had since been allocated the same index. Creatures are organisms now; stale handles resolve to None and the site drops itself.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — organism-substrate-design.md opens on exactly this failure (a third private per-organism state solution).
- **src/sim/organism.rs CreatureDef carry-threshold field doc ('Not "below full"')** - Gating eating on 'below full' (below start_energy) was first written and silently deleted the entire foraging loop: a creature is below start energy within one tick of birth and stays there forever, so 'eat if not full' means always eat — measured at 14 eats against 8 pickups and zero deliveries, while the colony looked exactly like one foraging correctly in the picture. The threshold is a fraction of start_energy in species data.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — and a reminder that this failure is invisible in images; count deliveries.
- **src/sim/pheromone.rs fn build_decay_lut (doc)** - Plain multiplicative decay on a u8 cannot promise reaching zero: at small rho, or with rounding instead of truncation, a low value maps to itself forever, filling the world with permanent ghost trails. Not hypothetical — the 4-bit canopy-density scalar measured 0.800 -> 0.533 -> 0.267 -> 0.267, a permanent floor that survived because its test asserted at full scale. The strict decrease is forced (min(v - 1)) and asserted at construction.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any decay on quantized storage; a fixed-point check belongs at construction, not in reasoning.
- **src/sim/pheromone.rs PheromonePlane::step (doc: 'Jacobi, front-canonical, two-phase')** - Do not optimise the diffusion pass into an in-place sweep: reading and writing one buffer makes the result depend on visit order, which costs the required determinism and reintroduces exactly the chunk-decomposition asymmetry field.rs is shaped to avoid (the codebase's most-repeated root cause).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while same-build determinism is required (PLAN.md) and the plane is swept in tiles.
- **src/sim/world.rs fn field_at_bilinear** - Block-nearest field reads (field_at) for a short-sensor gradient follower degenerate: a worm's four +/-1-cell sensor probes land in the same FIELD_SCALE block ~7 times in 8, turning min_by into "always pick the first candidate" rather than real thermotaxis. Bilinear interpolation added for fractional-position reads.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the sensor offset is smaller than FIELD_SCALE.
- **src/sim/world.rs struct EnergyLedger doc** - Reading a balanced energy census as "energy is conserved" was a real misread: granted, eaten and died_holding are free terms defined as whatever happened, so the identity holds while a bite conjures energy (eat_energy is the eater's constant, unrelated to what the victim had). The ledger catches charges that fail to land, nothing more.
  *Re-test when:* Holds until food/plants book real energy accounts into the same ledger (the sun is entirely outside it today).
- **src/sim/world.rs struct World (creatures comment near body_index)** - M18's parallel `creatures: Vec<CreatureState>` is gone, not moved: it had no generations, no slot reclamation, and a u16 overflow guarded only by a debug_assert. Creature state lives in the shared organisms arena (creature-as-organism, Reports/creature-direction.md §3a).
- **src/sim/creature.rs test the_mutation_rate_does_not_change_who_gets_born (doc)** - Guarding the shared-`Rng` invariant as "changing only the mutation rate must not move a single movement decision" over a breeding population: written, run, and red against correct code — a mutated child walks differently from a clonal one, which is the mechanism working, and the two effects cannot be separated in a population run because by the time a perturbation of the *parent* would show, the children have been moving for several ticks and are blocking it (measured: births identical 12/12, moves 779 vs 759, tumbles 303 vs 319). Replaced rather than widened, per CLAUDE.md's rule that a guard which cannot fail for the right reason is blind: the invariant is now asserted one level down by `brain::tests::the_mutation_rate_changes_how_many_draws_it_takes` (the caller's generator ends in a rate-dependent state, so sharing it is never safe) plus the dedicated `RNG_SLOT_BIRTH` stream, and the population test asserts only what is legitimately invariant — who gets born, since the birth decision takes no draw at all.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while children act in the same world as their parents. A harness that could freeze or remove newborns would make the stronger assertion available again.
- **src/sim/organism.rs CreatureDef::reproduce_threshold doc; examples/creature_probe.rs reachability line** - Shipping `ant.ron`'s `reproduce_threshold` as a live knob without checking it against the bank ceiling: it is unreachable by arithmetic, not by scarcity, and no setting of `start_energy` rescues it. An ant banks food only while below `hunger_fraction * start_energy` and one mouthful carries it over that line, so its ceiling is `hf*SE + Y` for a digestible yield Y; a birth costs `SE + body_energy*cells`. Reachable needs `Y - body_energy*cells > SE*(1 - hf)`, and with `ant.ron`'s deliberate `food_energy == body_energy` and a 2-cell body the left side is negative for any `hf <= 1`. Measured on the shipped animal: richest bank 568 against a 1,860 bar, `births 0`, and the ceiling *falls* faster than the bar when the grant is cut (bank 151 against 1,050 at `start_energy` 90). The only arm that opens it needs `hunger_fraction > 1`, which is `ant.ron`'s own measured dead end — deliveries 1,733 -> 3.
  *Re-test when:* Any of three change: the ant's body gets cheaper relative to what it can digest, an ant keeps eating past the hunger line, or a body plan smaller than two cells exists. It is an ecology decision, not a threshold to nudge.

## field  (34 entries)

- **`src/sim/field.rs`'s `skip_momentum` gate (per-tile form); measured 2026-08-26** - **Deciding the momentum fast path per tile instead of for the whole world.** `skip_momentum` is `sky_fast() && !any_fluid && read_coords.iter().all(momentum_zero)`, and `any_fluid` is `chunk_awake` ORed over every coord -- so one ant, one falling grain or one wave keeps it true. Measured at 8192x2560, `momentum` equalled `solved` on **every line the pass printer has ever produced**: the saving its own comment promises the "calm world" is never collected in the shipped world, which has a sea in it. The per-tile version asks the same question of one tile's 3x3 read halo (all chunks settled, all tiles `momentum_zero`), computed by dilating the few *carriers* by one ring rather than probing every tile's halo, and it narrows `step_velocity`/`step_advection`'s `read` snapshots to match. **The premise is sound and the mechanism fires**: on the frames the sky steps, 1,497 solved tiles become **147** momentum tiles (91% skipped), and the per-pass timings move a long way -- frame 3000 went pressure 0.92 -> 0.39, velocity 2.87 -> 1.11, advection 3.25 -> 1.49, field step 14.50 -> 9.87 ms. **Bit-identical**, verified by `FIELD_HASH` at 2048x640 and at the shipped 8192x2560, with the gate confirmed firing in the hash scene itself (`solved 320 momentum 118`) so the match is not vacuous. **And the whole frame got slower.** Eight alternating paired runs of two fixed binaries, 2,000 frames each: paired difference **median +0.594 ms, mean +0.590 ms, slower in 7 of 8 pairs** (15.75-17.53 treatment against 14.86-16.68 baseline). The gate's own bookkeeping is not the cause -- timed directly at **0.33-0.41 ms on a busy frame and 0.01-0.05 ms otherwise**, ~0.15 ms amortised against a 0.59 ms regression. What is left is that the momentum passes were touching every solved tile and warming them for the passes that follow: `diffusion` runs on the full `solve` set either way and measured *up* on the same frames the momentum passes measured down. The arithmetic was removed; the memory traffic was only moved to a colder pass.
  *Re-test when:* The idea is not dead, the accounting is. Retry if the field's tile storage stops being a `HashMap<ChunkCoord, FieldTile>` walked by pointer-chasing (a dense array or an arena would remove the cold-miss transfer), or if a pass ordering lands that does not run a full-`solve` pass after the momentum passes. Do **not** retry it as-is on the strength of the per-pass timings alone -- they are real, they are large, and the whole frame does not follow them, which is the entire content of this entry.

- **PLAN-log.md 'Architecture §4 (moisture field channel)' entry, rebuild_blocked paragraphs** - Keeping rebuild_blocked's early-exit while adding liquid detection failed twice in the same shape: exiting on the first solid cell broke the puddle-on-thin-floor case whenever a solid preceded the water in scan order (caught by moss_spreads_over_damp_stone regressing), and the rewrite's exit on the first out-of-bounds cell reintroduced the identical scan-order bug one level up for field blocks straddling a non-aligned world edge. The scan must run the full block with no early exit on either condition.
- **README.md 'M14 status' — 'The field coupling in diffuse_heat was tried, measured, and removed' paragraph** - Pulling every visited CA cell toward the field's ambient temperature in diffuse_heat: it never fired for the case it was built for (sleeping cells are by definition not visited) and cost a HashMap lookup per visited cell — worst frame ~16 ms to ~64 ms on the full-screen stress scene. Removed; ambient coupling is pushed from burning cells (World::add_heat) instead, a small bounded set.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while diffuse_heat runs only for visited cells and per-cell field lookups cost a hash; the Performance section adds that the sweep has no headroom for any per-cell check run 1e5 times a frame.
- **README.md 'The coarse field grid' — 'A fifth channel, moisture' paragraph, rebuild_blocked early exit** - rebuild_blocked's 'stop at the first solid cell' short-circuit: with Liquid detection added to the same pass, an unrelated solid cell earlier in scan order hid a directly-adjacent puddle and moss stopped spreading over damp stone. Removed; every field block is scanned in full.
  *Re-test when:* Full scan measured at no regression (28.0 ms serial / 8.3 ms parallel, same as record) on the full-screen stress scene at 512x320 — the scan was never the bottleneck. Re-examine only if a future scene measurably feels this cost; the README names it as the first place to look.
- **README.md 'The coarse field grid' — evaporation prerequisite paragraph on step_diffusion's wall rule** - Applying the strict field wall rule to moisture sources: step_diffusion discarded the moisture source in any block that also held solid, so water in a rock basin humidified nothing at all (0.000 against 2.310 for the identical 240-cell body shifted three rows). Moisture now reads through a blocked neighbour that is itself a source.
  *Re-test when:* The exception is moisture-only: heat and light must keep the strict wall rule because the sealed-room guards depend on it — do not extend the moisture exception to them.
- **README.md 'The coarse field grid' — 'LIGHT_DECAY retuned from 0.85 to 0.997' paragraph** - LIGHT_DECAY at 0.85: the steep decay effectively required every plant to sit within a couple of field rows (~20 world cells) of open sky once Germinate made light-gated growth real; retuned to 0.997 (~75 cells of useful depth) on owner request for real outdoor sunlight depth.
  *Re-test when:* Owner requirement of realistic light depth. Recorded cost of the new value: convergence to a static sky amplitude takes roughly 100x longer, so the field spends more frames awake near day/night peaks — re-open only if that cost becomes a problem.
- **README.md 'The coarse field grid' — 'Wall boundary conditions went through three attempts' paragraph (code: src/sim/field.rs)** - First wall boundary condition: zero a cell's whole velocity whenever it touched any blocked neighbour, regardless of direction. In a small sealed room almost every interior cell borders a wall, so it bled energy out of sealed rooms faster than open ground — measured backwards: a sealed room retained less pressure than open air.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while walls are handled per field cell in a continuum velocity field; the textbook no-penetration condition (zero only the into-wall component) is what replaced it.
- **README.md 'The coarse field grid' — 'Wall boundary conditions went through three attempts' paragraph (code: src/sim/field.rs)** - Second wall boundary condition: reflect the blocked velocity component (flip its sign to bounce, conserving kinetic energy). Made the sealed-room pressure measurement worse than attempt one — most likely energy pooling at wall-adjacent cells and repeatedly hitting the pressure/temperature clamps, which are themselves lossy. Reflection is a billiard-ball model for discrete particles, not a continuum field.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the field carries lossy pressure/temperature clamps and models a continuum; do not retry bounce for the field grid.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13d Corrections (decay/interval)** - DECAY_RHO in the literature band with PHEROMONE_INTERVAL=4 kills trails before ants can use them: the forced strict-decrease LUT caps unreinforced trail life at 255 passes ≈ ~1,000 frames against a ~2,200-frame round trip. Retuned to interval 12, rho 0.03 — rho wants to sit below the literature band, and the interval is the load-bearing knob.
  *Re-test when:* Tied to the u8 LUT's forced-decrease floor and current round-trip distances.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §13d Corrections to the design's own numbers (DIFFUSE)** - DIFFUSE = 0.1 for the u8 pheromone plane does essentially nothing — the blended value rounds to zero before propagating two cells. 0.25 was set from a sweep of tracking performance.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for a u8 plane with a 3×3 mean kernel; a wider-precision plane would need re-sweeping.
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §5b The pass (build_decay_lut) / §10 P-13** - Plain multiplicative decay on a u8 plane fix-points above zero (rounding), leaving permanent ghost trails — the same quantization failure the canopy-density 4-bit packing already demonstrated in this codebase. Decay must go through a LUT with a forced strict-decrease step, asserted at startup.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any quantized (u8/4-bit) decaying channel.
- **Reports/emergent-world-architecture.md §4 'Settled: field channel' (moisture)** - Per-cell moisture on Cell was rejected (settled with the owner) in favour of a field channel: it would cost 2 bytes on the size-asserted Cell, collide with an aux slot already carrying four meanings, and need its own CA-grid diffusion pass, while the thing organisms navigate is the gradient — a field-scale property that diffuses free on step_diffusion.
  *Re-test when:* Visibly-wet sand rendering is recorded as a separable per-cell concern that can be added later without disturbing the channel.
- **Reports/emergent-world-architecture.md §6b Per-channel resolution + creature-direction.md §2a/§13a** - Pheromone as a sixth FieldCell channel at FIELD_SCALE=8 was rejected: a one-cell trail smears across an 8-cell block, two trails 4 cells apart are indistinguishable, and bilinear interpolation of a smear is a smooth smear. Pheromone shipped as its own CA-resolution double-buffered plane, measured at 0.0014 ms settled with 0 tiles processed.
  *Re-test when:* A per-channel field-resolution split (pheromone at FIELD_SCALE=1) was the alternative deliberately not built; revisit only if the standalone plane becomes the M10 streaming problem its own comment predicts (per-chunk migration is 'mechanical').
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md §5 "Two of the three things an explosion writes are inert" (fire.rs::diffuse_heat note)** - Having fire's cell heat diffusion read the temperature field was removed for a measured 16 ms → 64 ms regression — which is why the field temperature spike ignites nothing and glows nowhere (only `cell.temperature()` is read by ignition and the render glow).
  *Re-test when:* The 4x measured regression is the condition; the recorded workaround is bounded per-blast direct cell-temperature writes (`Blast::scorch`) rather than a per-frame field read.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md round 4 §"Free particles feel wind" (particle::WIND_DRAG)** - Wind as an added force on particles was rejected from measured numbers: field velocity peaks near 86 while `MAX_SPEED_PER_AXIS` clamps particles to 8, so any formula adding a fraction of raw wind swamps `debris_velocity`'s launch direction and, once the shock reflects, drives debris back into its own crater. What shipped is relative-velocity drag *toward* the wind, which is self-limiting — fast debris moving with the blast feels almost nothing.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while field and particle velocity scales differ by an order of magnitude; `debris_is_thrown_away_from_the_epicentre_not_toward_it` exists to catch the failure.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md round 4 §"Gas drifts on wind" (WIND_BIAS_FULL_SPEED)** - `WIND_BIAS_FULL_SPEED = 0.35`, set by eye, saturated permanently: probing real field velocity around a blast gives a peak near 86, falling through ~20 by frame 24, sitting at 1–3 for another forty frames, under 1 by frame 80. The constant was re-set from that measured velocity profile, not from eye.
  *Re-test when:* The constant must track the measured field-velocity scale; re-derive if blast strength or field velocity scaling changes.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md round 4 §"Tried and reverted: ambient wind as a real field forcing term"** - Ambient wind as a per-cell vx forcing term on the field — the better design — was tried and reverted: a uniform velocity in a bounded world hits walls, creating divergence, then pressure, then more velocity, so `is_converged` never returns true and field sleep (issue #4) is destroyed. Measured: settled-field frame cost 0.0002 ms → 3.55 ms, permanently, on every scene, with six field tests failing. `PREVAILING_DRIFT` (a constant read by the gas rule alone, costing nothing) shipped instead as the acknowledged lesser thing.
  *Re-test when:* Explicit recorded prerequisite for retry: make the solver settle with a steady forcing term present. Re-attempting the same nudge rediscovers the same 3.55 ms.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md Phase 0 status (0f) — with the failure inventory in tree-diagnosis-review.md §4 and doc-rot notes in tree-architecture-variety-review-verification.md §3.2** - Diffusion-carried sky light with LIGHT_DECAY = 0.9997 (non-attenuating air, justified by a 25x outcome table): light brightened with height, creating a false climb incentive that pinned every scene against the world top; Germinate's 0.1 gate became unreachable anywhere with sky access; near-surface light inverted so phototropism pointed down for ~45% of each cycle; caves 150 cells deep lit above the germination gate; photosynthesis income pinned mature cells at the cap. Superseded by field.rs::apply_sky as a per-column downward cast with LIGHT_DECAY cut to 0.95 (diffusion carries only lateral bleed).
  *Re-test when:* field.rs's ~90-line comment still argues for 0.9997 — re-deriving from that doc re-derives the wrong constant. The 25x table was evidence for reach under the old model only; under the column cast, reach and amplitude are decoupled.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-implementation-plan.md Phase 0, item 0f discussion** - Justifying or A/B-ing light-model changes on a depth profile: 'the depth-profile argument has been wrong twice.' Light changes must be A/B'd on tree outcomes.
  *Re-test when:* Standing method rule for the light channel; no recorded condition under which a depth profile suffices.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-variety-review-verification.md §2.1 (history recorded in the same section)** - The original binary whole-block `blocked` flag as the shade signal: any single plant cell made an entire 8x8 field block opaque, which is the artifact the graded occupancy channel was built to cure. Do not reintroduce a binary transmission signal in either axis.
  *Re-test when:* Pairing graded rules with a binary light signal is the repo's recorded failure mode; only graded per-column transmission avoids it.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-variety-review-verification.md §2.1 'Its occupancy fix overshoots and reintroduces binary shade, rotated 90°'** - The variety review's proposed occupancy fix — count columns hit instead of cells filled — was rejected: it makes any 1-cell-deep structure (plate, diagonal twig, sparse leaf spray) as opaque as solid rock, restoring the 'one twig makes a whole block opaque' disease in the horizontal direction. The consistent fix is per-column Beer-Lambert (T = 0.2^(d/8)) on the existing scan.
  *Re-test when:* The underlying diagnosis (orientation-blind occupancy under-charges horizontal plates by 4.5x) stands; only the cols-hit formula is dead. The Beer-Lambert prediction still had to be A/B'd on tree outcomes.
- **Reports/tree-architecture-variety-review-verification.md §2.4 'Its §1e dusk-lag claim is derived from a stale doc'** - Attributing the day/night sampling problem (0e) to deep-field relaxation lag ('field relaxes over ~3,300 steps, night reads brighter than the amplitude') was rejected: that figure belongs to LIGHT_DECAY = 0.9997 and the constant in the file is 0.95, under which the field tracks dusk in ~60 frames (~1.7% of the cycle). The 0e problem is purely the 20:1 oscillator against instantaneous gates.
  *Re-test when:* Held at LIGHT_DECAY = 0.95 under the column-cast model; re-derive if the constant or model changes — from the code, not the stale comment.
- **Reports/tree-shape-problem-statement.md §6 'What is already known to work, and should not be re-litigated'** - The original LIGHT_DECAY setting attenuated light through empty air, capping tree height at ~21 rows. Corrected; do not reintroduce air attenuation.
  *Re-test when:* Held under the diffusion light model; the successor 0.9997 value had its own recorded failures (see the diffusion-sky entry) and the current model is a per-column cast with LIGHT_DECAY 0.95 carrying only lateral bleed.
- **Reports/weather-handoff.md 'Bugs this push introduced and then fixed' (the advection clamp)** - Clamping advection back-trace to one field cell (ADVECTION_MAX_TILES) on the claim it only cost very-fast-flow accuracy: never measured, and wrong — open-ground pressure went from dispersing to 2.9 to freezing at 2177.8, 750x too concentrated, forever. Re-sized from measurement at four tiles, byte-identical to the pre-sleeping baseline.
  *Re-test when:* An overlong back-trace is safe (advection only reads, writing nothing outside its tile), so the clamp costs accuracy never correctness — any future tightening must be measured against dispersal.
- **src/sim/field.rs fn step, next-map construction ('Merging solved tiles into the live map instead was tried and reverted')** - Merging solved tiles into the live map (to remove the clone per sleeping tile) was tried and reverted: it forced apply_sky to run after convergence was judged, so its light writes landed in old but never in next, every touched tile read as changed forever, nothing converged, and a four-cell impulse went from 5.2 ms back to 47 ms at 2048x1280. Whatever writes a channel must write it before the comparison that decides whether the tile is done.
  *Re-test when:* Removing the clone is legitimate but requires apply_sky to write into the solved subset while walking the full column — a second mechanism, not a reordering.
- **src/sim/field.rs occluder-light test ('The invariant here was deliberately reversed')** - Solid field blocks used to carry no light of their own — which looked obviously right and was backwards: the light arriving at an occluder is what it intercepts, and a leaf is an occluder. Under the old rule 88% of canopy-interior leaves measured under 0.05 light, shade-driven abscission had no threshold between pruning nothing and defoliating the world, and light_weight was inert across 1,024 genomes because no gradient survived to steer by. Occluders now hold the light arriving at them.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while leaves are occluders reading their own block's light; re-derive if leaf light-sampling moves off the occluder cell.
- **src/sim/field.rs step_advection, back-trace clamp comment** - Clamping the semi-Lagrangian back-trace to FIELD_SCALE (one field cell) was a physics change dressed up as a safety margin — the old comment's claimed cost ('very fast flow transports one cell per step instead of many') was never measured and was wrong: an impulse in open ground stopped dispersing almost entirely, total |pressure| after 200 steps going from 2.9 to 2177.8. The sealed-room test caught it and was misread as pre-existing for most of a session (the suspect was the sleeping that landed with it; a probe showing all 16 chunks solving every step pointed here). The clamp is one tile — exactly the halo the awake set provides.
  *Re-test when:* The clamp size is set by, and only by, the halo width; re-derive it if the awake-set ring changes.
- **src/sim/field.rs step_velocity, no-penetration comment (first recorded thing)** - The original wall handling zeroed whichever velocity axis merely touched a blocked neighbour regardless of flow direction. In a small sealed room almost every interior cell borders a wall, so velocity was force-zeroed nearly everywhere every step, bleeding energy out of sealed rooms faster than open ground — measured exactly backwards: a sealed room retained less total pressure than open terrain. Only the component actually flowing into the wall is stopped now.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while rooms are small relative to FIELD_SCALE; any direction-blind wall damping re-creates it.
- **src/sim/field.rs step_velocity, no-penetration comment (second recorded thing)** - Reflecting velocity at walls (flipping the sign to bounce, conserving the component's energy) was tried as the fix for the sealed-room leak and made the same measurement worse (0.99 against the zeroing bug's 44) — energy pooling at wall-adjacent cells and repeatedly hitting the lossy pressure/temperature clamps. Reflection is a billiard-ball model for particles; the boundary condition for a continuum velocity field is no-penetration: the normal component goes to zero, nothing bounces.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the field has lossy clamps and is a continuum solver; do not reintroduce bounce.
- **src/sim/field.rs test advection_weight_varies_smoothly_across_a_field_cells_full_width** - sample_bilinear's interpolation weight was computed as fx - fx.floor(), which spans 0..1 across a single world unit rather than across the FIELD_SCALE-wide field cell — the weight swung through its whole range in the first world-cell and sat pinned at an extreme for the rest, reading backwards for small offsets. The weight must be normalized over the field cell's full width.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while field cells span FIELD_SCALE world units; any fractional-part weight on world coordinates repeats it.
- **src/sim/field.rs test open_air_is_equally_lit_at_any_depth_and_a_roof_still_shades (doc)** - Seeding light on the topmost chunk's top row and carrying it down by diffusion made illumination a function of distance from the world's top edge: plants got an unbounded reward for climbing and every scene's trees pinned against the world boundary (apply_sky's doc holds the four symptoms and measurements). Replaced by the invariant that clear air does not attenuate sunlight; only occluders do.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while plants make decisions off the light channel; any depth-attenuating open-air light re-creates the climbing reward.
- **src/sim/field.rs test pressure_stops_at_a_wall_the_same_frame_it_appears** - step_pressure originally checked blocked status against the previous field step's occupancy (old) instead of the just-rebuilt next, so a solid placed mid-run took one extra field step to start blocking pressure. Separately recorded: an earlier version of this guard built up velocity for 20 steps before placing the wall, a confound that could fail the assertion for unrelated reasons — the impulse must be added the same step blocking should take effect.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while occupancy is rebuilt per step; blocking must read the current rebuild, and its guard must not pre-build motion.
- **src/sim/plant.rs fn ambient_light_above doc and test section 'A plant reads real light at its own position'** - Sampling light one field block up was a workaround for rebuild_blocked marking a whole block opaque the instant any Solid/Plant cell sat in it (apply_sky skipped opaque blocks, so a plant reading its own position got a permanent 0.0 — a seed in open sky that could never germinate). apply_sky now writes the light arriving at a block, occupied or not, so the offset's premise is gone and the offset itself became a bug. Do not reintroduce either half.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while apply_sky writes arriving light at occupied blocks; the guard tests fail under the old rule without the offset and pass under the new one without it.
- **src/sim/surface.rs CellSurface::field_wind_at doc** - A per-visited-cell field read through World::field_at — a HashMap lookup per call — in fire::diffuse_heat took the worst frame from ~16 ms to ~64 ms and was removed. Reading from the worker's own field tile by direct array index is fundamentally cheaper, measured indistinguishable even at 56,640 gas cells (6.39 vs 6.46 ms means).
  *Re-test when:* Holds for reads that must go through the shared World's hash map from a hot per-cell rule; ChunkView own-tile reads are exempt (measured).

## weather  (19 entries)

- **src/sim/clock.rs Clock::weather_slowdown, and src/sim/weather.rs step()** - Coupling the weather clock to the day clock, so that lengthening the day lengthened the weather epoch with it. Built first, on a sound argument: `WEATHER_EPOCH_FRAMES` is *defined* as `DAY_NIGHT_PERIOD_FRAMES * 2`, so a world with an eight-minute day would otherwise see the sky change its mind eight times between one noon and the next. Withdrawn by owner preference rather than by measurement — the day and the weather are two things a player wants to set separately, and a knob that silently moves another knob is not a separate knob. What survives is that the constant is two *baseline* days: a statement about where the number came from, not an invariant. Note the shipped `assets/clock.ron` may still pair the two by *default* while leaving them independent as a *mechanism*; that is not a re-coupling.
  *Re-test when:* Depends entirely on the owner's preference for independent knobs, not on any measurement — re-open only if that preference changes. Also recorded: a per-frame rain-budget divisor was drafted alongside it as "required" and is separately wrong (see the entry below).
- **src/sim/clock.rs Clock::weather_slowdown (rain budget)** - Dividing `MAX_COLUMNS_PER_FRAME` by the weather slowdown, to stop a slowed front depositing N times more water over its longer life. Wrong in the opposite direction: rain spends per real frame and evaporation refills the atmospheric bank per real frame, so a divisor returns 1/N the water against unchanged evaporation and the world dries out — the bank pins at 1.0 and standing water drains. Left alone, the precipitating *fraction* of real frames is unchanged and the ledger balances itself; a front simply lasts longer and drops more over its own life, which is what slower weather means. Also recorded: the obvious guard ("total water per epoch is flat across the knob") passes for the broken version, because an epoch is N times longer too — `0.14 · N·W · C/N` is flat either way.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while rain is spent and evaporation credited per real frame. If deposition ever moves onto the weather clock itself, both sides of the ledger move and this needs re-deriving from scratch.

- **README.md 'The coarse field grid' — 'Standing water dries up' paragraph (code: src/sim/evaporation.rs)** - Evaporation driven from the CA sweep dried a lake faster than a puddle (7% against 1.7%), because a lake stays awake settling longer while still water is exactly the state the sweep stops visiting. Moved to the active-site scheduler; the sweep now only schedules.
  *Re-test when:* Holds as long as settled chunks are not swept — i.e. permanently under the dirty-rect architecture.
- **Reports/weather-handoff.md §1 'A gale erases every lake in the world'** - Rescuing lakes from gale-drying using any function of humidity: impossible, because a gale mixes the atmosphere so the air over a puddle and over a lake read identically (2.31 to 0.23 within ten frames; an 800-cell lake behaves exactly like a 240-cell one). Hence the separate evaporation::shelter factor read off the never-advected moisture-source stencil.
  *Re-test when:* Stated in the file: if the gust field is ever retuned so a windy epoch stops scouring the humid layer, the shelter term can go and the humidity deficit alone does the whole job again — it is a separate factor precisely so that it can.
- **Reports/weather-handoff.md §1 'Puddle evaporation — landed'** - Gating the evaporation sweep hook on (x + y) % INTERVAL == frame % INTERVAL to save reads silently turned the whole mechanic off: a chunk is swept only briefly before sleeping, so a gate open one frame in sixty misses essentially every visit. The stagger survives as an offset on the first next_frame instead — nothing is skipped.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for any modulo gate on a sweep hook: the sweep's visits are the scarce resource, and anything discarding one discards the only chance there was.
- **Reports/weather-handoff.md §1 'Puddle evaporation — landed' (dedup note)** - An earlier evaporation version was reverted because it dried a lake faster than a puddle: duplicate scheduled sites accumulated in proportion to how long a body stays awake settling. The dedup in World::pending_evaporation is load-bearing for the rate, not the frame cost — removing it brings the failure back by a different route.
  *Re-test when:* Held while sites are enqueued per failed-move; do not remove the dedup without re-measuring lake-vs-puddle drying order.
- **Reports/weather-handoff.md 'Bugs this push introduced and then fixed' (the gust monopole)** - A gust as a lone positive pressure impulse injects net pressure into a closed world with nowhere to go, so surrounding tiles never reconverge. A gust must remain a dipole (high behind, low ahead, summing to zero); do not make it a monopole again.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional while the world is closed and pressure conserves.
- **Reports/weather-handoff.md 'Bugs this push introduced and then fixed' (the gust monopole)** - A steady wind forcing term was tried and reverted: measured at a permanent 3.55 ms/frame on every scene, and it is the same non-reconvergence failure as the monopole by another route. Do not reintroduce a steady forcing term.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional at that cost profile; the gust field's own 26-frame timer already drifts toward this shape and is flagged as the thing to fix instead.
- **src/sim/evaporation.rs fn shelter (doc) and module doc** - Judging shelter from the humidity deficit alone fails on a windy world: a gale mixes the atmosphere so the air over a puddle and over a lake read identically, and no reading of the advected humidity channel can tell them apart. The second factor must come from field::moisture_source_at, which is rebuilt from the CA grid and never advected; the gale guard fails outright if shelter is made a function of advected humidity.
  *Re-test when:* Explicitly recorded: if the gust field is ever retuned so a windy epoch stops scouring the humid layer, the shelter term can go and the humidity deficit alone will do the whole job again.
- **src/sim/evaporation_tests.rs module doc ('One body per world')** - The first paired puddle-vs-lake scene put both bodies in one world 200 cells apart, and the lake humidified the puddle: air over the puddle read 1.82 with the lake present against 1.45 alone (lake's own asymptote 2.31), slowing the puddle by the same margin. Rejected for one body per world — weather is a pure function of (seed, frame), so two worlds stepped identically get identical weather and the pairing survives the separation.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while humidity advects across a world; re-testable only with genuinely isolated atmospheres.
- **src/sim/evaporation_tests.rs scene constants comment ('Sized for cost, then re-derived from measurement')** - The original guard scenes ran six tests in 52 seconds — 17% of the whole suite — because sky-lit field tiles never sleep, so cost is roughly world width times frames. The world was quartered and bodies halved, with every bar re-measured rather than scaled. Do not re-grow these scenes without re-measuring what it buys.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while sky-lit field tiles never sleep; if field sleeping ever covers the sunlit surface, the cost model changes.
- **src/sim/update.rs const PREVAILING_DRIFT (magnitude note)** - PREVAILING_DRIFT = 0.35 (the first value tried) produced a downwind preference of 0.53 against a fair 0.5 and was invisible; 2.0 gives a clear lean while staying far below an explosion's ~86 transient so blasts still dominate locally.
  *Re-test when:* Re-derive if WIND_BIAS_FULL_SPEED's measured scale changes.
- **src/sim/update.rs const PREVAILING_DRIFT doc (field-forcing paragraph)** - A real wind forcing term in field::step was built, measured, and reverted: uniform velocity in a bounded world hits walls, creates divergence/pressure/velocity, so the solve never converges — settled-field frame cost went from 0.0002 ms to 3.55 ms permanently on every scene, and six field tests failed. Wind is instead a constant only the gas rule reads.
  *Re-test when:* Retry only after the field solver can settle with a steady forcing term present; do not re-attempt the same nudge.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn update_cell (Liquid arm) and src/sim/world.rs field pending_evaporation** - Doing evaporation inline in the CA sweep was built and reverted: a settled chunk is not swept, so still water — the one state the mechanic is about — is never visited again; and raw site requests scaled with how long a body stays awake settling, so a lake evaporated faster than a puddle (Reports/weather-handoff.md §1). Evaporation now only hands settled surface cells to the active-site scheduler, deduped.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while settled chunks are skipped by the sweep and request volume is proportional to settling time.
- **src/sim/update.rs fn update_gas and test a_rising_plume_leans_downwind** - The first wind-bias version weighted only the horizontal choice while the straight-up move was tried first — a complete no-op for a plume in open air (there is always an empty cell above), only affecting gas trapped under a ceiling. Bias-on and bias-off contact sheets were identical; the fix is strong wind taking the downwind diagonal before rising, verified by plume centroid (+19.4 vs -3.2 cells).
  *Re-test when:* Permanent: any preference placed downstream of an unconditional vertical move is dead in open air.
- **src/sim/weather.rs const MOISTURE_PER_DROP** - The first value (0.10) was chosen as if drops accumulated; they do not — they reach a balance against continuous evaporation, and that balance measured 0.031 of saturation across a whole 128-wide world: real, conserved, and completely invisible. Retuned to 0.45 by judging the steady state under continuous rain, not the size of one write.
  *Re-test when:* Re-derive if the moisture evaporation/decay model changes, since the constant is sized against that balance.
- **src/sim/weather.rs fn gust (dipole comment)** - Delivering a gust as a single positive pressure impulse injects net pressure into a closed world with nowhere to go: measured, a calm world left 0 field tiles unconverged and a gusty one left 4, permanently — the reverted steady wind's failure by another route. A high/low dipole summing to zero is required.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the world is closed with no pressure sink; any impulse with nonzero net pressure re-creates it.
- **src/sim/weather.rs fn gust (doc: 'The mistake this is shaped around')** - A steady global wind term was built, measured, and reverted: a uniform velocity in a bounded world pushes air into the walls, creating divergence, pressure, and more velocity, so field::is_converged never returns true again — settled-field cost went from 0.0002 ms to a permanent 3.55 ms on every scene and six field tests failed. Recorded as do-not-retry; gusts (bounded impulses that disperse) are the replacement, guarded by the gust-settling test.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the world is closed and field sleeping is convergence-based; a gust fired every frame is the same term wearing a different hat.
- **src/sim/weather.rs fn strike (checked_sub comment)** - Computing the previous strike window with wrapping_sub wrapped to u64::MAX inside the very first window, overflowing w * STRIKE_WINDOW and panicking in the opening seconds of any world whose u64::MAX window passed the roll. It hid because release builds do not check overflow — every cargo test --release was green. Use checked_sub, and run plain cargo test on boundary arithmetic.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while window arithmetic can run at frame ~0; do not switch back to wrapping_sub.
- **src/sim/weather.rs tests, guard doc above fn a_gust_disperses** - Measuring gust dispersal through summed pressure was tried three times and each measured something else (an unsupported slab falling ~16000 in both windy and calm runs, the world's construction transient, and a background relaxation an order of magnitude larger than the gust). Rejected for counting unconverged field tiles, the quantity field::step actually branches on.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while background pressure noise exists; summed pressure cannot distinguish 'never dispersed' from 'dispersed slowly' under it.

## rendering  (39 entries)

- **`examples/subpixel.rs` `posterize` — quantising RGB channels independently to flatten a reconstructed fill** - Asked for as "even flatter, there are still 3dish looking shading" and this is the obvious implementation; it wrecks hue. At five steps per channel `wood`'s browns went **maroon** and the darker tones collapsed to flat black, because a uniform ladder in RGB does not respect where a palette's colours actually sit — the three channels round independently and the ratios between them, which is what hue *is*, do not survive. Replaced by snapping to the nearest colour that actually occurs among the contributing cells, which can only emit a colour the engine already drew.
  *Re-test when:* Never in RGB. A ladder in a perceptual or hue-preserving space is a different mechanism and untested here; snapping to the source palette is cheaper than either and exact.

- **`examples/subpixel.rs` — thresholding a kernel field at a *constant* level, as the finished silhouette** - Rejected on sight by the owner: *"the smooth circular shape/edges look fake"*. It is an artifact of the method rather than a tuning error, and it cannot be tuned out — a sum of radial kernels cut at one constant level can only ever produce circular arcs and smooth joins between them, so an isolated cell is a disc and a clump is a run of fused discs. Soap bubbles. Perturbing the threshold with coherent value noise (keyed to **world** position, or it crawls when the camera moves) makes the same field cut a ragged outline at no extra kernel work, which is what foliage wants: a leaf edge is serrated and a canopy edge is a thousand leaf tips.
  *Re-test when:* A constant level is fine for anything genuinely smooth-edged — a liquid surface, a bubble. It is wrong for anything grown.

- **`examples/subpixel.rs` `field` — normalising the reconstruction gradient into a unit surface normal** - The textbook move, and it quilts every interior into visible squares. A kernel field over cell occupancy gives a free surface normal as `-grad(cov)`, and normalising it to unit length is what any lighting code does — but deep inside a mass the contributions from the surrounding cells cancel, so `grad` there is lattice-scale floating-point residue, and dividing it by its own tiny length promotes that residue to a full-strength normal. The raw, un-normalised gradient is already the right shape: large at the rim where the surface genuinely faces somewhere, vanishing in the interior where it genuinely does not. Dot it with the light direction and clamp.
  *Re-test when:* Never for an interior. A normalised normal is fine if the evaluation is restricted to a band around the isosurface, which is a different mechanism (and costs the band test).

- **`examples/subpixel.rs` `partition_table` — reading a raw kernel sum as a thickness** - The second half of the same quilting, from an unrelated cause, which is why it survived fixing the first. A kernel sum over a *regular* lattice ripples at the lattice period unless the kernel is far wider than the spacing, so deep inside a solid mass — where the answer should be a flat "completely full" — the sum oscillates between cell centres and cell corners, and reading it as an ambient-occlusion depth amplifies that ripple straight into a grid of squares. Divide by what the kernel sums to when every cell in range is occupied: the denominator carries the same ripple so it cancels, and it depends only on where the pixel sits inside its cell, so it is a `scale x scale` table computed once rather than per-pixel work.
  *Re-test when:* Only if the kernel radius grows past about 2 cells, where the ripple is small on its own — but the normalisation is nearly free, so there is no reason to drop it.

- **`examples/subpixel.rs` `arm=all` with colour blending — putting rock and soil through the plant reconstruction** - Smoothing terrain the same way as foliage destroys the per-cell shade grain, and the grain is the entire reason soil reads as soil rather than as a flat brown field. Measured by eye at 4x on the `PlantScene` bed. The split that works is by *class*, not by strength: reconstruct the **silhouette** for everything, blend the **interior colour** only for thin structures (branches, roots, foliage) and leave a mass's interior exactly as the 1:1 renderer drew it.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while materials carry a deliberate per-cell palette spread. A material whose interior is meant to be flat has nothing to lose and can blend.

- **A pixel-art upscaler (hqx / xBR / EPX) over the finished frame, as the answer to "the plants look blocky"** - The obvious reading of "upsample it", and it is backwards here. Those filters infer shape from **colour** — which neighbouring pixels happen to match — and this world is their worst case: every material carries a deliberate per-cell shade jitter, so adjacent pixels of one material rarely match and the filter has nothing to latch onto. It would smear the grain, which is the thing that works, and leave the one-cell plant strands alone, which is the thing that does not. Reconstructing from **what the cell is** (material, organism cell type) is information a post-hoc filter cannot recover and the renderer already holds.
  *Re-test when:* Never while the palettes carry grain. `Reports/subpixel-rendering-2026-08-29.md` §4.

- **Reports/grassfire-and-the-desert-2026-08-23.md §6 (code: assets/materials/flame.ron `glow`)** - Giving the new `flame` material `glow: 0.9` so fire is a real light source — which is exactly what `Material::glow` is for, and which a night fire wants — made the fire look *worse*. `render.rs`'s glow path is built for a crystal geode in a dark cave: it lifts a lit cell multiplicatively (`rgb * (1 + glow * GLOW_SOLID_LIFT)`, taking smoke's grey 84 to 160) and blends the surrounding *air* toward `GLOW_AIR_TINT` (226, 222, 208), near white. A daylight grassfire drew as bright specks on a bleached sky and read as a snow flurry. Cost argument too: `near_glow` splats a disc of radius 14 per glowing *cell*, and a front carries hundreds where a geode has a static lining. Left at 0.0.
  *Re-test when:* The glow path learns about daylight and about sources that move — i.e. when `glow_tiles`/`GLOW_AIR_TINT` stop assuming a dark cavity and a stationary emitter.
- **Reports/grassfire-and-the-desert-2026-08-23.md §6 (code: src/render.rs `HEAT_GLOW_RANGE`)** - Widening the heat ramp from 400 to 1000 on the theory that fire has no colour gradient because everything burning saturates it (grass burns at 520C, flame at 780C, the ramp tops out 400C above ambient). The diagnosis is right and this is the wrong direction: rendered, the fire came out a murky **olive**, because at a low heat ratio the tint sits near `FIRE_TINT_LOW` and is blended over the fuel's own green rather than replacing it. Reverted.
  *Re-test when:* Only alongside a change to how the tint composites (replace rather than blend at high `t`); widening the range alone always mixes fuel colour into the flame.

- **PLAN.md Progress log, M6 entry; expanded in the overnight-run §11 dirty-rect-render entry ('Reframed to the CPU-side cost...')** - The originally planned GPU dirty-region texture upload is blocked by pixels 0.17.2's own architecture (it uploads the full texture regardless), and forking the dependency was rejected: the saved copy is ~655KB/frame (~39MB/s), nowhere near a bottleneck. The real win was the CPU-side per-chunk skip in Renderer::draw (6.6ms → 0.0ms worst frame on a settled scene).
  *Re-test when:* Tied to pixels 0.17.2's architecture; a different presentation crate or a version exposing partial uploads would reopen the question.
- **README.md — 'Playtest feedback drove two more changes' paragraph (code: src/render.rs fire tint)** - Rendering fire as one flat tint: a burning cell flashed orange for a frame and reverted, with no real flame look. Replaced with position-plus-coarse-time-bucket flicker (rng::jitter3) and a heat_ratio hue ramp FIRE_TINT_LOW to FIRE_TINT_HIGH so intensity changes colour, not just blend strength.
  *Re-test when:* Playtest-driven; unknown further condition (real emissive/bloom remains M6's job).
- **README.md 'M14 status' — 'Screenshotting the live app doesn't work on this machine' paragraph** - OS-level screen capture of the app: BitBlt/CopyFromScreen and PrintWindow(PW_RENDERFULLCONTENT) both return solid black for the DXGI/wgpu swapchain client area (chrome captures fine). Workaround is the in-app framebuffer dump (PIXEL_PHYSICS_SCREENSHOT_AFTER_FRAMES, save_framebuffer_png in src/main.rs).
  *Re-test when:* The Status section's known-limitations list explicitly says this is worth re-checking as possibly machine-specific before assuming every future visual milestone needs the framebuffer dump.
- **README.md 'Rendering performance — overnight run, section 11'** - The originally-planned GPU dirty-region texture-upload route for the render skip is blocked by the pixels crate's own architecture: render/render_with always re-upload the whole frame internally, with no accessor for the underlying surface to drive an alternative path. The fix landed CPU-side (skip recomputing pixel colour for unchanged chunks: 6.6 ms to 0.0 ms worst frame settled), where the real cost already was.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the renderer is built on the pixels crate; a renderer swap re-opens the GPU route.
- **README.md 'UI improvements — overnight run, section 9' — hud.rs paragraph** - Hand-authoring the plan's originally-sketched full ASCII 0x20-0x7E bitmap font (95 glyphs): with no reference font to check against, it risks silently shipping wrong bitmap data for characters nothing exercises before they are seen. Scoped down to space, A-Z, 0-9 and small punctuation, with HUD text upper-cased — and even the small set shipped one invisible-glyph gap ([ and ]) caught only by rendering to PNG.
  *Re-test when:* Re-open only with a verification method per glyph (the module's every-claimed-character-lights-a-pixel test is the existing bar).
- **Reports/creature-direction.md §5d Overlays and the probe** - A magnitude-scaled blend of a debug channel into the cell's own colour was tried once and read as blank (red ramp on brown wood moved one byte 139→155), nearly sending a fix at working code. Overlays must be full-replace on a fixed dark→bright ramp.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional; also house law in CLAUDE.md.
- **Reports/destruction-plan.md "What we are explicitly not doing" (live per-cell strain tint)** - A continuous live per-cell strain tint was evaluated and rejected: no per-cell storage exists (`Cell::flags` 8/8 full, `aux` taken), and re-deriving stress at render time defeats the dirty-rect skip on exactly the settled worlds where that skip pays (the animated-grain lesson). The on-demand overlay (A2, on N) is the surviving version — costs the skip only while enabled.
  *Re-test when:* Would need both a free per-cell storage channel and a render path that does not defeat the settled-world skip.
- **Reports/explosion-mechanics-diagnosis.md §1 "The detonation frame is very nearly a no-op"** - Changed-pixel *count* as the detonation-visibility metric was tried and rejected live: it counted the position-keyed grain jitter difference between `cell_colour` and `draw_particles` and reported 104% of the footprint changed — real numbers meaning nothing. Mean absolute RGB delta is the usable metric (it showed the crater changes by 5%).
  *Re-test when:* Any pixel-difference count over grain-jittered rendering hits this; the CLAUDE.md metric-trap pattern observed directly.
- **Reports/load-model-fit-review.md §6.4 'Give the player strain feedback before failure'** - A live per-cell strain tint was evaluated and rejected: there is no per-cell storage for it (Cell::flags is 8/8 and aux is taken), and re-deriving stress at render time defeats the dirty-rect skip on exactly the settled worlds where that skip pays — the animated-grain lesson again. The recorded cheap routes are the hover inspector and crack propagation along the stress ratio.
  *Re-test when:* Held while Cell has no spare storage and dirty-rect skipping is the settled-world optimisation; new storage or a skip-preserving derivation would reopen it.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §4b 'A cell alone in the air drops its column's skyline' (closed)** - Every version of inferring the sky surface from world geometry failed: the last one (topmost cell plus repair within six columns) gave a floating grain 20 rows of cave, treated a 51-wide plank identically, and opened daylight 35 rows into a mountain at a 13-wide shaft. No reach setting fixes it — hill, shaft, roof and grain are the same arrangement of cells, and 'I dug this' versus 'this is a hill' is history, not geometry.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for inference; the fix stores history (World::sky_surface recorded once at first frame). Revisit only by storing more history, never by inferring. **Revisited exactly that way and it worked** (Reports/dark-bands-diagnosis.md): the per-column form still could not tell a cave roof from a cliff brow, because that is a *column* question asked of a per-cell fact, so the same answer is now stored per cell (World::freeze_underground_map, one bit, seeded by a genesis flood fill). No inference, no threshold, and every property the column form was chosen for survives. The remaining case — a wide open pit you dug still reading as cave — is not reachable by any better boolean and needs light propagation instead (Reports/prior-art-underground-lighting.md). **Propagation shipped and only half-answered it**: seeded spread measures distance from a lit cell, which cannot tell a hole from a hollow, so a broken-open hilltop still drew black on a 2026-08-29 playtest. Closed by adding a sky-*visibility* term beside it (render.rs SKY_VIEW_RAYS, Reports/sky-light-design.md) — still not a boolean, and still not a threshold on width.
- **Reports/underground-definition.md §"The options weighed" (option A)** - Patching the inference with the missing spike-mirror (ignore a column standing much higher than both neighbours) was weighed and rejected: it fixes the cheap half (tree shadow, floating pixel) while leaving the shaft-width threshold exactly where it is and adding a second threshold for plank width — the half mining will hit stays broken.
  *Re-test when:* Same condition as the inference dead end itself; any threshold-bearing repair rule breaks precisely while a shaft is being widened.
- **Reports/underground-definition.md §"What it does" (ground exclusions)** - Counting `Liquid` tops (or `Plant` canopy) as ground for `sky_surface` was rejected: freezing frame one's waterline leaves a false-cave band hanging above a draining lake and creeping down as it falls — caught in a `viewshot mine=1` render where mining into a lake drained it — and counting a canopy would bake the black-rectangle-under-a-tree bug into the one place nothing can later correct. Ground means `Solid` or `Powder` only.
  *Re-test when:* Both exclusions are load-bearing; `draining_a_lake_does_not_leave_a_dark_band_above_it` and `a_tree_does_not_turn_the_sky_behind_it_into_a_cave` pin them.
- **Reports/underground-definition.md §"What was wrong with inferring it"** - Inferring "underground" from the standing world — four successive rules, the last being topmost-non-empty skyline plus repair of columns lower than ground within six columns — failed on measurement (`probe_what_counts_as_underground`): one floating cell at y=40 cast 20 rows of cave; a 51-cell plank same; a 13-wide shaft became open daylight 35 rows into the mountain while 12-wide stayed a tunnel. Raising the reach moves the threshold, never removes it, and widens the wrong answer — "there is no setting that does not exist." Replaced by `World::sky_surface`, frozen on the first frame.
  *Re-test when:* A hill, a dug shaft, a built roof and a mid-air grain are identical from cells alone — only history distinguishes them. Re-open only via option C's player-placed wall/background layer, never via another inference rule.
- **Reports/underground-definition.md §"Why storing it does not repeat the reverted attempt"** - A stateful skyline remembered in the `Renderer` was built once and reverted: it broke `dirty_rect_skip_is_pixel_identical_to_a_full_redraw`, because a fresh renderer could not agree with one that had history.
  *Re-test when:* Explicitly conditional and now resolved: the objection was *where* the state lived, not that it existed. Stored in the `World` (`World::sky_surface`), which both renderers read, the same test passes unchanged — the dead end is renderer-resident world state, not stored state.
- **Reports/weather-handoff.md 'Bugs this push introduced and then fixed' (the skyline)** - A stateful skyline (remember the highest the ground has ever been, Terraria-style) fixed the mined-shaft bug but broke dirty_rect_skip_is_pixel_identical_to_a_full_redraw — a skyline depending on history cannot agree between a renderer that has the history and a fresh one that does not. The shipped rule is stateless (inside-ground where terrain on both sides stands higher); note 'and' not 'min', because 'min' paints a dark band along the foot of every cliff.
  *Re-test when:* Held while the dirty-rect pixel-identity guarantee stands and history lives in the renderer; history stored in the world (as sky_surface later did) evades the objection.
- **src/render.rs cell_colour, fake AO comment** - Fake ambient occlusion (darkening a cell by enclosure) was tried and measured at ~10 ms on the 512x320 stress scene alone — four extra World::get HashMap lookups per pixel, paid on every visible pixel every frame including settled worlds. Cut rather than shipped over budget.
  *Re-test when:* Explicit re-test condition recorded: safe to turn back on after PLAN.md M19 gives rendering direct chunk-array neighbour access instead of a World::get per neighbour (the lesson ChunkView already applied to the sweep).
- **src/render.rs cell_colour, liquid partial-fill comment** - Rendering liquid occupancy only (a 2% cell drawn identically to a full one) made the compressible-volume model unfalsifiable by eye: three sessions of liquid work measured fill while the screen showed occupancy, and the ballooning failure mode read as water multiplying rather than thinning — which got two otherwise-promising fixes reverted. Fill now dims toward black, with the hardcoded dimming floor replaced by per-material Material::fill_dimming because conspicuousness is a look judgement.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while Cell::aux carries continuous fill; any liquid-model work must be judged against a display that draws fill.
- **src/render.rs const UNDERGROUND / sky-to-dark ramp doc** - A one-row transition from open daylight to full underground was shipped and read as 'way too intense': a room went from sky to near-black across a single cell the instant it was enclosed, and every cave mouth was a flat black cutout. Replaced by a 24-cell ramp (three field blocks), set by eye — shallower reads as a black slot, deeper stops caves reading dark.
  *Re-test when:* The ramp must stay a pure function of position so a renderer with history and a fresh one agree (the dirty-rect pixel-identity requirement that sank the stateful skyline).
- **src/render.rs dirty-rect skip test, warm-up comment (near dirty_rect_skip_is_pixel_identical...)** - An earlier version of the dirty-rect guard stepped the world once before both draws and never again, so nothing differed between warm-up and the real draw — skipping the wrong chunks entirely still passed. The world must step between the two draws so a buggy skip leaves genuinely stale pixels to catch.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any before/after redraw guard: the state under test must change between the compared draws.
- **src/render.rs draw, full-redraw conditions ('Chunk bodies used to be in this list')** - Any chunk body in flight used to force a full-screen redraw every frame. Reported from play as 'when something big breaks into lots of little pieces, the performance gets bad' — fracture made it much worse (a collapse that promoted 13 bodies now promotes over 100), so the dirty-rect skip did nothing during exactly the busiest events. Bodies' current and last-frame rectangles are unioned into the dirty region instead.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while collapses promote many bodies; the same trick (dirty rectangle, not full redraw) is what the moon uses.
- **src/render.rs draw, touched-chunks doc ('the first version of this did exactly that')** - Snapshotting is_settled() at draw time missed chunks that went active and settled again between two draws — which happens whenever the catch-up loop runs more than one tick per draw — and a debug harness caught the buffer stale. World::take_touched_chunks accumulates across every tick's end_step instead of answering only 'did it change on its most recent tick'.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while more than one world tick can run per draw (MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME catch-up).
- **src/render.rs fn apply_field_overlay (doc) and test field_overlay_leaves_an_unaffected_cell_unchanged_even_when_on** - The opposite mistake to the organism overlay: an earlier field overlay used a flat constant blend to avoid washing out low readings, and because every ramp's zero-magnitude colour is saturated, it tinted every ambient pixel — for pressure, nearly the whole visible world blended 60% toward white (caught by independent review; confirmed to fail with the blend reverted to flat). Field channels covering every pixel need magnitude-scaled blending; full-replace is only for per-cell channels.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while field ramps have saturated zero-points; the blend/replace split is per channel kind, not a global rule.
- **src/render.rs fn apply_organism_overlay (doc)** - The first shipped version copied apply_field_overlay's magnitude-scaled blend for per-cell scalar debug channels, and the canopy-density sheet read as blank: the ramp was red, wood is brown, and a mid-range value moved the red byte from 139 to 155 — present but invisible, inviting a fix aimed at working code. Scalar debug channels must be a full replace on a fixed dark-to-bright ramp; a debug readout must not be a function of the thing it debugs.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any per-cell debug channel drawn over coloured material; the resource channel only looked fine because green sits far from brown.
- **src/render.rs fn rebuild_horizon (doc)** - The old rebuild_sky_floor repair pass inferred 'this is a dug hole' from shape (a column lower than both neighbours) and was wrong in both directions at once: a 13-cell-wide shaft let daylight 35 rows into a mountain while a 12-cell one stayed a tunnel (and widening shafts is what mining is), and a single cell or plank left in the air put a column of false cave beneath it. Replaced by World::sky_surface, written once at the first step and never revised — a surface that cannot move needs no repair.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the sky surface is frozen at first step; if the skyline ever becomes mutable again, the shape-guessing question returns and must not be answered geometrically.
- **src/render.rs fn sky_depth (doc)** - under_sky was once written as y < floor directly; re-expressing it as depth <= 0 silently moved the boundary one row, which draw_lightning's ground-finding scan reads. The convention is depth < 0 for exactly that reason — do not 'simplify' the comparison.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while draw_lightning and other consumers read the sky-floor row; a boundary-row audit is needed before changing the sign convention.
- **src/render.rs Renderer::organism_overlay field (doc)** - This overlay once kept the dirty-rect skip wholesale, on the argument that every channel it draws lives in Cell::aux and can only change via a set. Decision 2 step 2c falsified that: carbon, canopy_density and carbon_conductance moved to OrganismState and are deliberately written without touching the grid, so on a settled world the overlay would freeze while values moved — a frozen sheet reads identically to a dead mechanism, and that misreading already cost one wrong diagnosis. Scalar channels force a full redraw; only CellType (genuinely aux-resident) keeps the skip.
  *Re-test when:* The split is exact: re-derive it whenever a drawn channel moves between Cell::aux and out-of-grid state.
- **src/render.rs test a_burning_cells_colour_flickers_across_time_buckets** - Fire was once a static blend keyed only on temperature, so a burning cell held one flat colour for its whole burn — no frame-to-frame animation at all, a user-facing complaint. Replaced by time-bucketed flicker; the test guards that a burning cell's colour varies across buckets.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while burn rendering derives from a slowly-varying scalar; any static mapping reads as a decal.
- **src/sim/world.rs fn end_step and test a_single_out_of_sweep_write_to_a_settled_chunk_is_touched_after_one_end_step** - Keying the touched-chunks render set on `!was_settled` alone misses a fully-settled chunk that received exactly one out-of-sweep write (organism growth, explosion, collapse, landing particle): such a write only arms pending_dirty, so was_settled — read before end_sweep promotes it — still reports settled and the pixel change surfaces one whole tick late. Confirmed via revert to fail. Both sides of end_sweep must be checked.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while out-of-sweep writes only ever set pending_dirty and promotion happens in end_sweep.
- **src/sky.rs const SKY_QUANTUM (doc)** - Putting the moon into the sky's key-change redraw accounting was evaluated and rejected: a full redraw is 10.49 ms and the moon moves about a cell every three frames, so treating it as a colour change would repaint the whole screen several hundred times a night for a disc a few hundred pixels across. It moves on a dirty rectangle instead (current plus last-frame rect), the same trick as chunk bodies.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while a full redraw costs ~10 ms; the moon must stay out of the SKY_QUANTUM key-change count.
- **`Reports/physical-trees-design-2026-08-23.md` §3.2; measured 2026-08-23** - **Continuous render-side tree sway** -- a per-column shear of the drawn position off the wind field, proposed as "zero physics, zero wake cost". The shear maths is free; the *repaint* is not, and it was the whole cost. Measured on a grown 8-tree stand (28,421 plant cells) at the shipped 512x320 viewport: repainting every chunk holding a plant cell, every frame, costs **7.997 ms mean / 9.302 ms worst**, against **0.012 ms** for the same stand settled with the dirty-rect skip working and **11.948 ms** for a full redraw. That is 2.3x the ~3.5 ms wind-revert-class kill bar `ascii` itself prints, on half a 60 Hz frame. Not an artifact of chunk granularity: per-tree bounding boxes come to **66.2%** of the screen against the 24 canopy chunks' 60.0%, because grown crowns interleave and their boxes overlap -- a multi-rect renderer would repaint *more*. And `render.rs` folds every rect into one union anyway. Cost is linear at ~76 ns/pixel and every arrangement lands on that line. Replaced by a discrete, gust-local lean (two repaints per gust of one ~78-cell band, ~0.11 ms/frame amortised).
  *Re-test when:* The rejection depends on a grown canopy covering most of the viewport and on the renderer repainting by rect. A **cell-granular** repaint list (only the cells that moved, plus the cells they vacated) is the version that could scale -- bounded below by 2 x cells x 76 ns, so foliage-only over this stand is >=1.5 ms -- and no such render path exists today. Build that first, then re-price.
- **`Reports/physical-trees-design-2026-08-23.md` §3.5; measured 2026-08-23** - **Sim-side tree sway** -- moving plant cells in the grid so the world, not just the renderer, sees a tree bend. Rejected on four independent counts: it wakes **24 of 40** chunks permanently on the measured stand, against a river-cost measurement putting 13 awake chunks at **+1.58 ms/frame standing** and a pre-registered 2.0 ms bar; it invalidates `plant::anchor_support` (a per-organism Dijkstra run once per `ORGANISM_TICK_INTERVAL`) every frame instead of every 45, over 28,421 cells; every geometry change raises support distances, which is exactly what `anchor_support` schedules structural checks on, and a structural check fired mid-organism is the amputation landmine (measured precedent: a stand 20,213 living cells -> 772 from one check); and nothing but the renderer reads a swaying tree's positions, so it buys nothing the render version does not.
  *Re-test when:* Not a tuning question -- three of the four are architectural. Re-opens only if plant cells ever move in the CA sweep for some other reason, which `wood.ron`'s own doc says they deliberately do not.

## worldgen  (17 entries)

- **Review card `20260822T180743652Z-e79163`; `probe_p1_is_there_a_pool_behind_the_cliff` (tests/worldgen.rs); measured 2026-08-22** - Sourcing a waterfall from a **pool the terrain already has** -- finding a basin behind a cliff lip, filling it, and letting it spill over the edge. Asked for directly: *"spring should originate in depressions so they fill up and spill out into a waterfall. So it comes from a pool."* Built, measured at every stage, and withdrawn: **the landform does not exist in this generator's terrain.** For a basin to spill over a *particular* cliff, that cliff's lip must be the basin's lowest exit, which means no ground between the basin and the rim may stand above the lip.
