Requiring that (`hollow_start == 1`) placed **zero springs across four presets and six seeds** -- and it is not a tuning failure, it is definitional: a cliff edge is a local high point, so the ground behind it rises away from the lip rather than dipping below it. Relaxing the requirement is what produces the illusion of progress: a walk that steps *over* the intervening high ground finds a basin 200-450 columns back that is behind a wall, and a spring put there fills a pond that never moves toward the cliff at all (seed 7 held 1212 cells beside its outlet with `drained 0`; seed 42 the same at 330 columns). Five successive fixes -- rock bed, wider reach, bigger minimum volume, higher outlet lift, plan-ground lip -- each recovered some placement or some emission and every one still returned `drained 0`, which is the "two fixes failing the same way means the approach is wrong" signal at five.
  *Re-test when:* never as a *search* over existing terrain, at any tuning -- the rejection is of the premise, and the census is the evidence: about half of all cliff rims do have a hollow within 120 columns behind them (canyon 49/92, rolling 33/70, terraced 29/63, median 12 rows deep), but essentially none of them are adjacent to the lip. **The open route was to author the landform rather than look for it, and it is now taken** -- the pass excavates a basin immediately behind the rim and fills it, the way `ponds`, `residuals` and `vaults` all author what they need. What made that work is the *same* measurement that killed the search: requiring the ground to drop below the lip matched nothing, so the complement holds -- the shelf behind a rim stands at or above the lip for a median 107-120 columns, which is exactly the back wall a cut basin needs. Also still true, and cheap: the *sink* half of the same card shipped separately and works (`f5f3b19`), because a drain's **height** is what decides whether a pool stands.


- **Review card `20260822T084126139Z-201147` (answered 2026-08-22); `params::residual_density` doc** - Cutting `residual_density` 1.4 -> 0.45 uniformly across every preset to answer *"they shouldn't be common ... shouldn't be dominate"*. Set from a sweep, green on every test, and rejected on the render: *"Spires should not just be thinned out. They should be part of a specific biome. They should not exist at all in most biomes but some biomes should have them and they can be more regular. **I didn't mean a uniform decrease in spires.**"* Measurement afterwards showed the cut was worse than a thinning: on `rolling` at 0.45 the spire census (`probe_p2_how_common_are_standing_features`) was **identical to a residuals-off control** in all three statistics -- med 0 / p90 1 / max 2 either way -- so the pass had stopped contributing anything and what remained on screen was `boulders`, `brows` and proud talus, which this knob cannot reach. No further cut could have helped. The monuments shrank with the count too (`heights` p90 33 -> 23), because a smaller count is fewer draws at the tall tail. Replaced by a gate on where rock country *is*; `residual_density` is back at 1.4 and now answers only "how emphatic is rock country where there is rock country".
  *Re-test when:* Never as a global thinning -- the rejection is of the *axis*, not the value. If spire frequency needs to come down again, the levers are `region::FORMATION_BARREN` (how much of the world is rock country) and `FORMATION_FLOOR`/`FORMATION_PEAK` (how emphatic it is inside), which move the two halves independently.


- **`region.rs` `FORMATION_BARREN`'s doc comment; measured 2026-08-22** - Gating `Character::formation` on the region's **own** draw -- the obvious implementation of "spires belong to a specific biome", and the first one built. It works, in the sense that the gate fires: at a 0.86 threshold, 87% of the blocks the residual pass samples read exactly zero. It is nonetheless indistinguishable from the thing it replaced, because a `RegionMap` region is **102-256 columns** at the shipped size -- `MIN_REGIONS`/`MAX_REGIONS` are per *window*, so a region is a fifth to a half of one screen. A rock country smaller than the view reads as a cluster rather than a place, and it *measures* as one: the per-screen spire census came back canyon 0/2/4, rolling 0/1/2, which is the census the rejected uniform thinning gave. Two completely different worlds, one statistic, because the thing meant to distinguish them was smaller than the window it was measured in. Replaced by `Purpose::RockCountry`, a low-frequency field at `ROCK_COUNTRY_SCALE` = 1700 columns (~3.3 screens), sampled at each region centre so the region blend still carries it.
  *Re-test when:* `COMPOSITION_WINDOW` or `MIN_REGIONS`/`MAX_REGIONS` change enough that a region is comparable to or wider than a screen. The rejection depends only on regions being small relative to the view, not on anything about `formation` itself. The general lesson outlives this case: **a feature whose whole point is spatial contrast cannot be gated at a scale below the one it is judged at**, and a statistic aggregated over that scale will report success either way.


- **Review card `20260822T043758278Z-018304` (blind A/B, answered 2026-08-22); `Reports/world-scale-phase-2.md` §5** - Scaling `strata_thickness` 4x with the cave envelope in the 4x world (canyon 12 -> 48), on the argument that a 4x cave shears along bedding four times finer than it was designed against, and that thicker bands give a residual tor fewer, chunkier shelves and so a better silhouette (`residual.rs` stacks `height / band_thickness` rings, clamped 3-8). Put to the owner as a **blind** A/B rather than argued, because the session had a stake in the answer. Verdict: the *shipped* 12 has the better pattern (`blind_was: [1, 0]`, so the pane the owner called B was `strata_thickness` 12). Not adopted; `strata_thickness` stays as shipped in every preset. The blinding is what caught it -- the session's own prediction was 48.
  *Re-test when:* the cave-shape work (`Reports/world-scale-handoff.md` Phase 3) changes how a cave reads against its bedding, since the underground half of the argument was never tested on its own -- the card showed a *surface* strip, and the owner judged the surface pattern. A card that shows the bedding *inside a cave* would be asking a different question and is not covered by this rejection.


- **`Reports/world-scale-phase-2.md` §7, and `examples/ascii.rs`'s generation-cost comment** - Replacing `RegionMap::sample`'s linear scan over region centres with a `partition_point` binary search, to explain generation scaling at "199x the 512x320 build for 128x the area -- worse than linearly". Implemented, verified bit-identical by world hash (`WORLD_HASH=1 scale_probe`, `aba4aaa19def5bed` both sides), and **measured at zero**: 5031 -> 5082 ms place, 9087 -> 9037 ms total, i.e. inside the noise on a 9-second build. Reverted. The scan really is O(regions) per cell on the shading path and regions really do grow with width (2-5 at 512 wide, up to 80 at 8192) -- the asymptotics were right and the hypothesis about *what was costing the time* was wrong. `PASS_TIMING=1` puts `stone_massif` at 3946 of 5188 ms and 201 ns per placed cell, against ~300 ns/cell at 512x320. There was no super-linear term to remove: the ratio was against **area**, and `sky_rows` does not scale with world height, so the shipped world is 94% solid against 512x320's 59% and its solid-cell count went up 206x for 128x of area. Against the cells it actually writes, the build is linear or slightly better.
  *Re-test when:* the region count per world grows well past 80 (a world much wider than 8192 at the current `COMPOSITION_WINDOW`, or a smaller window), or a profile puts `Terrain::character` on the hot path. The change is safe and bit-identical; it simply bought nothing at this size.


- **Reports/worldgen-design.md §5b Erosion (CFL constraint)** - Accelerating pipe-model erosion by taking larger time steps is ruled out by the model's CFL stability condition (timestep × velocity must not exceed cell size). Fast-forward has to run more steps or a coarser grid. Also: Mei's model is a heightmap model — it applies to the coarse (x,z) layer only, not to the side-view play world where erosion is whatever real water does.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any explicit shallow-water/virtual-pipes solver.
- **Reports/worldgen-design.md §6a 'Generated terrain must be at rest'** - Two options for generating loose material were rejected: making worldgen respect every material's repose angle (couples worldgen to material data; one bad .ron silently breaks terrain) and settling at generation time (settling needs neighbours that may not exist — circular). Recommended: generate only Solid, with loose material limited to trivially stable configurations — otherwise a slump propagates into chunks that do not exist yet via World::set's on-demand creation.
  *Re-test when:* Unknown — recorded at design time; a streaming design that guarantees neighbour existence before reveal would change the settle-at-generation calculus.
- **Reports/worldgen-design.md §7 'Aquifers: local water bodies beat one global table'** - The single global water table proposed in §2 was superseded by localized aquifers with their own levels: with one table, almost all caves below sea level simply flood (the behaviour Minecraft explicitly chose against), whereas a water level that is a field gives perched springs, flooded caverns, and dry-next-to-wet moisture gradients.
  *Re-test when:* Unknown — a design revision from literature, no engine measurement recorded.
- **Reports/worldgen-design.md §7 'Generation: additive density, not subtractive carving'** - The original recommendation — worm-carving as the primary cave mechanism, framed as carving voids out of rock — was wrong. Additive density (noise decides where stone is placed at all; below threshold is cave) removes the carve pass, the carve/placement ordering question, and carves landing in ungenerated space, and resolves §6b's carve-then-anchor sequencing.
  *Re-test when:* Worms are not discarded entirely: surface reachability must still be made to happen, and a noise-defined ceiling has no bounded thickness against stone's max_unsupported_span: 3 — the structural-span problem Minecraft never had is the surviving argument for a controllable-radius mechanism somewhere in the pipeline.
- **Reports/worldgen-design.md 'Noise definitions, corrected'** - Defining ridged noise as 1−|fBm| (the original draft's definition) is the common simplification and does not produce terrain-like heterogeneity. Musgrave's ridged multifractal is a multiplicative cascade (weight *= signal per octave, with offset and gain) whose feedback makes rough areas rougher and plains smooth — that heterogeneity is the whole point. Start at H=1.0, offset=1.0, gain=2.0.
  *Re-test when:* Also recorded: ridged noise has a derivative discontinuity at the ridge — irrelevant for thresholding, a trap if the field is ever sampled as a gradient-followed channel.
- **Reports/worldgen-design.md 'Wave Function Collapse: examined and ruled out'** - WFC was investigated for terrain and ruled out: generation depends on the order of visits and therefore the player's path — precisely what worldgen(seed, coord, age) forbids — memory can never be released, dead ends accumulate with distance, and it cannot be threaded. The per-chunk-constrained variant fails generally (constraints propagate several slots, leaving neighbouring chunks with no solution), and the backtracking mitigation can regenerate the slot the player stands in.
  *Re-test when:* Remains plausible for bounded, non-streamed content generated once — a fixed-size ruin or structure template where the constraint region has an edge.
- **Reports/worldgen-design.md 'Why not full 3D'** - Full 3D was ruled out by arithmetic: 2048³ is 68 GB of cells, 512³ is 819x today's compute (seconds per frame under perfect scaling), the checkerboard proof and MAX_REACH need re-deriving — and the real objection is legibility: in 3D you see a surface and the interesting interactions are hidden inside it.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional for the simulation; the coarse (x,z) planar layer is kept because it is one value per chunk-column and never simulated.
- **Reports/worldgen-design.md 'Why not top-down 2D'** - Top-down 2D was ruled out: it puts gravity perpendicular to the simulated plane, which deletes update.rs's movement rules, the bottom-to-top sweep order, angle of repose, all of M17 and most of M16 — discarding the falling-sand half that is M1–M15 and the engine's identity. A top-down map view survives as a rendered-never-simulated UI feature from the coarse layer.
  *Re-test when:* The recorded honest cost of side view: foraging is natively top-down, so double-bridge scenarios are harder to stage (later confirmed the hard way in creature-direction §13b/§13c).
- **src/sim/world.rs field sky_surface (struct World)** - Inferring "underground" from world shape has been wrong in a new way every attempt. The replaced version (topmost non-empty cell, patched against higher ground within six columns) put twenty rows of cave under one floating cell, did the same under a plank of any width, and flipped a dug shaft from tunnel to daylight between 12 and 13 cells wide. The surface is now recorded once at generation and never revised.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent: geometry cannot tell dug from built from hill; no reach or threshold makes it able to. Revisit only if the underground definition stops needing that distinction (M10 streaming re-keys the storage, not the model).
- **src/worldgen/column.rs fn base_wave (doc)** - The macro silhouette used to be one sine with a fixed phase — fixed deliberately to guarantee a ridge and valley in frame at every seed. It did, and it put them in the same place every time, so the first thing anyone sees never varied, and amplitude knobs could not fix it because the shape was not a parameter. Replaced by regional layout (region.rs), with the composition guarantee moved into RegionMap::new's minimum elevation spread.
  *Re-test when:* Holds whenever a compositional guarantee is implemented by fixing shape rather than constraining it; parameterise the shape instead.
- **src/worldgen/passes.rs talus pass ('Make the heap stand up, rather than trusting the geometry that drew it')** - Deriving a stable talus-apron shape from cliff geometry had four separate at-rest failures — a wedge inheriting the ground's slope, a cut-off toe, an apron proud of rising ground — and each fix produced the next; four failures of one kind means the approach was wrong, not the tuning. The shape is no longer derived: the apron's top profile is clamped by the same two-sweep repose taper the soil blanket uses, so non-avalanching is a property of the sweep, not of a case enumeration that was never complete.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while terrain can undercut an apron in unenumerated ways — i.e. always; do not return to case analysis.
- **src/worldgen/passes.rs water-table distance transform comment (two-pass Manhattan)** - A per-column distance-to-water transform was tried first and left the wetted perimeter of every above-table pond unwrapped: saturated bank cells were not seeds, their outward neighbours computed as far-from-water and got no moisture fringe, so capillarity assembled it at runtime — 82 frames of awake chunks on the rolling preset against 31 once ponds seed the full two-pass transform (liquid at -1, capacity-bearing cells at 0).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while ponds can sit above the smoothed table; any transform that ignores 2D adjacency to standing water re-creates the runtime cost.

## scheduler  (10 entries)

- **src/sim/explosion.rs `settle_structure` (prototype, `claude/perf-blast-relax`); Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §S** - Giving a finished blast the one converged `structural::relax_region` pass that `World::paint_capsule` already pays for over a brush stroke, to stop §S's structural-check backlog: **it does not work at any box size, and the arm that looked like it worked was measuring an immunity.** Sized to the charge (`damage_extent + 4`) it buys nothing -- 45,134 pending against the baseline's 43,789 at frame 3,600 -- because `relax_region` seeds its boundary from the values just outside the box and trusts them, so where a charge severs a load path the outside is stale-low and the correction restarts from the box edge. At 8x the box the backlog appeared to *vanish* (5,134 pending against 25,876 at frame 2,400, scheduler 0.03 ms against 10.08 ms, whole frame 31.21 -> 18.98 ms, `scripts/acceptance.sh` green on every case) -- and the control killed it: `relax_region` anchors any `is_resting_on_ground` cell at distance 0 outright where `tick` roots on ground only as a last resort, so a big box over a blast's rubble field roots the whole neighbourhood at zero. With the rule held fixed to `compute_world_distances`' bedrock-only seeding (`SETTLE_GROUND=0`), the same 8x box gives **19,674 pending / 10.00 ms at frame 2,400 and 32,877 / 8.85 ms at 3,000** -- tracking the baseline -- and `max aux` returns to 2,482 from 142. Separately, the pass costs a 440.75 ms frame.
  *Re-test when:* This rejects a **box-shaped** region, not the converged pass as an idea. The untried shape is a region derived from what actually changed -- invalidate the subtree of every cell whose support parent was destroyed, then Dijkstra from the boundary of *that* set, which has correct values by construction and needs no box. Any such attempt must (a) hold the anchor rule fixed or fix §S2 first, since eager ground-rooting silences the measurement, (b) be judged on `max aux` and `FailureCounts` as well as on the queue, and (c) be amortised across frames -- the 440 ms spike is independent of how the region is chosen. Note that acceptance was **green on all cases** through the immunity arm: it is not a guard against this.

- **README.md 'M16 status' — independent-review list ('Moss never went dormant' and 'Roots grew for free' items)** - Unconditionally rescheduling an organism's own site every tick regardless of whether it can ever act again: a fully-enclosed moss tip, and later a root waiting on energy that will never arrive, each became an immortal active site checked forever — exactly the unbounded cost the scheduler exists to avoid. Both fixed with stale/starvation-tick counters that force dormancy past a threshold.
  *Re-test when:* Nothing re-wakes a dormant tip if conditions later improve — an accepted limitation; any new organism site kind needs its own dormancy bound from the start.
- **README.md 'M16 status' — scheduler issue #7 paragraph ('That checking only sites that are actually due claim...')** - Active-site storage as HashMap<ChunkCoord, Vec<ActiveSite>> with a full drain-and-retest of every pending site each frame: real cost proportional to total pending sites rather than due sites, and HashMap iteration order was the engine's one documented non-determinism source (two sites due the same frame could resolve differently run to run). Replaced by BinaryHeap<Reverse<ActiveSite>> min-heap on next_frame with a deterministic (x, y, kind) tiebreak — true O(due log n).
  *Re-test when:* Same-build determinism is a committed requirement; any scheduler storage change must keep a deterministic tiebreak and stop at the first not-yet-due site.
- **Reports/open-bugs-handoff.md §3 'Scheduler under-enforces max_active_tips'** - Dispatch-one-at-a-time as the fix for the invisible in-flight batch was evaluated and rejected: it changes the cap's meaning and risks a tip producing a due-now tip in the same frame, costing more than the defect. The landed fix counts the organism's own cell list (the sidecar) instead of scanning the schedule heap.
  *Re-test when:* Held given the sidecar route exists and shipped; the heap-scan blind spot is structural, so any counter must not read the heap.
- **src/sim/chunk.rs Chunk::is_settled (alternative paragraph)** - Clamping the dirty position into the chunk's own bounds inside mark_dirty was tried and reverted: it fixes the awake-forever chunk but throws away the issue #3 optimization — an empty neighbour chunk then pays a wide, pointless sweep whenever something moves far away next door. Answering from sweep_region keeps both.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the per-chunk tracked-reach optimization (neighbour_waking_stops_at_the_neighbours_own_reach) is wanted.
- **src/sim/chunk.rs Chunk::is_settled (definition paragraph)** - Defining settled as the narrower "dirty is empty" left chunks awake forever while never being swept: a neighbour's wake mark lies outside the chunk by construction, and a short tracked reach cannot expand it back in-bounds, giving an empty sweep region — measured at 3 such chunks on the seam-cliff scene under the parallel driver. Settled is now "sweep_region has nothing to give".
  *Re-test when:* Holds while touch_neighbours marks out-of-bounds positions and per-chunk reach can be shorter than the wake distance.
- **src/sim/evaporation.rs fn schedule_from_sweep (doc)** - Gating the sweep hook on (x + y) % INTERVAL == frame % INTERVAL to skip reads on 59 of 60 frames was written and silently turned the whole mechanic off — nothing evaporated anywhere, because a chunk is swept only a handful of frames before sleeping, so a 1-in-60 gate mostly never opens while the sweep still visits. It was written to fix a frame cost that turned out not to exist: the apparent 4x regression (52.7 vs 11.9 ms) was machine drift; re-measured in-session it was noise (worst frame 11.5 ms, 70 sites all run).
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the sweep's visits are the scarce resource (chunk sleep). Re-measure a baseline in the same session before fixing a 'regression', not only before reporting one.
- **src/sim/evaporation.rs module doc, heading 'Why it is on the scheduler and not on the CA sweep'** - Evaporation fired from the CA sweep's MaterialKind::Liquid dispatch arm was built and reverted (Reports/weather-handoff.md §1): a settled chunk is not swept, so the rule only ran while water was moving, and a lake lost 7% against a puddle's 1.7% — backwards, because a lake stays awake settling longer. Moved to the active-site scheduler; the sweep only schedules a site when a liquid cell fails to move with air above.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while chunk sleeping exists. Any mechanism whose rate scales with time-spent-awake re-creates it — schedule_active_site's positional dedup is load-bearing for exactly that reason, not an optimisation.
- **src/sim/scheduler.rs fn step doc ('Every due site is popped… never taken out as a whole heap') and decay.rs test a_reseeded_organism_keeps_growing_after_its_first_tick** - The take-the-whole-heap-out/swap-back dispatch shape was rejected: it left World's heap genuinely empty for the whole dispatch loop, so any schedule_active_site call made from inside a dispatched tick was silently discarded when the real heap was written back — a decay-reseeded seed got planted but never grew a single cell beyond itself, forever. World::pop_due_active_site pops due sites into a Vec up front instead; confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code.
  *Re-test when:* Unconditional — any dispatch loop must keep the live heap writable for mid-tick scheduling.
- **src/sim/scheduler.rs impl Ord for ActiveSite and fn step doc** - The old HashMap<ChunkCoord, Vec<ActiveSite>> active-site storage was replaced for two measured reasons (issue #7): it tested next_frame against every pending site and rebuilt the whole map every frame regardless of how many were due (its own doc's O(due) claim was false), and its per-process-randomized drain order made two sites due the same frame resolve differently run to run — the engine's only documented non-determinism source. The BinaryHeap min-heap with a full total order (next_frame dominant, (x,y,kind) tiebreak) fixes both.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while determinism is required (PLAN.md; note it was once 'not required' and older comments still say so) — but the performance half stands alone regardless.

## parallelism  (10 entries)

- **PLAN.md bud-break section, 'Two bugs step 2c exposed', first bullet (organism cell-list hook)** - Hooking OrganismState::cells maintenance at World::set was recorded as 'complete by construction' and was not: parallel::ChunkView::set writes same-chunk cells straight into its own Chunk, so a falling seed silently dropped out of its organism's cell list — a no-op until carbon moved into the list, then fatal (forest scene 8 organism cells after 8,000 frames vs 5,468 before). Membership changes must be queued in ChunkView and replayed after the pass; and the guard test ran only the serial driver so it could never fail — test both drivers.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any per-cell bookkeeping hooked at a 'single' write seam: verify ChunkView's same-chunk branch too.
- **PLAN.md Progress log, M5 (multithreading) entry** - The plan's original sketch — a single unsafe function handing out overlapping mutable 3x3 chunk neighbourhoods — was abandoned: a CellSurface trait plus a per-pass exclusive-ownership-and-deferred-queue design covered the same ground with no unsafe code at all, at a ~3.6x sweep speedup.
- **README.md 'M5 status' — 'Per-chunk RNG, not one shared stream' paragraph** - One shared World-level RNG stream for the sweep: a parallel worker cannot safely share a mutable generator across threads. Each Chunk owns its own Rng seeded from its coordinate; World keeps a separate Rng for non-sweep uses.
  *Re-test when:* The determinism decision (same-build, per PLAN.md) never required reproducible randomness, so splitting the stream cost nothing — revisit only if cross-run reproducible randomness becomes a requirement.
- **README.md 'M5 status' opening paragraphs (code: src/sim/parallel.rs)** - The plan's original M5 sketch — a single unsafe function handing out overlapping mutable 3x3 chunk neighbourhoods — was rejected before build: it needed a second, harder proof (that reads within one worker's sweep see that worker's own earlier writes) which does not announce itself as missing and would have shipped as a subtle load-bearing gap. The shipped design uses Vec ownership plus deferred queues, no unsafe anywhere in simulation, at the cost of one serial merge step per pass.
  *Re-test when:* Re-open only with that second proof actually in hand; the no-unsafe design's one serial merge is the recorded price of not needing it.
- **Reports/coupling-research.md §7b (rapier features)** - Do not enable rapier's parallel feature: a parallel constraint solver is where local determinism is most likely to be lost (its documented incompatibility with enhanced-determinism is the signal), the engine's own parallel sweep already delivers the speedup, and same-build determinism only needs the default build. Pin the feature set in Cargo.toml with a comment, and guard with a run-twice bitwise-transform test.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while determinism is required (PLAN.md reversed it to required, same-build scope); enhanced-determinism itself is also not needed at that scope.
- **src/sim/parallel.rs fn pass_key and fn step (four-group comment)** - The plan's four-group (cx % 2, cy % 2) checkerboard silently breaks the bottom-to-top row invariant: cy and cy+1 always land in different groups, so half of all horizontal seams sweep the upper chunk first and a crossing cell moves twice per frame, thinning a falling body into a dark one-row line exactly on the seam (reported live on exactly the predicted half). Replaced by one pass per (chunk row, cx parity), bottom row first.
  *Re-test when:* Any pass grouping must sweep the lower chunk row before the one above; safety proof re-derived for the narrower grouping.
- **src/sim/parallel.rs fn run_pass (None-arm comment) and test a_chunk_touched_only_by_a_neighbours_dirty_mark_is_never_lost** - run_pass used to filter_map away a chunk whose sweep_region() came back None even though it had been selected as dirty — silently dropping it from world.chunks forever, since only chunks that reached `owned` get put back. The None arm must be a skip that still honours put-back, never a filter.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent while run_pass owns take/put of chunks.
- **src/sim/parallel.rs fn step (revisited-marking paragraph) and src/sim/update.rs test the_parallel_driver_does_not_band_a_falling_body_worse_than_the_serial_one** - Marking seam-crossing cells `revisited` so the receiving chunk skips them was tried twice and reverted both times (e816477): it clears the torn dark line but replaces it with a throttle at the same seam — summed row-fill deficit 2236 (all crossings) / 1948 (downward only) against 988 for correct pass ordering. One shipped and had to be reverted because its guard test only looked at rows on seams; the every-row banding bar caught the second before shipping.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent: penalising the crossing cell moves the artifact rather than removing it; fix ordering. The banding bar (parallel <= 1400, serial <= 700) exists to catch this class.
- **src/sim/parallel.rs module doc (opening paragraph)** - The plan's original concurrency design — a single unsafe function handing out overlapping mutable 3x3 chunk neighbourhoods — turned out to need a second, unproduced proof that queuing everything through it resolves to a correct write order within one worker's own sweep. Replaced with take/put exclusive ownership plus deferred queues: no unsafe, at the cost of one serial merge step per pass.
  *Re-test when:* Re-evaluate only if someone actually produces that second proof; the current design needs none.
- **src/sim/update.rs mod seam_cliffs (cause paragraph) and test chunking_the_sweep_does_not_change_where_a_pile_settles** - Three movement-rule hypotheses for seam cliffs were wrong first, including the recorded leading one (seam cells failing to get flowing() set — they do get it). The cause is the chunk-by-chunk sweep order making the seam column a one-grain-per-frame conveyor (33 straight-down slumps vs 0.9 sideways escapes/frame); the step_monolithic control is what found it.
  *Re-test when:* Any artifact aligned with the F1 chunk grid: suspect sweep decomposition before movement rules, and use the monolithic control.

## character  (14 entries)

The M9 gnome. Every entry here is from the tree-interaction pass
(`claude/gnome-tree-interaction-l87ijo`); the citation is the record.

- **src/sim/player.rs `step`'s climb gate (comment)** - Climbing keyed on `W`/`S` against a handhold, on the argument that the keyboard was full and the verb could ride the jump key for nothing. `W` is jump *and* climb, so jump-walking through a wood grabbed every trunk clipped at the top of an arc and kept lifting: reported from play as "if I am just jump walking in a forest, I can basically fly/hover". Replaced by an explicit grab key (Shift). The trade was "no new key" against "no accidental grab" and it picked wrong.
  *Re-test when:* Never as stated. A verb that fires when the player did not ask for it is worse than a verb that costs a key; if the keyboard is ever the binding constraint again, take the key from something else.
- **src/sim/player.rs `step`'s climb gate (comment); test `a_falling_gnome_does_not_catch_himself_on_a_tree_he_is_not_holding`** - Engaging the climb on contact alone, with no input required. A gnome falling *past* a tree caught himself on it — flypaper, not a climb. Measured arriving at row 48 against a floor at 88.
  *Re-test when:* Holds as long as trees are walk-through; the grip needs an intent because contact is now the normal state, not a special one.
- **src/sim/player.rs `step`'s climb gate (comment)** - Requiring `!grounded` so that feet-on-ground always jumps. Reads well and cannot be entered: `jump_pressed` is an edge, so walking up to a trunk with `W` held offers no second press to hop with, and he stood at the base of a tree he was inside. The root problem it guarded against is handled by `GRIP_ROWS` instead.
  *Re-test when:* Moot under an explicit grab key; would return only if someone tried to make the grab implicit again.
- **src/sim/player.rs `shake_target` (comment); commit "The green marker was a symptom"** - The shake aiming along a ray *from the gnome's centre*, taking the first living tissue on it. Standing in or beside a tree that is distance 1 in every direction, so the verb fired wherever you pointed: the marker was on permanently (reported as "a green square when near trees"), digging inside a tree was impossible, and a trunk between `shake_reach` and `dig_reach` was a dead click. Replaced by taking the cell under the cursor.
  *Re-test when:* Holds. The pick wants the nearest thing it can cut; the shake wants the thing you are pointing at. They are different questions and must not share an aim.
- **src/sim/player.rs `face_toward` (comment); test `the_pick_aims_through_a_tree_at_the_rock_behind_it`** - Stopping the pick's aim ray at living tissue, so the shake could aim at a tree in front of a cliff. `mine_swept` skips organism cells, so a tree the ray stopped at was a bite that did nothing — measured landing at (85, 83), on the trunk. Movement collision, aim collision and *cut* collision are three questions and this merged the last two.
  *Re-test when:* Only if the pick ever becomes able to cut living tissue.
- **src/sim/player.rs `SHAKE_CELLS` (comment)** - A 250-cell cap on how much of a plant one shake reaches. Grabbing the base of a 2,609-cell tree filled the flood with trunk before reaching one leaf: 43 shakes shed nothing and sowed nothing off a plant with plenty of both. A size cap must bound work, never gate whether something happens.
  *Re-test when:* Raised to 3,000. Lower it only with a measurement showing the flood is a real frame cost, which it is not — one flood per `dig_cooldown` against a sweep doing 163,840 cells per frame.
- **src/sim/player.rs `sow_from_crown` (comment)** - Sowing a shaken seed into the first open cell *below the shaken point*. Never fires: he shakes a trunk at chest height and every cell under that is the ground the tree stands in. Seed comes off the crown now, whichever part is shaken.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while trees are rooted in ground.
- **src/sim/player.rs `shake`'s dislodge pass (comment)** - Shaking loose material off every cell of the plant, with no test for whether the cell is in the open. Roots are part of the plant and the soil bed rests on them, so every root read as a laden branch: 1,235 cells "knocked loose" in 43 shakes, nearly all of it soil being stirred round a root system. Gated on the cell having air beside it: 429.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while roots are `climbable` and grow through soil.
- **src/sim/player.rs `shake`'s seed gate (comment)** - Sowing on a shake with no maturity gate. A sown seed germinates into a sapling that can immediately be shaken for more, so holding the button turned the ground into a thicket — measured on `scene=shake` as 199 shakes sowing 10 seeds and growing a bush around him. Gated on `shoot_cells >= SEED_BEARING_CELLS`.
  *Re-test when:* Holds until a tree actually produces seed cells of its own, at which point the gate becomes the tree's business rather than the verb's.
- **src/render.rs `OCCLUDED_ALPHA` (comment)** - Drawing the gnome through a tree in front of him at alpha 0.28, on the argument that a character who vanishes into a crown is a character you have lost. Overruled by playtest — "behind has too much transparency, the player character should be hidden" — so the concern was the author's and not real at this zoom. Now 0.0.
  *Re-test when:* The constant and its branch are kept; restoring the ghost is one number. Worth revisiting at high zoom or with a busier canopy.
- **src/sim/player.rs test `a_canopy_breaks_a_fall_without_stopping_it` (comment)** - Measuring the canopy's fall-catch by *peak* `vy` over the drop. Measured 2.80 against 2.25 and read as the mechanism barely working, because almost all of the peak was the thirty cells of open air above the crown, where nothing is meant to differ. Arrival speed is the quantity: 2.80 against 1.41.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent measurement lesson — measure the quantity the mechanism changes, not the one that is easiest to sample.
- **src/render.rs `last_player_pose` (comment); test `turning_on_the_spot_repaints_him`** - Keying the sprite's dirty-rect comparison on its screen *rectangle* alone. Turning on the spot or starting a swing changes every pixel of him without moving his rectangle, so the comparison skipped the repaint and left the old pose on screen. The key is the whole pose now.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any per-sprite state that changes appearance without changing extent.
- **src/sim/player.rs `Tuning::shoulder_grains` (comment); test `a_bank_of_soil_is_still_a_wall`** - Counting the chest-height powder that blocks the gnome **per rect** rather than per row. It cannot tell seven scattered grains from one full course of seven, so keeping a drift solid caps the allowance below `PLAYER_WIDTH`, and that cap is already too small to clear a scatter: measured over `scene=wood`, 98 cells travelled at an allowance of 0 and 103 at 6, against 357 once the same count was taken per row.
  *Re-test when:* Never as stated — the row is the unit because a drift is made of courses. The shape to revisit is the *quantity*, if a cheap way to ask "is this grain part of a large connected mass" ever exists; a flood fill per cell per frame is not it.
- **src/sim/player.rs `Tuning::shoulder_grains` (comment)** - Setting the allowance to 6, one short of a full course, on the reasoning that higher is more forgiving. It is a cliff rather than a plateau: he stops walking over the forest floor and starts sinking into it, and does *worse* than the original veto — min/median cells travelled over six start frames 47/47, against 50/161 at 4 and 1/44 at 0.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while `wade_rows` is 4 of 14. A forgiveness knob has a ceiling as well as a floor, and the sweep found it.


## other  (19 entries)

- **`Reports/documentation-overhaul-plan.md` §17; `Reports/` directory layout** - An `Reports/archive/` subdirectory for superseded and historical reports was proposed, **chosen by the owner, and then reversed mid-plan** — so the refusal leaves no trace in the tree and reads as an idea nobody has had. Two reasons, both still live: with per-report status in `Reports/README.md` an agent routes by the **index**, never by browsing the directory, so moving a file buys the reader nothing it does not already have; and the `git mv` breaks `Reports/` paths held in other sessions' uncommitted worktrees (eight were open when this was written) and in ~150 source-comment citations, which name reports by path. Supersession became an index annotation instead. **The plan's own §17 predicted the recurrence — *"a later review that does not know this will re-propose them"* — and it was right: a 2026-08-27 review of the plant documentation reached the archive proposal independently and found this entry missing.** That is why it is here rather than only in the plan: `CLAUDE.md` routes every "should we build X" through this file, and a refusal recorded only in a design document is not on that route. Deleting rather than archiving was never proposed and is worse on the same two counts, plus a third: a superseded report is the *why* behind shipped code, and half this register's entries cite one.
  *Re-test when:* the index stops carrying per-report status (then directory position becomes the only signal), or `Reports/` grows past the point where `ls` is unreadable *and* nothing routes by index. Neither is close: 110 reports, every one indexed and status-marked, and `docscheck` gates both.
- **`Reports/measurement-under-contention.md`; branch `perf-lock`** - A machine-wide timing lock (`src/perf.rs`, 733 lines), a `TRUSTED`/`UNTRUSTED` verdict on every timing run, a quiet-probe (`examples/quiet_probe.rs`) and `scripts/perf.sh` were built and verified, and deliberately **not landed**. The investigation was sound and its measurements are kept: two runs of a byte-identical `examples/ascii` on identical work disagreed 2.42x and reversed the serial/parallel ordering, and the box measured 8% quiet over 45 minutes with a longest quiet spell of 40 s. What did not survive is the mechanism's premise - it arbitrates several concurrent sessions contending for the same four cores, and development moved mostly to cloud containers, which do not share cores that way. **The perf lane declined it independently and for a sharper reason** (2026-08-25): every timing it took was in a cloud container, and the contention it actually suffered was *self-inflicted* - its own `cargo build` and some spinning waiter loops - which a machine-wide lock would not have touched. Note that the report's own section 6 declines making `cargo` take the lock, so the lock as built would not have helped even on a shared box. Discipline about not building during a run is the fix, not a mutex. Landing it would cost 1,546 insertions across 11 files, including a 317-line rework of the CI-gated `ascii` example and an edit to `examples/filmstrip.rs` (the most-collided file in the repo), from a branch 547 behind scoring `BxF 6,017` against the project's own 300 bar. The two findings that generalise past the box were lifted into `CLAUDE.md`'s Method section instead - gate on counters never wall clock, and measure one scene not the suite - neither of which needs the lock to be true.
  *Re-test when:* development returns to a machine where concurrent sessions contend for the same cores. Then the report is the design and `perf-lock` is the implementation - but **re-derive rather than re-merge**: the branch will be further behind still, and the report's §6 records what was deliberately left unbuilt (making `cargo` take the lock, declined because it would buy speed rather than correctness and is the one mechanism whose failure blocks other people's builds).
- **`src/sim/world.rs` `push_organism`; merge of `plant-substrate-v2` onto `main`, 2026-08-21** - A **debug-only** organism generation-wrap counter (`pub static ORGANISM_GENERATION_WRAPS: AtomicU64` behind `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`) was built on `plant-substrate-v2` and dropped at the merge in favour of `main`'s always-on `World::organism_generation_wraps: u32`. Two reasons, both `main`'s own: a `u32` add on the allocation path is free next to the `HashMap` the same function just built, and a counter that only exists in debug builds cannot say anything about a long *release* session, which is the only place the count gets interesting. The dropped version also had **zero readers** — a declaration and an increment, nothing else.
  *Re-test when:* Not applicable as a mechanism; recorded so the debug-only form is not re-proposed as a "cheaper" option. It was not cheaper, it was blinder.

- **PLAN.md 'Decisions locked during planning' table, Determinism row** - 'Determinism not required' was reversed to required (same-build): off-camera persistence, slow processes in unloaded chunks, and fast-forward all need catch-up, and catch-up is only sound as outcome = f(state, elapsed, seed) — a pure function needs determinism to exist as a shortcut. Some older comments still say 'not required' and are wrong.
  *Re-test when:* Scoped narrowly: same-build only, not cross-platform bit-identical; worldgen reproducible within one world's lifetime.
- **PLAN-log.md 'Overnight run, section 6: explosion debris realism' (live verification paragraph), corrected by the §11 dirty-rect entry** - The windowed capture-sequence tool is useless for headless behaviour checks in this environment: captured frames were pixel-identical because the fixed-timestep accumulator does not advance under how the background invocation paces RedrawRequested — a frame-advancement pacing issue, not a render-path bug (screenshots and captures do work with the real window). Use a throwaway headless example in ascii.rs's style instead. Also: the first debug scene showed no scatter because the stone block was thicker than the blast radius's margin — debris re-embedded in still-solid stone; correct physics, useless scene.
  *Re-test when:* Specific to headless/background invocation pacing; the render path itself was later confirmed fine.
- **PLAN-log.md 'Overnight run, section 8', organism.rs bullet (Behavior enum)** - Encoding Behavior variants as newtypes wrapping separate structs was rejected on first attempt: RON's syntax for it requires an awkward doubled Divide(Divide(...)), caught by a failing embedded-species-parse test. Struct-shaped enum variants are used instead.
  *Re-test when:* Tied to RON's serialization syntax.
- **README.md 'M12/M13 status' — 'Widened again, 8 -> 12 bytes' paragraph** - Packing the burn timer into Cell::aux under a 'burning always overwrites aux' rule turned out to be a real bug, not a harmless simplification: burning oil needs aux to keep meaning fill fraction, and a burning Plant cell needs its aux-held cell-type tag to survive the fire. burn_timer (and organism_id) became dedicated fields; aux is kind-specific with no exceptions.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent — do not re-overload aux with burn state.
- **README.md 'M14 status' — 'heat_conductivity defaults to 0.0, not a moderate always-on value' paragraph** - Defaulting heat_conductivity to 0.15 so every material conducted plausibly: nonzero conductivity denies diffuse_heat its cheap early exit, so sand and water paid four neighbour reads per visit for nothing — a separate contributor to the same ~64 ms regression. Default is 0.0 with explicit opt-in (oil, ash).
  *Re-test when:* Any future target of burns_into/melts_into/boils_into or a reaction must opt in with a real conductivity, or the heat it inherits has nowhere to go and its chunk never sleeps (see ash.ron's comment).
- **README.md 'M14 status' — 'Residual heat needed the same class of fix roll_reach_at needed' paragraph** - Naively rounding the diffused temperature: hits a real numerical fixed point a few degrees short of ambient (21.6 rounds to 22, which pulls by only -0.4, which rounds back to 22) — a 200-degree cell never reached ambient in a 5000-frame test. The fix guarantees at least one degree of progress when the raw pull is real but rounds away.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for integer-rounded per-cell temperature; permanent for this representation.
- **README.md 'M5 status' — 'A real, pre-existing M14 bug found along the way' paragraph** - The unconditional form of that minimum-one-degree fix: forcing a whole-degree jump on any nonzero pull makes a connected mass of cells cooling together (40 ash cells) pull on each other by tiny nonzero amounts forever and never settle. The forcing must be gated on being outside THERMAL_SETTLE_EPSILON of ambient — the same 'is this settled' question must_stay_dirty asks, not a separate looser one.
  *Re-test when:* Regression test a_connected_mass_of_cooling_cells_actually_settles guards it; the gate must stay tied to the settle epsilon.
- **README.md 'Two subtleties worth knowing before editing update.rs' — 'The moved flag, and why it is not a parity bit'** - Replacing FLAG_MOVED with a frame-parity bit (compare the cell's bit to a world parity flipped each frame, nothing needs clearing): broken, and was the cause of sand freezing in mid-air — a cell skipped for a single frame aliases with the current parity, reads as already-handled, and is skipped every alternate frame forever. A flag cleared when consumed cannot go stale.
  *Re-test when:* Parity only stays in step if every cell is visited every frame, which is precisely what dirty rectangles stop doing — the rejection holds as long as chunk sleeping exists.
- **Reports/coupling-research.md §6 'What is rejected' (MPM)** - The Material Point Method — the rigorous unified sand/water/solid answer — is rejected because adopting it means replacing the CA, not augmenting it: the engine's whole economy (dirty rects, chunk sleeping, active sites, 12-byte Cell) is built on discrete cells and activity-proportional cost, and MPM is a particle-and-grid solver with a global transfer step every frame. Rejected architecturally, not as 'too slow'.
  *Re-test when:* If high-speed impact behaviour (outside RFT's validity, §3a) ever becomes important enough, MPM is the direction — and it is a rewrite.
- **Reports/coupling-research.md §6 'What is rejected' (salva2d)** - salva2d (dimforge's SPH crate with built-in rapier coupling) is rejected despite free integration work, on Report B's unchanged grounds: SPH is a particle method, the budgets don't fit a 163,840-cell world, and there is no compute path. Named so 'why aren't we just using salva' is answered once.
  *Re-test when:* Same condition as the SPH rejection — a GPU compute path plus a much smaller role for particles would be needed to reopen it.
- **Reports/ecological-lod-design.md §1 + §4 'The answer to §7b'** - Advancing off-camera populations with a continuous model (the ODE 'correct population after N frames' framing) was rejected: it produces atto-fox behaviour — a predator population of 10⁻¹⁸ that recovers to carrying capacity. Individuals are frozen at unload, only fields and the patch tier advance, and every population-like quantity is quantized to integers so a patch that decays to 0.4 individuals rounds to zero and stays zero.
  *Re-test when:* Structural to advancing discrete populations; the off-camera world owes plausibility and conservation, not correctness. Corollary that must ride along: frozen means frozen for hunger, age and breeding alike — a creature that ages but does not eat off-camera is the worst of both.
- **Reports/fracture-mechanics-design.md §3.5 "Determinism"** - Keeping load in a `HashMap` that is iterated for the structural walk — nondeterministic iteration order, which is issue #7's trap and has already bitten this codebase twice. Any side table feeding an order-sensitive walk needs deterministic iteration; parent tie-breaks must stay deterministic (`NEIGHBOURS_4` order).
  *Re-test when:* Applies to any order-sensitive walk; same-build determinism is a standing requirement per PLAN.md.
- **src/sim/cell.rs struct Cell doc and test igniting_a_cell_no_longer_touches_aux** - The 8-byte Cell that aliased the burn timer (and organism id) into aux was a real, confirmed bug for a burning Liquid: oil's fill amount would be stomped by the timer mid-fire. Confirmed by temporarily reverting ignite to write aux — the guard test fails. Widened to 12 bytes with dedicated fields; both fields landed in one widening because the organism rewrite would hit the identical aliasing.
  *Re-test when:* Aliasing was safe only while aux held recomputable values (anchor distance); permanent once aux carries state that must survive a burn.
- **src/sim/fire.rs test a_settled_temperature_stops_forcing_its_chunk_awake (comment)** - An earlier version of this guard asserted active_chunk_count() was 0 before anything had visited the cell — but a freshly created chunk starts fully dirty regardless of writes, and dirty regions are double-buffered, so only a real visit narrows the pending region. The bug was in the test, not the code it was testing; assert chunk-activity properties only after a visit.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while Chunk::new starts fully dirty and dirty regions are double-buffered.
- **src/sim/surface.rs CellSurface::field_wind_at doc (measurement parenthesis)** - The first cost measurement of the wind read reported 73 ms against 30 ms and was entirely warm-up: the first ~20 frames of any scene are dominated by allocation and first-touch page faults. Do not believe a single early worst-frame number in this repo.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent measurement practice; re-measure past warm-up and average runs.
- **src/sim/world.rs fn set (managed-check comment)** - Reading the old cell with a separate self.get before the write (to check managed()) means two chunk-map lookups per write in the hottest function in the engine — measured at ~1.7x the serial stress-scene worst frame before the check was folded onto write_cell's returned old value.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while World::set is the hottest path and a get is a chunk-map lookup.
- **`examples/filmstrip.rs` fn paint_exposure doc; measured 2026-08-23** - **The per-cell exposure overlay** — painting every cell of the sheet with `weather::exposure` at that cell's own position, which is the obvious reading of "show the channel" and is what the point form actually returns. It is worthless as a picture and looks fine: a cell inside rock has every upwind surface above it and reads ~0.0, a cell in open sky has every one below it and reads ~1.0, so the sheet comes out a straw sky over a navy solid split on the ground line, and the terrain-driven variation the channel exists to show is a one-pixel band nobody can see at contact-sheet zoom. The numbers printed under that same sheet were healthy — ground spread 0.941 — which is the whole argument for `CLAUDE.md`'s rule that an overlay is paired with a probe that prints values. Replaced by a per-column wash of `ground_exposure`, the quantity the consumer actually samples, with the surface row marked in a fixed cyan that is nowhere on the ramp.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any channel whose interesting variation lives on a 1-cell-thick surface while the function is defined over the whole volume. The general lesson: a readout of a point function is not automatically a readout of the thing being decided.
- **`src/sim/player.rs` `Dir::sticky` doc + test `walking_does_not_change_which_way_he_is_boring`; measured 2026-08-29** - **Damping the bore's direction flip with a ratio-plus-floor rule on the *current* offset** (keep the held direction unless the new axis leads by 1.6:1 and by at least 6 cells). It is the obvious hysteresis and it cannot work, for a reason no amount of tuning reaches: the complaint is that walking past a stationary cursor turns a corridor into a shaft, and after that walk the offset is `dx=2, dy=11` -- a genuine 5.5:1 vertical. That is *the same offset* a player produces by deliberately pointing down, so no threshold on the offset separates the two cases. Replaced by hysteresis on the **cursor**: `Dir::sticky` latches the direction together with the cursor position that set it and holds it until the cursor moves `REAIM_DEADZONE` (12 cells) from there, so it is the hand that re-points the dig and never the legs.
  *Re-test when:* Structural, not tuned -- the two cases are indistinguishable in the quantity the rejected rule reads. Generalises to any "did the player mean that" damping over a relative vector whose *other* end also moves: damp the end the player controls.

- **`src/sim/rigid.rs` `strike`'s crack call + const `CRACK_RAYS` (kept, `#[allow(dead_code)]`); test `a_blow_cracks_along_the_rocks_own_joints_and_not_in_rays`; measured 2026-08-29** - **Drawing a blow's cracks as radial rays** (`score_cracks`: five straight rays keyed on the site, `chip` out to `radius * CRACK_REACH`). Rejected on the owner's playtest of the gnome's hammer -- *"it mostly makes big strike lines instead of breaking rock into pieces"* -- and then again, explicitly, on a blind A/B of two tunings of it: *"neither, fully get rid of the lines that it makes; it should just make cracks similar to an explosion."* This is the **fourth** rejection of a ray/walker crack pattern by the same owner: `fracture_field`'s module doc records the blast's three (*"looks like a graphic, not physics"*, *"it shouldn't look like a scribble"*, *"I thought we were going to match the Voronoi type pattern"*), and the blast stopped drawing them at `d5cb19a` while `strike` kept doing it for another five months. Measured at `hammer_radius: 7`: 122 cells carrying a fissure against 98 cells of damage, so the mark outnumbered the breakage. Replaced by `structural::shatter_joints_around` over the same joint fabric the blast reads; 82 cracked against 317 broken, and 68% of what comes off is now chunks rather than 42%.
  *Re-test when:* Do not re-test on the destruction path -- four rejections, and the two properties the walker cannot have (straight edges, closed cells) are structural rather than tuned. `score_cracks` is still correct for `mine`, at `radius + MINE_CRACK_REACH`, where the reach is short enough to read as a cut fraying its edges rather than as a line drawn on stone. **The tell that a fix is needed here again is the same one: cells cracked outnumbering cells broken.**

- **`src/sim/structural.rs` `reveal_in_disc`'s `flat_to` argument (comment); measured 2026-08-29** - **Revealing a blow's joints under the crush's pure ramp** (`CRUSH_JOINT_DENSITY * (1 - d / reach)`, `flat_to = 0.0`), which is the obvious reuse and draws **dashes rather than pieces**: everything inside the chip radius is already gone, so every joint a blow can reach sits at `d >= chip` where the ramp is well under 1, and a domain whose boundary only partly parts is not enclosed and does not fall out. Measured on `scene=smash`: 135 cells as chunks and 2 bodies against 226 and 3 with the ramp held flat out to `chip`. The ramp is right for a *crush*, whose object is centred on the failure and has rock all the way in.
  *Re-test when:* Holds for any joint reveal whose inner region has already been removed. The general shape: a density ramp measured from a centre that is not there is a ramp measured from the wrong place.

- **`src/sim/rigid.rs` const `BLOW_JOINT_REACH` doc; `src/sim/structural.rs` `shatter_joints_around`; measured 2026-08-29** - **Bounding by distance the joints a blow unbraces**, instead of cutting how far it opens them. The problem is real: the joint fabric opens several hundred edges where a ray star scored ~85 cells, `detach_around_crack` schedules a check for every cell it unbraces, and one radius-14 blow into a solid massif left `acceptance.sh`'s `strike` case with **2,284 pending scheduler sites against its bar of 1,500** -- §S's backlog that climbs instead of draining. Post-filtering the severed set by distance from the blow does not fix it, because **the cascade is chaotic in that parameter rather than monotone**: measured on that one case, 1,553 sites at twice the chip radius, **3,109 at 1.75x**, 1,277 at 1.5x -- while `scene=worked`'s shelf needs the wide bound to give way at all (7 overload failures at 2.0x, 2 at 1.75x and 1.5x). Any value clearing both is landing on luck. Cutting the *reach* instead -- `BLOW_JOINT_REACH: 2` where the rays ran to `CRACK_REACH: 3` -- opens fewer joints rather than filtering them afterwards: `strike` 1,163 sites with 22% headroom, `worked` 6 overloads against a bar of 3.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent as a shape: a post-filter over a chaotic cascade is fitting, not bounding. Two other bounds were tried inside it and are recorded here for the same reason -- unbracing only the two cells a joint separates (right by `sever_joint`'s "no mirror write" argument, wrong by `CRACK_DETACH_DEPTH`'s: it left the rock between boundaries braced and `worked` fell to **1** overload from 7), and calling `schedule_structural_check_around` beside `detach_around_crack`, which is **fully redundant** -- `detach_around` already schedules every cell it unbraces, and removing the extra call moved the site count by exactly zero.

- **`src/sim/structural.rs` `reveal_in_disc`'s `damaged` set (comment); test `a_second_blow_completes_what_the_first_one_left_open`; measured 2026-08-29** - **Keying the repeat-blow joint bonus on whether the two *cells* of a boundary are already cracked.** It is the obvious reading of "damage concentrates at damage" and it is **vacuous**: a domain with five of its six boundaries open has cracked cells along those five and none at all in the middle of the sixth, which is precisely the edge the bonus exists to persuade. Measured by its own positive control -- a second blow at the same spot on solid stone opened **36 fresh edges against the first blow's 36**, i.e. nothing. The unit that has "been hit before" is the block of rock, not the pixel, so the flag is per **domain**, collected in the same first pass that builds the domain map. With it: `scene=worked`'s overload failures 6 -> 14, `scene=smash`'s largest failing region 8 -> 15 cells and pieces in flight at once 3 -> 4.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent for any rule that asks "has this piece of rock been damaged" while the damage is recorded on *boundaries*. The interior of a face carries no mark; only its edges do. **Note the bonus is a per-call argument and the crush passes 0.0**, which is not tidiness either: `Crush { fresh: 0 }` is the re-crush treadmill guard, so a crush that accumulates re-queues itself for ever. Making the bonus unconditional turned `crushing_the_same_rock_twice_writes_nothing_the_second_time` red at 2 fresh edges -- accumulation is what a *blow* wants and exactly what a *crush* must not have.

- **`src/sim/structural.rs` `free_blocks_around` doc; `src/sim/rigid.rs` `strike`'s `core`; measured 2026-08-29** - **Letting the ordinary cascade collect a joint-enclosed block**, rather than promoting it whole on the blow. It looks like it needs nothing: a crack is a place support cannot cross, so a block whose outline has fully parted *is* unsupported and `structural::tick` does find it. What `tick` then does is the defect -- it hands the region to `rigid::fracture`, which re-cuts it on the power-of-two fragment ladder, so a ~170-cell block bounded by four visible joints comes apart into ladder-sized fragments and grit. **The outline and the piece end up different shapes**, which the owner named exactly: *"The chunks should break off along the crack pattern that is already there."* Promoting the block through `promote` directly (never `fracture_with_impulse`, whose ladder is the thing that would break it up again) makes the hole the polygon the cracks drew. Measured on `acceptance.sh`'s `strike` case: 18 bodies carrying 937 cells on the blow frame against 11 carrying 553.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the fragment ladder is size-driven and joint-blind. The general shape, worth carrying: **a mechanism that produces a shape and a mechanism that consumes it have to agree about the shape**, and "the cascade already handles it" is not the same claim as "the cascade handles it the way the player is being shown".

- **`src/sim/rigid.rs` `strike`'s `core` (comment); measured 2026-08-29** - **Pulverizing a `radius / 3` disc on every blow.** It is grit made by fiat rather than by anything breaking, and it dominates what the eye reads because it lands on the *swing frame* -- where a piece has to wait for its outline to close and its body to fly. Reported as *"this mostly looks like small dust breaking first... maybe as a first step just no dust"*. `core` is 0; the whole disc is loosened instead and whatever comes off it below `MIN_BODY_CELLS` is grit as a **consequence** of the fragment draw, which is the distinction `fracture`'s own doc draws between the two.
  *Re-test when:* Reversible in one number, kept at zero rather than deleted for that reason. If a blow ever needs a guaranteed instant mark on unbreakable-looking rock, this is where it goes -- but the mark should then be visible damage rather than manufactured powder.

- **`src/sim/explosion.rs` `Blast::calve`'s release bound (comment); measured 2026-08-29** - **Widening a blast's joint-block release from the calved shell (`radius + calve_depth`) to the fissure halo (`joint_reach * radius`, ~3x the reach and ~10x the area)**, on the reasoning that the blocks a star encloses are out in the near field and the rim is not where they are. The reasoning is sound and the result is **nothing**: paired over five presets x four seeds against the release switched off, both bounds give a **median cells-promoted ratio of exactly 1.00x** -- the tight one better on 8 runs of 20, the wide one on 9 -- while the wide one's worst frame reached **70.6 ms against acceptance's 60 ms budget**. The search radius was never the constraint. What limits it is how often a blast *encloses* a block at all: a hammer completes an outline because its **second** blow reopens the boundaries the first declined (`structural::JOINT_REPEAT_BONUS`), and a blast is one event, so `default_joint_density`'s deliberate one-in-ten holdout leaves most blocks one joint short.
  *Re-test when:* Re-opens the moment a blast's front gains an already-damaged bonus of its own, or `default_joint_density` goes to 1.0 in the near field -- then there are blocks out there to find and the reach starts mattering. **Do not read the pooled total here**: 1.23x pooled is one run (`arid 7`, +2,746 cells, 2.21x), with half the worlds unchanged and a quarter *worse*, because releasing a block early changes what the cascade later finds.

- **`src/sim/explosion.rs` `Blast::calve`'s open-sector gate (comment); tests `a_fully_buried_stone_blast_crushes_a_pocket_and_scores_a_crack_star`, `a_disturbance_extent_licenses_the_wound_but_not_the_chain`; measured 2026-08-29** - **Releasing a blast's joint-enclosed blocks without asking about confinement.** The release is bounded by a *disc* while everything else on the calve path is bounded by a *sector fan* (`ShellSectors::Open` / `Contained`), so it happily took out the blocks a fully buried charge's own crush had enclosed inside its pocket: **648 of 1,257 disc cells cleared**, which is containment not shrinking the cavity, and both guards above went red on exactly the claims they exist to make. Gated on `report.open_sectors > 0`.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent as a shape. The general lesson is the one `CLAUDE.md` states as *which object does this rule evaluate*: a rule ported from a per-cell or per-sector context into a per-*block* one inherits none of that context's gates, and a block is not a sector.

- **`src/sim/weather.rs` const EXPOSURE_HALF_SLOPE doc; measured 2026-08-23** - **Saturating exposure with a hard clamp** (`(slope / EXPOSURE_RELIEF).clamp(0.0, 1.0)`) rather than a soft curve. At the measured median slope it saturated more than a quarter of all terms, and the consequence was not merely cosmetic: **every seed of every preset then scored a full 1.000 spread between its most and least exposed column**, so the order statistic meant to discriminate landscapes could not tell any two of them apart, and the overlay was a binary black-and-white mask. Measured over 24 seeds each of `rolling`, `terraced`, `canyon`, `arid`. Replaced by `slope / (slope + EXPOSURE_HALF_SLOPE)`, which approaches 1.0 without reaching it; spread then runs p10 0.804 / median 0.899 / max 0.941 on `rolling` and can rank worlds again. `CLAUDE.md`'s ethos names the failure directly — a graded outcome beats a binary one, and all-or-nothing outcomes pass every test they have.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the raw upwind slope distribution keeps its measured shape (median 0.37-0.50 cells/column, p99 4.4-7.0). Note the largest slope the walk can even observe is capped at `LOCAL_RELIEF` by `surface_near`'s search window, so a term of 8.0 is the instrument's ceiling and not the terrain's.

- **`src/sim/plant.rs` fn try_bend_at, the cut; measured 2026-08-29** - **Splitting the swinging set on Chebyshev distance from the hinge — "the outer half steps, the inner half holds" — tears the limb in two.** It looks sound for one step: the boundary pair goes from side-by-side to diagonal, so the plant stays eight-connected. It is wrong across *successive* steps, because the cut never migrates. A ten-cell arm drooped its outer four one row; next tick the same four were still the outer half by Chebyshev, so they stepped again while their parent held, and the diagonal link became a two-row gap. Measured: six cells stranded from their anchors, at ticks 2, 5 and 7 of an eight-tick run. Replaced by a level set of `support` — a genuine geodesic cross-section — taking the *largest* threshold that can still move, so the cut travels inward as the tip runs out of room.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent for any one-cell displacement of part of a connected body. The general shape: a split chosen by geometry from a fixed origin cannot express a curve that propagates.

- **`src/sim/plant.rs` fn try_bend_at, the step direction; measured 2026-08-29** - **Stepping the swinging set straight down relieves nothing, and passes a termination guard while doing it.** `CellStress::moment` is a *horizontal* lever arm (`Sx − x_c·M`), so dropping a cantilever one row cannot reduce it — measured, **13.50 before and 13.50 after**. The guard in place at the time asserted only that the plant stopped moving, which it did, so the null was invisible; the plan's own bar ("bending relieves the moment") had been *weakened* to match the broken behaviour rather than the behaviour fixed. Replaced by a step perpendicular to the limb, quantised to the eight neighbours, which rotates the mass and pulls its reach inward: peak moment 19.3 → 2.40 on the same fixture. `rigid_tissue_does_not_bend_and_soft_tissue_does` now asserts relief *and* termination.
  *Re-test when:* Permanent while `moment` is a horizontal first moment. Note the corollary that is still live: a *trunk's* wind moment is a vertical lever, which a lean does not shorten either — which is why wood has no stiffness until breaking exists.

- **`src/sim/plant.rs` fn bend_under_load; measured 2026-08-29** - Two shapes of the same error, both built and both replaced. **Moving the loaded cell itself** rather than what its hinge carries: **0 moved against 452 refused**, because the two sets are disjoint by construction — a cell with a high moment is one that something is resting on, and a cell free to move is one carrying nothing. **Trying only the worst hinge**: on an isolated tuft the most-loaded hinge is free and swings, but in a stand its distal mass has a neighbour in the way and the plant does nothing at all — **1 moved against 295 refused** over six plants, against 2 against 0 for the same species alone. A real stem that cannot swing its whole length bends further out instead, so candidates are worked down in deflection order (`BEND_ATTEMPTS_PER_TICK`).
  *Re-test when:* Permanent. Both are instances of testing a mechanism on one isolated subject and shipping it into a crowd.

- **`src/sim/plant.rs` fn stress_field, the wind term; measured 2026-08-29** - **Reading the wind as `World::field_at_bilinear(..).vx` at the cell** — which is what `organism::wind_lean_dir` uses for growth, so it is the obvious and apparently-precedented choice. Over every cell of living tissue in a grass stand it measures **`vx` from −0.040 to +0.077, median 0.000**: the momentum solve holds no-penetration at the ground and plants grow at the ground, so the one channel that looks like wind is flat exactly where the plants are. A term built on it is a reader with no writer, and it produced moments indistinguishable from the no-wind case (max 10.4 → 10.5). Replaced by `weather::at().wind × weather::exposure(column)`, which is what `report_exposure` and the gust machinery already mean by surface wind, and which is live: max moment 9.2 → 12.2 across a gust, cells wanting to lean 5 → 13.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while the field's velocity solve zeroes the normal component at solid boundaries. It is not a claim that `vx` is wrong everywhere — it is a real channel aloft — only that it is dead at ground level, which is where tissue is.

- **`src/sim/plant.rs` fn try_bend_at, the cut loop; measured 2026-08-29** - **Recomputing the step direction from each cut's own centroid**, rather than once from the whole limb. More defensible on paper — each cross-section is a different piece of limb — and it makes both of the loop's tests non-monotone, so neither can be carried forward as the cut grows and every level rebuilds a `HashSet` and re-checks everything. On a crown-sized flood that was the whole of a worst-frame spike: **136,885 blocked cross-sections** on one run, and mean frame cost **+0.96 ms** against **+0.46 ms** for the same mechanism with the direction taken once. Every cut shares the hinge, so they all want the same perpendicular anyway.
  *Re-test when:* Holds while clearance and the one-piece rule are both monotone in the cut, which they are because a growing set only frees targets and only brings parents along.

- **`assets/materials/leaf.ron` / any wood material, `stiffness`; reasoned 2026-08-29, not built** - **Giving wood a stiffness before breaking exists.** A limb sagging before it snaps is the more wanted picture and it is unbuildable in this order: a bend relieves its moment by shortening a **horizontal** lever, and a trunk's wind moment is a **vertical** one that a one-cell lean does not shorten at all. So a trunk with a stiffness leans again every tick for as long as the wind blows, with nothing in the model to stop it — the creep failure, not a tuning problem. What stops a real trunk is that it breaks.
  *Re-test when:* Stage 3 (break). The wind load is already summed into every wood cell's moment, so nothing further is needed on the wind side — only a rule that consumes the moment by failing rather than by leaning.
